# v19c — the five diagnoses and the four killed proposals (2026-09-04)

Raw structured outputs of the planning workflow, lightly formatted. Numbers are the agents' own measurements;
artifact paths point into the session scratchpad and are promoted by R0.4.


## D: Is the ONSET deletion (the model attenuating the first ~1 s of a near talker before any anchor exists) present on SYNTHETIC in-domain data, or only on real recordings? (v8 and v16 ep19, dry_blend 1.0)

Onset deletion is a REAL-recording phenomenon; on the current synthetic in-domain distribution it is essentially absent in the median and 4-8x smaller even in the tail. Measured with the same definition everywhere (excess = keep-level delta over the first X s of foreground activity minus the delta over the remainder of the same span; and, where a reference exists, a foreground-projection gain 10log10((<enh,ref>/<ref,ref>)^2) that is immune to how much interferer sits in the window): on real Dawn Chorus (n=450, condition none) the first 0.5 s loses -3.85 dB (v8) / -3.90 dB (v16) of its own foreground energy relative to the steady state, p=5e-21 / 2e-21 paired Wilcoxon, p10 -51.5 / -52.5 dB, p5 -69.4 / -75.6 dB; split by structure, the true cold onset (foreground starts <0.1 s into the file, n=146) is -4.22 / -4.61 dB and an onset after >=1 s of real background (n=217, the wrong-anchor case) is -7.65 / -6.07 dB. On synthetic wer_set_moderate_test and indomain_wer_set (200 utts each, both models, GPU cuda:0) the same measure gives -0.25 / -0.41 dB (moderate) and +0.30 / +0.21 dB (indomain, i.e. the onset is treated slightly BETTER than the steady state), with p5 tails only -8.4 / -10.3 dB. Both synthetic sets start speaking at t~0.04 s with no leading silence (ref onset median 0.040 s, p90 0.050-0.060 s, foreground active 93-96% of the file), so I also built prefixed variants: bg1 = 1.0 s of the utterance's own background (mix-ref, longest ref-inactive stretch, loop-tiled with 20 ms crossfades; 232/400 had a usable >=0.12 s inactive stretch, 168/400 fell back to the first 1 s of mix-ref) and sil1 = 1.0 s digital silence. bg1 moves the synthetic median only to -0.52 / -0.67 dB (moderate) and -0.03 / -0.10 dB (indomain) and the p5 tail to -16.4 / -15.9 dB - statistically real (p=2e-10 / 3.6e-11) but still an order of magnitude short of the real number. Matched ratios real/synthetic: cold onset median 11-23x (v8 17.0x, v16 11.3x at 0.5 s vs moderate; undefined vs indomain because synthetic has no deletion there), background-first onset median 7-15x, and p5 tail 3.9-7.9x across every window and both checkpoints. A strict-mask control (ref frames within 20 dB of the utterance max, so quiet onset-ramp frames cannot drive the result) only softens Dawn from -3.85 to -2.87 dB while leaving synthetic unchanged (-0.22 / -0.41), so the gap is not an onset-ramp artifact. On the field set the cold `none` condition shows nothing (near excess -0.05 to -0.12 dB) for the trivial reason that v8/v16 are passthrough cold (d_rest -0.12 to -0.17 dB - there is no attenuation to concentrate at the onset); field onset deletion shows up only in the ANCHORED conditions, near_speech dt -3.86 dB (v8, 0.5 s) and stream near -0.69 dB (v8, 1 s, p=0.004) with p5 tails -29 dB, which is exactly the wrong-anchor failure the AnchorGate `pna` clause removes. Practical consequence: a loss-only change cannot fix onset deletion, because the current synthetic training distribution does not contain the failure - there is no gradient to shape. The one synthetic knob that did reproduce the real tail is a digital-silence lead-in on v16 (sil1, moderate: median -0.67, p10 -24.1, p5 -70.2 dB vs Dawn's -75.6), which is a data-construction change, not a loss change, and it is checkpoint-specific (v8 sil1 p5 only -20.0 dB).

**Findings**

- **Real recordings show a large, highly significant onset deletion in the first 0.5 s of foreground activity; both checkpoints.** — Dawn Chorus, condition none, n=450, foreground-projection excess @0.5 s: v8 median -3.85 dB (p10 -51.51, p5 -69.41), Wilcoxon p=5.3e-21; v16 median -3.90 dB (p10 -52.52, p5 -75.60), p=1.6e-21. Keep-level (span_dbfs) excess @0.5 s: v8 -1.15, v16 -1.39 dB; @1.0 s -0.68 / -0.78 dB. _(/tmp/claude-1001/-home-milowu-A4Audio-PureSound/c52f3b49-8e8f-4f9f-a5ca-862771bd494c/scratchpad/v19_plan/onset_profile/onset_profile_tables.txt sections A and C; rows in real_onset_rows.jsonl (from the existing agcache dawn records, no model re-run).)_
- **On synthetic in-domain data the same measure finds no onset deletion in the median - on indomain_wer_set the onset is if anything treated better than the steady state.** — fg-projection excess @0.5 s, n=200 each: v8 moderate -0.25, v8 indomain +0.30, v16 moderate -0.41, v16 indomain +0.21 dB. Keep-level excess @0.5 s: v8 moderate +0.42, v8 indomain +0.65, v16 moderate +0.20, v16 indomain +0.51 dB. p5 tails (fg-proj) -8.35 / -8.41 / -10.27 / -8.00 dB. _(onset_profile_tables.txt sections B and C, variant t0; raw rows synth_onset_rows_v8.jsonl / synth_onset_rows_v16.jsonl produced by onset_synth.py on cuda:0 over all 200+200 utterances.)_
- **Both synthetic WER sets start with speech at t=0, so the requested prepended-background variant was built; giving synthetic a 1 s background lead-in makes onset deletion statistically detectable but still an order of magnitude smaller than real.** — ref onset: moderate median 0.040 s / p90 0.060 s / max 0.090 s; indomain median 0.040 / p90 0.050 / max 0.070 s; ref active 93% (moderate) / 96% (indomain) of the file; longest ref-inactive run median 0.23 / 0.15 s, only 1-2% of files have >=1.0 s. bg1 (own background, mix-ref, loop-tiled; 232/400 inactive_loop, 168/400 head_fallback) fg-proj excess @0.5 s: v8 moderate -0.52 (p=2.0e-10), v8 indomain +0.03, v16 moderate -0.67 (p=3.6e-11), v16 indomain -0.10; p5 tails -16.42 / -16.52 / -15.85 / -14.81 dB. _(onset_profile_tables.txt sections B, B2, C (variant bg1, and the inactive_loop vs head_fallback split which shows the fallback prefix is not driving it); build_bg_prefix in onset_synth.py; bg-source counts in synth_v8.log / synth_v16.log.)_
- **Matched real/synthetic ratio: 11-23x on the median for the cold onset, 3.9-7.9x on the p5 tail, in every window and for both checkpoints.** — fg-proj, @0.5 s, COLD onset (Dawn foreground onset <0.1 s, n=146 vs synthetic t0): real 4.22 vs synth 0.25 dB = 17.0x (v8), real 4.61 vs 0.41 = 11.3x (v16); vs indomain the ratio is undefined because synthetic deletion is negative (none). BG-FIRST onset (Dawn onset >=1 s, n=217 vs synthetic bg1): real 7.65 vs 0.52 = 14.7x (v8), 6.07 vs 0.67 = 9.1x (v16). p5 tail: 6.34x / 4.72x (cold) and 4.67x / 5.32x (bg-first) at 0.5 s; 3.91-7.87x across all windows. _(/tmp/claude-1001/.../v19_plan/onset_profile/RATIO_MATCHED.txt (full 48-row table); dawn_split.txt for the Dawn onset-structure split.)_
- **The gap is not an artifact of quiet onset-ramp frames entering the max-40 dB activity mask.** — Strict mask (ref frames within 20 dB of the utterance max), fg-proj excess @0.5 s: Dawn v8 -3.85 -> -2.87 dB (p10 -51.5 -> -34.6, p5 -69.4 -> -62.3), Dawn v16 -3.90 -> -2.97; synthetic t0 v8 moderate -0.25 -> -0.22, indomain +0.30 -> +0.22; v16 moderate -0.41 -> -0.41, indomain +0.21 -> +0.11. Ratio still 13.0x (v8 moderate) / 7.2x (v16 moderate). _(/tmp/claude-1001/.../v19_plan/onset_profile/control_strict_mask.txt)_
- **On the field set the cold `none` condition cannot show onset deletion because both checkpoints are passthrough cold; field onset deletion appears only in the anchored conditions, i.e. it is the wrong-anchor failure, not a state-warm-up failure.** — field first keep span, keep-level excess @0.5 s / steady-state delta: none near v8 -0.05 / d_rest -0.17 dB (n=27, 3/27 onset-only deletions, p=0.2), v16 -0.12 / -0.17 (2/27, p=0.049). Anchored: near_speech dt v8 -3.86 dB (n=11, p10 -26.86), stream near v8 -0.45 @0.5 s and -0.69 @1.0 s (n=19/17, p=0.04 / 0.0038, p10 -23.94 / -26.58), near_speech near v8 -0.49 (p=0.0059). _(onset_profile_tables.txt section A; real_onset_summary.tsv; consistent with benchmarks/probes/anchor_gate_README.md sections 2-3 (the pna clause halves keep violations).)_
- **One synthetic construction does reproduce the real tail, but it is a data change (prefix structure), not a loss change, and it is checkpoint-specific.** — sil1 (1 s digital silence prepended), fg-proj excess @0.5 s: v16 moderate median -0.67, p10 -24.13, p5 -70.17, 46/200 onset-only deletions, p=1.3e-13 - the p5 tail matches Dawn v16's -75.60. v8 moderate sil1: median -0.22, p10 -6.57, p5 -20.03 (v8 barely reacts). Keep-level: v16 moderate sil1 -0.09 median, p5 -44.69 dB. _(onset_profile_tables.txt sections B and C, variant sil1; matches reference_matrix_README.md section 2 (digital silence is a suppress-biasing, non-neutral prefix, not a neutral one).)_

**Implications for training**

- A loss-side fix alone cannot remove onset deletion: measured on both current synthetic training-distribution proxies the failure is absent in the median (-0.25 to +0.30 dB fg-proj excess), so no reweighting of an existing loss has anything to push on. Any v19 knob aimed at onset deletion must first change the DATA so the failure appears in-distribution, then let the loss see it.
- The synthetic distribution is structurally incapable of teaching onset behaviour as it stands: foreground is active 93-96% of the file and starts at t~0.04 s in >=90% of utterances (median 0.040 s, max 0.090 s). There is no anchor-free lead-in, no bystander-first row, and no long-gap row. Round-1 candidate: a lead-in knob that prepends 0.5-3 s of (a) the row's own room/noise background, (b) an interferer-only stretch (bystander-first = the wrong-anchor case, which is the WORSE real condition at -7.65 / -6.07 dB vs -4.22 / -4.61 for cold), and (c) digital silence, with the loss weighted normally over the first second.
- The bystander-first row is the higher-value target, not the cold row: on real data the onset after >=1 s of background is 1.3-1.8x worse than the true cold onset (Dawn v8 -7.65 vs -4.22 dB, v16 -6.07 vs -4.61 dB @0.5 s), and it is also the field condition where onset deletion is visible at all (near_speech dt -3.86 dB). This matches the anchor_gate finding that the pna clause plus the relative comparison are two separate halves.
- Digital silence is the one prefix that already induces the real tail on v16 (sil1 p5 -70.2 dB vs Dawn -75.6 dB) - so it is usable as a cheap in-training stressor AND as a cheap synthetic regression probe for whether a v19 candidate has fixed the tail. But it must not be used as a 'neutral' prefix in evaluation (reference_matrix_README section 2 already says this), and it does not reproduce the median, so it is a tail-only proxy.
- Do not evaluate onset deletion with the span-level delta alone on synthetic mixes: the mix contains interferers throughout, so span_dbfs(enh)-span_dbfs(mix) reads -3.5 to -4.3 dB everywhere for legitimate reasons and its onset-vs-steady difference is near zero. The foreground-projection gain (10log10((<enh,ref>/<ref,ref>)^2) per window) is the measure that separates 'deleted the user' from 'removed the interferer' and should be the gate metric for any onset knob - this is the same conflation span-energy-metric-conflates-four-layers warns about.
- Whatever v19 does, the onset gate has to be read on real recordings (Dawn, and the field anchored conditions), because the synthetic number will be near zero both before and after the change and therefore cannot distinguish a fix from a no-op. Conversely, if a v19 candidate makes the SYNTHETIC onset number worse while the real one improves, that is expected and not a regression signal.

**Caveats**

- 'Real' here is Dawn Chorus (real recordings, real background lead-in, reference available) plus the field test-vector set. The field set's cold `none` condition contributes nothing to the answer because v8/v16 are passthrough cold there (steady-state delta -0.12 to -0.17 dB); field evidence for onset deletion comes only from the anchored stream / near_speech conditions with n=11-27 spans per cell, which by the FIELD_BLOCK.md protocol is too small for a single-checkpoint verdict. The Dawn numbers (n=446-450, p<1e-20) carry the finding; the field numbers are corroborating, not decisive.
- Single checkpoint per version. FIELD_BLOCK.md requires 5-checkpoint blocks for field deltas; I ran v8 and v16 ep19 only, as instructed. The real-vs-synthetic contrast is 11-23x and reproduces on two independent checkpoints and two independent measures, so it is very unlikely to be checkpoint noise, but the exact dB values are not block-corrected.
- Dawn's pre-onset background is usually a bystander talker (median pre-onset mix level is only 5.1 dB below the file's own background level for the onset>=1 s group), so the Dawn onset>=1 s cell mixes 'cold start' with 'wrong anchor'. I split it (dawn_split.txt) but did not separately verify by listening which of the 217 files have a talker versus room noise before the onset.
- The synthetic bg1 prefix is imperfect: no synthetic utterance has 1 s of clean background (longest ref-inactive run median 0.23 / 0.15 s, only 1-2% reach 1 s), so the prefix is a loop-tiled 0.12-1.0 s segment (232/400) or, when nothing usable existed, the first 1 s of mix-ref which contains interferer speech (168/400). Both sub-groups were checked separately (section B2) and agree, but the prefix carries a loop periodicity artifact and, since the synthetic interferers are near-continuous, it is closer to the field `far_speech` condition than to true room tone.
- The foreground-projection gain is a single scalar least-squares gain per window; it does not distinguish 'attenuated' from 'distorted/phase-rotated', so a heavily distorted but not attenuated onset would also read negative. It agrees in sign and ordering with the span-level delta everywhere, and the strict-mask control rules out the quiet-frame confound, but it has not been cross-checked against ASR deletion on a per-window basis.
- Both synthetic sets are post-2026-08-18 synthesis (linear device chain) while v8 predates that boundary (synthesis-linearity-fix-breaks-ckpt-comparability). v8's synthetic numbers are therefore slightly out-of-distribution for v8. This works against the finding rather than for it - v8 sees a mildly shifted synthetic distribution and still shows no synthetic onset deletion - and v16 is on the current distribution and agrees.
- Not measured: ASR deletion on the synthetic onset windows (no transcription run in this task); onset behaviour of v11b/v10 or any other checkpoint; whether a lead-in training knob actually reduces the real number (that is the round the plan would have to run).

## D: Which candidate training objectives / architectures for "keep a persistent, correctly-assigned foreground identity without enrollment" are supported by evidence (repo history + 2024-2026 literature), and which are already refuted here?

Five mechanisms survive, and two numbers decide the ranking. First, the error is already localised and priced: an inference-side rule that merely refuses to attenuate until a talker has been heard for 1 s removes half the field keep violations (v8 26/148 -> 13, v16 20/148 -> 7) and, with the relative-DRR arm, cuts Dawn deletion 0.230 -> 0.096 (v8) and 0.207 -> 0.094 (v16), WER 0.333 -> 0.194 / 0.297 -> 0.188, every arm p < 1e-26 paired, at a price of -15.2 -> -11.0 dB median far suppression. So roughly 0.13 absolute deletion is onset + wrong-anchor deletion, and a trained fix would avoid the suppression price the guard pays. Second, that behaviour is not supervised: the row generator's inter-turn silence is capped at 0.4 s (turn_gap_seconds=(0.0,0.4), not overridden in v16) while the field probe measures the anchor needing ~1 s, saturating at 2 s, half-gone after 5 s of floor and dead at 10 s; and in the pipeline-built moderate set the foreground onset sits at 0.06 s median (max 0.14 s, 0/200 >= 0.5 s), the median longest internal foreground gap is 0.335 s, 0/200 rows have a >= 3 s gap, and onset frames carry only 14 % of active frames / 22 % of active energy. Hence M1 ranks first: long-gap return rows + wrong-anchor rows + an onset-weighted OverSuppressionLoss reusing the already-computed, loss-unconsumed vad_label frames — no new parameters, and literature-backed (LExt gets 22.0 dB SI-SDRi from a 0.5 s onset prompt and 24.1 vs USEF-TSE's 23.3 on WSJ0-2mix with no speaker-conditioning module at all, i.e. "earliest onset" is a strong learnable identity cue; Xia et al. and transient/spectral-flux weighting supply the per-frame weight; VoiceFilter-Lite supplies the asymmetric direction we already have globally at weight 3.0). M2 = train the relative comparison into a per-frame head read by the shipped OnsetGuard/PresenceGate (relative survived chain and checkpoint drift twice where absolute died twice: pooled QVF AUC 0.474 vs within-session 0.886/0.911; fixed threshold 0.74 -> 0.62-0.70 and -3.2 -> -0.5 dB vs calibrated 0.77-0.82 / -2.5..-4.2; Streaming Sortformer's arrival-order speaker cache and EvoTSE's reliability-filtered self-updating enrollment are the published analogues). M3 = self-conditioning the currently dead FiLM path (dvec_dim) with a bottleneck-pooled anchor: highest ceiling — Paernamaa's internal-embedding PSE holds background suppression (33.04 vs 34.30 dB) while cutting over-suppression 2.7x (TSOS 0.010 vs 0.027) at 1.07 M params / 0.135 ms per frame — but it can cement the wrong anchor and re-run the channel-signature anchoring failure, and its prerequisite (does the bottleneck separate talker from channel identity?) is unmeasured and unmeasurable on the existing cache. M4 = finish chain/compression invariance (v11b's compressor alone moved scenario3 AUC 0.252 -> 0.411, session 0.596 -> 0.726, held-out 180d 0.841 -> 0.889) is the cheapest and is a hard prerequisite for M2/M3, because on the QVF chain the distance readout is inverted (AUC 0.265) and DRR only 0.799. M5 = real paired-recording proxy supervision is still the only lever with a record of moving the real wall and the 2026 literature has converged on it (ctPuLSE, PS4, REAL-TSE SLT 2026), but at quarter scale, not round scale. Excluded, each against a recorded result: absolute thresholds of any kind; room/ambience reference conditioning (true floor is a null, delta -0.04/+0.03 dB, p 0.08/0.76; the v17 knob was negative); synthetic-RIR fixes and the direct-smear premise; relaxing the suppression floors (v18 cold-far +0.53 dB, p=0.017); target-absent silence rows as the persistence teacher (Dawn WER 0.392 -> 0.626); context-carrier swaps (v14 beaten by a free dry_blend knob, 0.181/0.092 vs 0.271/0.175); background-VAD-head double-talk logic (real AUC 0.475); LExt-style prepend-enrollment and mixture-to-set embeddings as deployed architectures (offline, over budget, selection delegated externally); and dry_blend for cold start (median gain 0.03 dB).

**Findings**

- **The deletion the v19 round must remove is onset + wrong-anchor deletion, it is already quantified, and an inference-side guard shows the size of the prize a trained fix could win without paying suppression depth.** — Keep violations v8 26/148 -> 13 (pna clause alone), v16 20/148 -> 7; DRR-relative arm Dawn deletion v8 0.230 -> 0.096, v16 0.207 -> 0.094; WER 0.333 -> 0.194 and 0.297 -> 0.188; all arms p < 1e-26 paired; bystander penalty +0.027/+0.028 -> +0.003/+0.005 (p 0.30/0.34). Price: fit-set far suppression -15.2 -> -11.0 dB median, held-out 180d -19.4 -> -11.6 (v8) and -22.3 -> -10.0 (v16). _(/home/milowu/A4Audio/PureSound/egs/voice_isolate/benchmarks/probes/anchor_gate_README.md sections 2, 3, 6)_
- **The persistence behaviour is not in the supervision at all: the training row generator's longest inter-turn silence is 0.4 s, and the pipeline-built distribution contains essentially no 'foreground returns after a multi-second gap' event, while the measured anchor half-life is ~5 s.** — turn_gap_seconds = (0.0, 0.4) s default, not overridden in the v16 recipe. Measured on 200 moderate-set refs: first onset median 0.06 s / max 0.14 s / 0 of 200 >= 0.5 s; median longest internal foreground gap 0.335 s; rows with a gap >= 1 s / >= 3 s / >= 5 s = 2 % / 0 % / 0 %. Field probe: anchor needs ~1 s, saturates at 2 s, half gone after 5 s of floor, dead at 10 s (v8 far -19.25 -> -9.83 -> -0.92 dB). _(/tmp/claude-1001/-home-milowu-A4Audio-PureSound/c52f3b49-8e8f-4f9f-a5ca-862771bd494c/scratchpad/v19_plan/objective_landscape/{first_onset.py,onset_mass.py}; /home/milowu/A4Audio/PureSound/puresound/config/augmentation.py:46-59; /home/milowu/A4Audio/PureSound/egs/voice_isolate/benchmarks/probes/reference_matrix_README.md section 3)_
- **An energy-pooled loss spends at most a fifth of its mass on the frames that get deleted, and the per-frame labels needed to reweight them are already computed every row and read by no loss - so an onset-weighted term is a small, cheap change.** — Onset frames (first 1 s after >= 1 s of foreground silence, row start included) = 14.1 % of active frames and 22.5 % of active energy, median, ~1 onset per row (0.5 s definition 19.9 % / 26.8 %; 0.2 s definition 31.5 % / 46.3 %). OverSuppressionLoss is a uniform mean over all T-F bins at weight 3.0; SDRLoss is global per row; vad_label is on in both v8 and v16 with the v16 config stating 'no loss consumes these labels'. _(/home/milowu/A4Audio/PureSound/puresound/nnet/loss/stft_loss.py:230-259; /home/milowu/A4Audio/PureSound/egs/voice_isolate/config/exp/train_dpcrn_v16_lengthmix.yaml lines 295-302 and 383-392)_
- **The literature independently validates both ingredients of the top mechanism: an onset is a sufficient identity cue for a generic separator, and per-frame / asymmetric weighting is the standard cure for over-suppression.** — LExt: 24.1 dB SI-SDRi WSJ0-2mix vs USEF-TSE 23.3; 18.3 vs 16.1 on WHAMR!; 22.0 dB SI-SDRi from a 0.5 s onset prompt, saturating at 4-6 s, with no speaker-conditioning module. VoiceFilter-Lite: deletions dominate the WER loss, answered with an asymmetric loss plus inference-time over-suppression compensation. Krisp VI 2.5: competing-speech WER ~36 % -> ~11 %, clean-audio penalty ~0 (2.15 vs 2.10), 15 ms algorithmic latency, CPU-only. _(https://arxiv.org/html/2505.05114v1 ; https://www.isca-archive.org/interspeech_2020/wang20z_interspeech.pdf ; https://krisp.ai/blog/voice-isolation-2-5/)_
- **A persistent, correctly-assigned identity slot without enrollment has a working streaming precedent - but it is supervised by arrival order and carried in an explicit, score-updated cache, which is exactly what our accidental 'recent-talker template' lacks.** — Streaming Sortformer (Interspeech 2025): Arrival-Order Speaker Cache stores frame-level embeddings ordered by arrival time, updated by selecting the highest-scoring frames of the model's own past predictions; trained with arrival-time-ordered targets ('sort loss'); streaming, competitive with the offline model on Fisher / AMI-ICSI / CALLHOME / AISHELL-4, and a speaker returning after silence is re-identified from the cache. EvoTSE: enrollment continuously updated by reliability-filtered retrieval over high-confidence historical estimates, consistent gains especially out of domain. _(https://arxiv.org/pdf/2507.18446 ; https://www.isca-archive.org/interspeech_2025/medennikov25_interspeech.pdf ; https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.06810)_
- **Self-conditioning through the model's own internal representation is validated at this parameter scale and, crucially, reduces over-suppression rather than causing it - which is why M3 has the highest ceiling despite its risk.** — Paernamaa (Interspeech 2024): embedding taken after the temporal block / final GRU + LayerNorm, frame embeddings averaged; background suppression 33.04 dB (internal) vs 34.30 dB (separate speaker model); TSOS over-suppression 0.010 vs 0.027 (2.7x less); MOS 3.25 vs 3.21; small model 1.07 M params, 0.135 ms/frame (RTF 0.0135). _(https://arxiv.org/html/2406.09928v1 ; repo injection point: /home/milowu/A4Audio/PureSound/puresound/nnet/dpcrn.py:36-47 and :122-135 (FiLM behind embedding_size=dvec_dim, dead because dvec_dim is None in every voice_isolate config), /home/milowu/A4Audio/PureSound/puresound/nnet/lobe/trivial.py:129-167)_
- **Any readout-driven mechanism (M2, M3) inherits the QVF-chain inversion as a hard prerequisite, and the compression axis is the one confirmed, unfinished lever against it.** — AnchorGate section 1: device-chain isolated AUC dist 0.989 / DRR 0.997, sessions 0.695-0.781; QVF isolated dist 0.265 (INVERTED) / DRR 0.799, sessions 0.357 / 0.517. v11b (compressor augmentation only): scenario3 head AUC 0.252 -> 0.365 (ep19) -> 0.411 (ep31), scenario3_session 0.596 -> 0.726, held-out 180d 0.841 -> 0.889; healed every QVF keep deletion including a -11.79 dB double-talk cut, for about half the anchored far suppression (-12.35/-11.29/-9.25 -> -6.64/-5.95/-6.02). _(/home/milowu/A4Audio/PureSound/egs/voice_isolate/benchmarks/probes/anchor_gate_README.md section 1; .../v11b_VERDICT.md; .../eq_probe_README.md)_
- **The 'reference' framing of v12 axis 5 must be rewritten before it is built: a room reference is a measured null, so the only thing worth conditioning on is a talker anchor - which is also why a naive self-enrolment loop would cement the current failure.** — True-floor pads vs no pad on 5-checkpoint blocks: delta -0.04 dB p=0.08 (v8) and +0.03 dB p=0.76 (v16); near speech through the same chain -15.12 dB p<0.001 (v8) and -12.03 p<0.001 (v16); v17 row_initial_ambient negative on its pre-registered primary (far none -1.71 -> -1.48, p=0.15). The same anchor that unlocks suppression creates 4-6 device keep violations on the NEXT near talker (v8) and 3 (v16). _(/home/milowu/A4Audio/PureSound/egs/voice_isolate/benchmarks/probes/reference_matrix_README.md sections 0, 2, 3.3, 6; .../v17_round_design.md VERDICT and ERRATUM)_
- **Two enrollment-free architectures that look like direct answers must be excluded as deployed mechanisms, for structural reasons rather than by our own negative results.** — LExt is offline by construction - the authors state that referring to the prepended enrollment's tensors in real time 'would be costly' and propose no frame-online variant. Mixture-to-set (arXiv:2604.03219) is non-causal on a WavLM backbone, Hungarian-matched to an ArcFace teacher, degrades when the candidate cosine margin is small, and explicitly leaves target SELECTION to external interaction or ASR cues - selection being our entire problem. _(https://arxiv.org/html/2505.05114v1 ; https://arxiv.org/html/2604.03219v1)_
- **The metric side of the round is under-equipped: the repo's keep-side measure is span energy, which its own record says cannot attribute, and two off-the-shelf metrics fit the onset/gap question exactly.** — TSOS (ICASSP 2023 DNS-5 PSE) is a signal-level target-speaker-over-suppression metric introduced because deletion rate alone misses over-suppression; it is what Paernamaa reports as 0.010 vs 0.027. REAL-TSE (SLT 2026) reports timing F1 alongside TER / SIM / DNSMOS with separate online (causality enforced inside the extractor) and offline tracks; BSRNN baselines TER 0.829 / 0.838, SIM 0.417 / 0.443, DNSMOS-P808 2.875 / 2.756 on a 1,991-sample dev set from AISHELL-4 / AMI / AliMeeting / CHiME-6 / DiPCo. _(https://arxiv.org/pdf/2303.06811 ; https://arxiv.org/html/2607.15198v1)_

**Implications for training**

- Rank 1 (v19 single axis): M1 - anchor persistence and wrong-anchor supervision. Three sub-changes, none of them new parameters: (a) widen turn_gap_seconds so a far turn can be followed by 3-10 s of true floor before the near turn, (b) add a row type where the SAME real near talker returns after that gap with the foreground label alive across it (never as a target-absent row), (c) weight OverSuppressionLoss per frame on the first ~1 s after each foreground onset using the existing vad_label frames. Warm-start v16 ep19; judge on 5-checkpoint blocks with paired Wilcoxon; guardrails = session suppression block band, Dawn deletion, moderate-WER deltas, s2f-swept keep curve.
- Run M4's offline half in parallel because it is nearly free and re-ranks M2/M3: codec / EQ / noise-gate through the eq_probe phantom-distance methodology, with any knob that moves the readout stacked on augmentation_compressor + channel_consistency. Do not start M2 or M3 on the QVF chain before this - the distance readout there is inverted (0.265), so no head or supervisor reads through it.
- State M1's success criterion as 'the OnsetGuard becomes unnecessary', measured with the guard OFF: keep violations at or below the guarded arm's 13/148 and Dawn deletion at or below ~0.10, while session suppression stays inside the v16-ep19 block band. If the trained model only matches the guard at the guard's suppression cost, the axis produced nothing the shipped guard did not.
- Do NOT implement the silence gap as a target-absent row and do NOT relax the loss floors while doing it - two separate recorded explosions (Dawn WER 0.392 -> 0.626, deletion 0.286; and v18 cold-far +0.53 dB, p=0.017). M1 adds keep-side weight; it must not remove suppress-side gradient.
- Before M3 (self-conditioning through the dead FiLM path) is scheduled, run one cheap probe: does the DPCRN bottleneck separate TALKER identity from CHANNEL identity? The existing per-frame cache cannot answer it (450 Dawn utterances, 26 recordings, one talker each, so same-talker pairs are always same-channel). Use a two-talker-per-recording source - the field set's own sessions, or a corpus_pair_probe pairing. If the bottleneck encodes chain rather than talker, M3 would re-run the real-e2e round-1 channel-anchoring failure and should not be built.
- If M2 is built, the comparison margin must be internal or self-calibrated, never an absolute dB: v8's 3 dB margin costs v16 ten suppress passes, the third independent instance of a fixed threshold dying across a chain or a checkpoint.
- Import TSOS and a timing-F1-style metric into the field scorecard before judging M1. The current keep-side measure is span energy, which conflates speech, noise and reverberation, and the deletion claim needed a full ASR run to be believed; both new metrics score the onset/gap question directly and cheaply.
- Treat arrival-order supervision (Sortformer's sort loss + score-updated cache) as the reference design for any future explicit slot memory and EvoTSE's reliability-filtered self-update as the reference update rule - but only after M1 has fixed WHICH talker the anchor should be. Both mechanisms make persistence stronger, which on today's model would make the wrong-anchor deletion worse, not better.
- Keep M5 (real close/far paired supervision: ctPuLSE / PS4 / REAL-TSE) as the quarter-scale line, not a v19 round; note that REAL-TSE's online track gives an external leaderboard our streaming export could be measured against without exposing the private recordings.

**Caveats**

- My two measurements are on data_report/wer_set_moderate_test, which is built by scripts/build_wer_set.py - a standalone LibriTTS + BUT-RIR + DNS-5 builder, NOT the training dataloader. They corroborate the training-side fact I read directly from the recipe (0.4 s inter-turn gap cap, no return-after-gap row type) but they are not a measurement of training batches. A direct measurement (instantiate the v16 dataloader, log per-row foreground-gap and onset statistics) is roughly 1 CPU-hour and was not done.
- The onset-mass numbers use an energy-based activity detector (frame within 30 dB of the file's 95th-percentile frame energy and above -60 dBFS) on ~8 s rows, so the row-start onset dominates the count; in the recipe's 30 s bucket (prob 0.20) the onset share of the loss mass will be several times smaller, not larger.
- 'Loss mass' is approximated by active-frame energy share - a proxy for where an energy-pooled SI-SDR / MR-STFT / OverSuppression gradient goes, not a gradient measurement.
- No training, no inference and no GPU work was done, so every claim about a MECHANISM's effect is either a repo number already on record or an external published number - none of it is a measurement of the proposed v19 changes.
- External numbers were read from paper HTML/PDF and product blogs via a summarising fetch; two fetches returned only abstracts or failed to decode (EvoTSE's full results, the positive/negative-enrollment paper's tables), so those entries carry mechanism descriptions without verified numbers, and the Streaming Sortformer fetch did not surface exact DER values or chunk latencies.
- The commercial numbers (Krisp VI 2.5 competing-speech WER ~36 % -> ~11 %, clean penalty 2.15 vs 2.10, 15 ms latency; 3.5x VAD false-positive reduction; 2x AMI WER improvement) are vendor-published on their own material, and neither vendor discloses how the primary speaker is determined - I checked the turn-taking blog specifically and the mechanism is undisclosed.
- 'Refuted' is asserted at the strength of the recorded verdicts, which are mostly single runs at one warm start with one loss weight; the exclusions are decisions about where not to spend GPU, not proofs of impossibility. The clearest example is v11b, stopped mid-cosine at ep31, so its suppression cost is provisional.
- M3's prerequisite question (talker vs channel identity in the bottleneck) is unmeasured, and I deliberately did not probe it on the existing cache because its single-talker-per-recording structure makes the result uninterpretable - any number would be channel identity wearing a speaker label, the exact confound that produced the withdrawn v17 section 2b.

- `/tmp/claude-1001/-home-milowu-A4Audio-PureSound/c52f3b49-8e8f-4f9f-a5ca-862771bd494c/scratchpad/v19_plan/objective_landscape/onset_mass_rows.json`
- `/tmp/claude-1001/-home-milowu-A4Audio-PureSound/c52f3b49-8e8f-4f9f-a5ca-862771bd494c/scratchpad/v19_plan/objective_landscape/first_onset.py`

## D: What does the CURRENT v16 training data actually contain in the dimensions that matter for onset / anchor behaviour, and does the loss weight time?

Measured on 1206 rows drawn through the real v16 pipeline (config/exp/train_dpcrn_v16_lengthmix.yaml, 104 consecutive train batches, CPU, seed 7; second 404-row pass for the scalar labels). The training distribution is "two people already talking from frame 0": the target's first onset is at median 0.00 s, p90 0.44 s, 81.7% of rows have it within 0.1 s and 90.7% within 0.5 s; only 6.6% of rows have a target onset at or after 1 s. The corpus is the reason — 62,148 chapter utterances of 30.93-31.0 s of continuous audiobook read, cropped at a random offset (dynamic_base.py:633), so a crop is essentially never silent at its start. Interferer-speaks-first ("wrong anchor") exists but is rare: 15.8% of rows have any interferer speech before the target's onset, 6.0% have >= 1 s of it and 4.6% >= 2 s, and that shape is almost entirely produced by turn-taking on real-recording rows (of the 72 rows with >= 1 s: 31 real-near-keep, 29 real-far, 12 synthetic) — turn-taking fires on only 11.7% of rows, because overlap_control.turn_taking_prob is 0.0 in v16 and the only sources are the per-row overrides realfar 0.3 / realnear 0.5 (voice_isolation.py:336). The "anchor must survive silence" condition is effectively absent: the longest target-free gap inside a row is median 0.53 s / p90 1.81 s, >= 2 s on 9.0% of rows, >= 5 s on 0.3% (4 of 1206), and target re-entry after >= 5 s of absence happens in 0.3% of rows — on the 3 s bucket, which is 49.8% of all rows, the longest interior target gap ever observed was 1.71 s. Gaps with no speech at all are shorter still (median 0.02 s, p90 0.71 s; >= 2 s on 1.7%), and only 1.7% of rows open with >= 1 s of speech-free room tone (the v17 row-initial-ambient knob is off). Target-absent rows are 3.4% of the batch, of which 2.5% are the planned lone-far rows and 1.0% are turn-taking rows whose near talker was gated to exact zero while the metadata still says target_present=1.0. On the loss side, none of the seven v16 terms weights time or segments: SDR is a whole-row ratio, MR-STFT / OverSuppression / ResidualReference are means over all frames or samples, DistHead is a global (F,T) mean pool. The only existing temporal weighting is ASRFeatureLoss's random 6 s crop, and it points the wrong way — on a 12 s row a sample at t=0 has crop-coverage probability 0.000 and at t=1 s 0.167; on a 30 s row 0.000 and 0.042, versus 0.250 mid-row — so 2.0 of the 7.0 total loss weight is nearly blind to the opening of the long buckets. Frame weights can be added without new infrastructure only as a NEW loss class: the provider table already hands a loss enhanced/target/batch/vad_target/inactive_labels (siso.py:317-357, base.py:18-51), but no per-frame weight tensor is emitted by the dataset and the existing SDR/STFT/OverSuppression classes reduce with torch.mean internally, so weighting them means editing them.

**Findings**

- **Target speech starts essentially at frame 0 in nine rows out of ten; the row shape that the onset guard protects (near talker entering with no prior speech) is not rare in training - it is the default, with the correct action being KEEP** — onset median 0.00 s, p10 0.00, p90 0.44 s; <=0.1 s on 81.7%, <=0.5 s on 90.7%, >=1 s on 6.6%, >=2 s on 5.1% (n=1165 target-present rows of 1206). Stricter -25 dB detector: <=0.5 s on 87.5% _(scratchpad tables.md section 'detector = pipeline' row ALL; corpus is data/dns5-read.chapters.train.list (62,148 utts, all 30.93-31.0 s) cropped by dynamic_base.py:633-678 align_audio_list (random offset, up to 10 retries to avoid an all-silent window))_
- **The wrong-anchor shape (another talker holds the floor >=1 s, then the near talker enters) covers about 6% of rows and comes almost entirely from turn-taking on real-recording rows** — any interferer before onset 15.8% of rows; >=1 s 6.0% (72/1206); >=2 s 4.6%; >=3 s 2.2%. Of the 72 rows with >=1 s: 31 real_near_keep, 29 real_far_itf, 12 synthetic. On turn-taking rows alone: 44.7% have >=1 s of interferer before onset _(tables.md 'far-first shape' table; mechanism = puresound/task/overlap_gating.py:189-226 _turn_script (far_first_prob 0.5, turn_far_seconds 2.0-4.5 s), enabled only via voice_isolation.py:336-344 row overrides because overlap_control.turn_taking_prob is unset (default 0.0, puresound/config/augmentation.py:54))_
- **Nothing in the data teaches an anchor that survives silence: long target-free gaps and re-entries after a long absence are absent, and structurally impossible on the bucket that holds half the rows** — longest target-free gap: median 0.53 s, p90 1.81 s; >=2 s 9.0%, >=3 s 5.8%, >=5 s 0.3% (4/1165); re-entry after >=5 s of absence 0.3%; max interior gap observed 6.64 s. Per bucket, frac(gap>=5 s): 3 s 0.000 (max gap ever 1.71 s), 6 s 0.002, 12 s 0.017, 30 s 0.033. Longest gap with NO speech at all: median 0.02 s, p90 0.71 s, >=2 s on 1.7%, >=5 s on 0.2% _(tables.md rows ALL / bucket=3.0s..30.0s; row length comes from trainer.length_schedule via puresound/task/sampler.py:113-127 and ns.py:310-313)_
- **Row-length exposure is much more short-row-heavy than the configured batch schedule suggests, because batch size travels with the bucket** — rows per bucket 600/456/120/30 = 49.8% / 37.8% / 9.9% / 2.5% for 3/6/12/30 s, against configured BATCH shares 0.25/0.30/0.25/0.20. Speed perturbation makes realised lengths 2.86-3.0, 5.71-6.0, 11.43-12.0, 28.57-30.0 s (no padding back up) _(tables.md 'length buckets'; config length_schedule n_spk 20/12/6/2; speed perturb ns.py:487-510 then fixed-length snip ns.py:595)_
- **3.4% of rows have an exactly silent target, and a third of those are an unintended by-product of turn-taking that is still labelled target_present=1.0** — clean_speech exactly zero on 3.47% of rows (14/404, and 41/1206 in the main pass); planned lone-far (scalar target_absent=1) 2.48% (matches design 0.85*0.20*0.15=2.55%); gated-to-zero-by-turn-taking but labelled present 0.99% (4/404), all four on the 3 s bucket; 0 rows had scalar=1 with a non-zero target _(tables.md 'target-absent provenance'; mechanism = overlap_gating.py:166-187 gates the target with near_mask, which on a 3 s row can be all-False when the far turn opens (turn_far_seconds 2.0-4.5); the metadata scalar comes from plan.target_absent (voice_isolation.py:546-557), while the loss's inactive mask is derived from the waveform (siso.py:305) - so SDR/MR-STFT treat those rows as lone-far while ResidualReferenceLoss(target_present_only) still includes them)_
- **No v16 loss term weights time or segments; the only temporal weighting that exists points away from the row opening** — 7 terms, weights SDR 1.0 / MR-STFT 0.5 / OverSuppression 3.0 / ResidualReference 0.2 / ASRFeature x2 1.0+1.0 / DistHead 0.3 (sum 7.0), plus channel-consistency mask L1 weight 1.0 on 30% of steps. ASRFeatureLoss crop coverage P(t inside the random 6 s crop): 12 s row t=0 0.000, t=1 s 0.167, t=3 s 0.500, t>=6 s 1.000; 30 s row t=0 0.000, t=1 s 0.042, t=3 s 0.125, t>=6 s 0.250; 3 s and 6 s rows 1.000 everywhere _(config lines 350-434; SDRLoss puresound/nnet/loss/sdr.py:130-222 (whole-row ratio); MultiResolutionSTFTLoss stft_loss.py:185-212 (mean over frames/bins); OverSuppressionLoss stft_loss.py:247-262 (torch.mean); ResidualReferenceLoss residual.py:77-82 (F.l1_loss over all samples); ASRFeatureLoss asr_feature.py:85-91 (uniform random crop start); DistHead global pool nnet/lobe/heads.py:246-248; channel consistency siso.py:381-403)_
- **Which losses touch target-absent frames/rows differs per term, and the largest-weight term is identically zero there** — OverSuppression weight 3.0 contributes exactly 0 on an all-zero target (ReLU(|T|^p-|E|^p) with |T| pinned at the 1e-4 stft floor); MR-STFT excludes target-absent ROWS entirely but covers every frame of an active row down to a fixed |X|=1e-4 (~-80 dBFS) floor; SDR routes absent rows to inactive_sdr_loss 'absolute' = 10log10(sum s1^2 + ...) (length-dependent eps floor, no time structure); ResidualReference skips planned lone-far rows and on active rows demands residual == mixture during the target's pauses; DistHead is per-row _(stft_loss.py:196-205 and 247-262; sdr.py:151-172 and 340-381; residual.py:68-82; stft.py power_floor note stft_loss.py:12-42 (v17 loss-floor debt, and v18's negative verdict on relaxing it))_
- **Frame-level weights can be injected with no new plumbing, but only via a new loss class - nothing today can accept them** — providers available to any loss: enhanced, target, batch, inactive_labels, vad_target, vad_logits, background_vad_logits, background_vad_target, dist_preds (9). Batch temporal content: vad_target and background_vad_target at hop 160 (100 fps) plus 22 per-row scalars; zero per-frame weight tensors and no onset index or turn script _(siso.py:317-357 provider table, base.py:18-51 invoke_loss (required_inputs contract); VOICE_ISOLATION_SCALAR_KEYS voice_isolation.py:25-47; the only frame-weighted losses in the tree are VADActivityLoss (nnet/loss/vad.py:105-122, class-conditional weights) and VADHeadBCELoss, neither of which is in the v16 recipe)_
- **The KEEP target is defined differently on real-near rows than on synthetic rows: on real rows the model is trained to reproduce the recording's own room tone** — target 5th-percentile frame level re its peak frame: real_near_keep -38.9 dB (p10 -282.7, p90 -24.4, n=195) vs synth_near+far -56.5 dB and synth_near_only -57.7 dB; target 'active' fraction under the pipeline's own VAD: real_near_keep median 0.98 vs synthetic 0.80 _(tables.md 'KEEP-target noise floor by row type'; real-near rows skip all synthetic reverb and use the recording as its own target (voice_isolation.py:301-306, plan.skip_whole_mix_reverb))_
- **The repo's own data audit tool measures no temporal property at all, which is why this distribution was never on the record** — check_training_data.py reports DRR gap / distances / RT60 / realized target-to-residual, target-absent count and turn-taking count - 0 onset, gap or re-entry statistics _(egs/voice_isolate/scripts/check_training_data.py:161-231 report_separability; dump_training_samples is a wav dump only (puresound/system/runner.py:208-222))_

**Implications for training**

- A v19 knob aimed at onset behaviour cannot be justified as 'the data has no onsets' - 90.7% of rows already start with the target talking, and at t=0 the correct action there is KEEP. What the data has no examples of is a decision that must be re-made after the context has gone stale: target re-entry after >=5 s of absence is 0.3% of rows and 0.0% of the 3 s bucket. If the goal is 'an anchor that survives silence', the row shape has to be manufactured (long quiet interior gaps, near onset after >=5 s, far-first turns) - and it cannot live in the 3 s bucket, which is half the rows.
- The cheapest data-side lever is already a knob: overlap_control.turn_taking_prob is 0.0 in v16, so turn-taking reaches only 11.7% of rows via the realfar 0.3 / realnear 0.5 overrides, and it is the sole generator of the far-first shape (6% of rows) that the reference matrix identifies as the harmful deployment prefix. Raising it (including for synthetic rows) needs no code. Two caveats measured here: on the 3 s bucket the far-first turn gates the near talker to exact zero on ~1% of rows (turning a keep row into a lone-far row that is still labelled target_present=1.0), and turn-taking rows already carry the target-gap mass, so the rate and the bucket have to move together.
- Any 'weight the onset frames' idea needs a new loss class, not a plumbing change: the provider table already exposes vad_target and batch, so a frame- or segment-weighted term can be added inside puresound/nnet/loss/ plus the recipe list. Editing the existing terms is the expensive path (they mean-reduce internally), and v18 is the standing warning that touching the floors of the suppression terms makes every suppression number shallower.
- Before adding weight to the row opening, fix the term that removes it: on the 12 s and 30 s buckets the two ASRFeatureLoss terms (2.0 of 7.0 total weight) supervise t=0 with probability 0.000 and t=1 s with 0.167 / 0.042. Anchoring the crop at the row start on some fraction of steps, or scoring two crops (one row-initial), is a one-argument change in asr_feature.py:85-91 and would restore intelligibility supervision exactly where onset deletions are measured.
- The keep-side target definition is inconsistent across row types (real-near targets carry an 18 dB higher noise floor than synthetic ones). Any new row shape that puts a real near talker after a long silence will also be teaching the model to reproduce that recording's room tone during the silence - which is the opposite of the lone-far rows' 'kill to the floor'. That conflict should be decided explicitly (e.g. gate the real-near target's inactive frames) rather than discovered after a run.
- The row-length schedule delivers 49.8% of rows at 3 s while the config reads as 25%; any per-row phenomenon (turn-taking, long gaps, re-entry) is therefore diluted about twice as much as the schedule's probabilities suggest. Round design should state the target share in ROWS, not in batches.
- scripts/check_training_data.py should grow the temporal columns measured here (onset, interferer-before-onset, longest target gap, re-entry rate, target-absent provenance) so a recipe's onset/anchor content is checkable before a run instead of after; sample_rows.py in the artifacts is a working prototype that uses the recipe's own dataloader.

**Caveats**

- 1206 rows from one seed (7) and 104 consecutive batches of a single epoch, plus 404 rows from seed 21 for the label check. The 30 s bucket has only 30 rows, so its numbers are indicative only; the length draw is seeded by (epoch, batch_idx) so a different epoch reshuffles bucket order but not the marginals.
- Activity is measured with the pipeline's own EnergyVADLabeler grid and rule (frame 400 / hop 160, > -40 dB relative to the row's own peak frame - my reimplementation matched batch['vad_target'] exactly), cross-checked with a stricter -25 dB detector; both are reported and agree (onset <=0.5 s on 90.7% vs 87.5%). On real-near rows the target carries its own noise floor, so its active fraction is inflated (median 0.98) and its measured onset is biased early - the -25 dB column is the safer read for those rows.
- Interferer activity uses batch['far_target'] (summed post-SIR interferer speech, pre device chain). Noise is never counted as speech, so 'no speech at all' gaps still contain the row's noise/floor whenever those branches fired; P(neither recorded noise nor capture floor) is 0.2*0.5 = 0.10 by config, which I did not measure per row.
- Row types are inferred from the documented NaN pattern of the DRR/distance labels (real-near => fg_drr NaN with fg_dist present; real-far => interferer DRR NaN with interferer distance present), not from an explicit row-type flag in the batch. The planned-lone-far vs gated-to-zero split comes from the target_absent scalar in the second pass.
- No model was run and nothing here measures model behaviour; the connection to onset/wrong-anchor deletions rests on the existing probe READMEs, not on this audit. I also did not verify v8's data distribution - v8 uses the 10.33 s dns5-read metafile without stitch_to_length or the ASR crop (its other data blocks are identical), so the onset/gap numbers above are v16's; at 6 s rows v8's 10.33 s utterances also always crop rather than pad, so I expect the same onset picture, but that is not measured.
- Nothing in the repo was modified; the two sampling runs were read-only CPU passes over the training dataloader.

**Artifacts**

## D: Does compression-invariance training (v11b) actually fix the representation the QVF chain inverts, does an offline compression probe show that compression flips the distance cue, and is chain invariance of the distance/DRR readout a trainable target with a fast in-loop metric?

No, on all counts that matter. I built the anchor_gate cache for v11b ep31 (the latest checkpoint that exists under exp/dpcrn_v11b_compinv; 499 field records, same 47 clips x 8 conditions as v8/v16) and ran the same readability metric. The QVF clip-scope distance AUC is 0.258 for v11b vs 0.265 for v8 and 0.255 for v16 — a paired clip-level bootstrap gives v11b−v8 = −0.006 [−0.161, +0.115], p 0.893; QVF session distance +0.020, p 0.707; QVF clip DRR +0.042, p 0.167; QVF session DRR +0.011, p 0.980. Per recording group the inversion is total and unmoved: 12 of 12 (4 QVF groups x 3 checkpoints) sit below 0.20, and v11b still reads QVF near speech farther (0.988 m) than QVF far speech (0.771 m). The one number v11b does move is the device-chain streaming DRR readout, 0.757 → 0.903 (p < 0.001) — real, but on the rig chain only. The offline probe then explains why the round could not work: benchmarks/probes/eq_probe.py never applied its compressor (compressor_gain returns a gain curve; device_chain._compressor multiplies it in), and on these low-level field clips that curve is all-ones, so the model was fed a constant DC signal (measured std 2.3e-10 / exactly 0) — which is where "1.009 → 0.582 m" came from, and I reproduced the artifact (three operating points returning identical 0.582/0.608 m) before fixing it. With the gain actually multiplied in at the recipe's −28 dBFS operating level, the envelope compressor moves the device readout by |dAUC| <= 0.013 at every operating point on all three checkpoints (0.989 → 0.990 for v8 at thr −28/r4), i.e. it is inert on this readout; the |x|^p waveshaper (a different operator, the one whose causality was actually established) does move it — far-talker DRR −0.55 → +5.00 dB at p 0.4, corroborating the historical +5.19 — but only degrades distance AUC to 0.880, never inverts it. Nothing I applied to a device clip reproduced 0.26. Finally, a fresh logistic probe on the same cached bottleneck window reads QVF near-vs-far at 0.789 (v8) when fitted on device recordings only and 0.999 when fitted on other QVF recordings (leave-one-group-out), against the DistHead's own 0.26: the information is in the representation, the trained readout is what inverts. So chain invariance of this readout is a trainable target and the AUC is a cheap in-loop metric (47 clips, one forward each, seconds per validation epoch — no such hook exists today, validation_step logs losses only), but it must be attacked at the head/readout, and the metric's resolution is ±0.14 on QVF clip scope: a round should be called uninteresting below 0.40, minimally useful at >= 0.50, and on target at >= 0.75 (where the device streaming readout already sits and the AnchorGate demonstrably works, and just under the 0.79–0.83 measured ceiling of a device-fitted linear read).

**Findings**

- **v11b (compression invariance, ep31) does not repair the QVF distance readout at all; its only measurable representation gain is device-chain streaming DRR readability.** — QVF clip dist AUC: v8 0.265, v16 0.255, v11b 0.258; paired bootstrap v11b−v8 = −0.006 [−0.161,+0.115] p 0.893. QVF session dist +0.020 p 0.707. QVF clip DRR +0.042 p 0.167. QVF session DRR +0.011 p 0.980. Device session DRR: 0.757 → 0.903, +0.145 [+0.102,+0.188] p<0.001. Device clip dist 0.989 → 0.962 (−0.027, p 0.102). _(New v11b cache built with anchor_gate_cache.py field (cuda:1, 499 records) from exp/dpcrn_v11b_compinv/lightning_logs/version_0/checkpoints/epoch=31-step=16000.ckpt + config/exp/train_dpcrn_v11b_compinv.yaml; anchor_gate_sim.py readability --tags v8 v16 v11b (reproduces the recorded v8/v16 table exactly); paired bootstrap in auc_delta.py. Tables in TABLES.md §1, §5.)_
- **The inversion is per-recording and complete on every QVF group, for every checkpoint — not a pooling artifact, and not narrowed by v11b.** — QVF clip dist AUC by group (gym/plumbing/price/scenario3): v8 0.191/0.170/0.047/0.041; v16 0.087/0.074/0.000/0.035; v11b 0.091/0.000/0.073/0.092. Prefix anchor reads on QVF, v11b: near_speech 0.988 m vs far_speech 0.771 m (v8: 0.869 vs 0.715). _(per_group_auc.py over the condition=none records of all three caches; readability prefix table. TABLES.md §2, §3.)_
- **benchmarks/probes/eq_probe.py's compressor conditions are invalid: compressor_gain returns a gain curve that the probe never multiplied in, so on these clips the model was shown a constant DC signal. eq_probe_README Verdict 2 rests on that.** — Clip peaks −33.8 (90d_far1) / −23.7 (90d_near1) / −26.2 dBFS (qvf_price_far1) are below the −28 dB threshold, so the curve has exactly 1 unique value; eq_probe's rms_restore(comp(x),x) then has std 2.33e-10 / 0.0 / 1.4e-09. My reproduction of the bug returns byte-identical medians for thr −34/r2, −28/r4, −22/r6 (0.582 m keep, 0.608 m suppress) — the signature. _(compressor_gain docstring ('Returned rather than applied') vs puresound/task/device_chain.py:312-322 which multiplies; direct measurement printed in this session; comp_readability.py (the deliberate reproduction) and its comp_v8/v16/v11b.json, TABLES.md §6.)_
- **With the compressor correctly applied at the recipe's operating level, the envelope compressor v11b trains against does not move the distance/DRR readout at all — so the round trained invariance to an axis that was already flat.** — Device chain, W=1s, v8 AUC dist: none 0.989; thr−34/r2 0.987; thr−28/r4 0.990; thr−22/r6 0.989; thr−40/r6 0.983. v11b: 0.962/0.962/0.962/0.963/0.948. Keep/suppress medians within 0.02 m of baseline. Gain reduction actually applied: mean 0.00–1.05 dB, max 5.9–16.2 dB (makeup normalisation pins mean gain to 1.0). _(comp_readability2.py (gain multiplied in, clip normalised to gain_normalized_to −28 dBFS before the stage and restored after, activity mask frozen from the unmodified clip); comp2_v8/v16/v11b.json; TABLES.md §7.)_
- **The |x|^p waveshaper does move the cue and reproduces the historical +5.19 dB DRR walk, but it degrades rather than inverts: no chain stage I applied to a device clip reproduced the QVF 0.26.** — v8 device, waveshaper p 0.8/0.6/0.4: AUC dist 0.981/0.957/0.880; suppress-span DRR −0.55 → +0.70/+3.42/+5.00 dB (record: −0.61 → +5.19); suppress distance 1.043 → 0.950 m. broadcast EQ 0.974, comp+broadcast 0.978, dark-tilt control 0.987. v11b is not more robust (p 0.4: 0.962 → 0.865). _(comp_readability2.py waveshaper conditions (same operator as benchmarks/probes/compression_probe.py, which does apply its transform); TABLES.md §7.)_
- **The near/far information IS in the bottleneck on the QVF chain and transfers across recordings and even across chains; the DistHead's learned mapping is what inverts.** — Logistic probe on the same pooled 128-d window, holdout unit = recording group: device-fit → device-test (LOGO) 0.999/0.999/1.000; device-fit → QVF-test 0.789 (v8) / 0.827 (v16) / 0.680 (v11b); QVF-fit → held-out-QVF-group 0.999/0.998/0.986; within-group in-sample 1.000. DistHead itself: 0.265/0.255/0.258. Slot 2 (interferer distance), never read before, is inverted on QVF too: 0.168/0.129/0.189. _(linear_probe.py + linear_probe.json (TABLES.md §8); slot2_auc.py + slot2_auc.json (TABLES.md §9).)_
- **The AUC metric is cheap enough for in-loop use but has hard resolution limits on the QVF side, and it is not a deployment gate.** — Paired bootstrap half-width on QVF clip dist AUC ≈ ±0.14 (n = 9 keep / 6 suppress recordings); marginal 95% CI [0.054, 0.668]. QVF session: [0.056, 0.935] with n = 3 — unusable as a gate. Cost: 47 clips x 1 forward ≈ seconds per validation epoch. v11b moved device-session DRR AUC +0.145 (p<0.001) while v11b_VERDICT records anchored sessions falling from −12.35/−11.29/−9.25 to −6.64/−5.95/−6.02 dB. _(auc_ci.py / auc_ci.json (clip-level bootstrap, B=2000) and auc_delta.json (paired, B=4000); puresound/system/siso.py::validation_step logs only losses today; benchmarks/probes/v11b_VERDICT.md for the suppression cost.)_

**Implications for training**

- Do NOT open a v19 round on input-side chain augmentation for the QVF distance inversion. The stage the previous round guessed at (compressor_gain) is measurably inert on this readout (|dAUC| <= 0.013), and the features already carry the cue (device-fitted linear probe reads QVF at 0.789). The lever is the readout objective, not the data pipeline.
- The concrete trainable target is the DistHead itself: add a discriminative near-vs-far contrast on the pooled window beside (or instead of) the metres regression, and/or a chain-consistency loss that forces the readout to agree across two synthetic device-chain draws of the same row. The second needs no field data and has high resolution, so it can be the in-loop objective while the field AUC is the acceptance check.
- Add a validation hook (on_validation_epoch_end in puresound/system/siso.py, which today logs losses only) that scores a frozen list of the 47 cold-start field clips and logs auc_dist_qvf, auc_dist_device, auc_drr_device_session. Cost is seconds per epoch. Gate bands: uninteresting < 0.40 on QVF clip dist, minimally useful >= 0.50 (sign of the cue correct), target >= 0.75; guards device clip dist >= 0.95, device session DRR >= 0.75, no QVF group below 0.35.
- Withdraw eq_probe_README.md Verdict 2 and the priority ordering it set. 'Compression + mild production EQ fully accounts for the phantom near user' and 'codec and noise-gate probes drop in priority' were derived from a probe that fed the model a DC signal. Codec (augmentation_codec is off in the v11b/v16 recipes), noise gate, and room/mic geometry go back onto the candidate list; the standing +5.19 dB claim in device_chain._compressor's and augmentation.py's docstrings belongs to the |x|^p waveshaper, not to compressor_gain.
- Keep the relative / self-calibrated program as the near-term answer for the QVF chain. Answer 3 says the boundary is chain-offset rather than absent, which is exactly the presence_selfcal Finding 1 structure (within-session 0.886/0.911 vs pooled 0.474); a session-relative DistHead rule is a cheaper path to a working gate than any retraining, and the OnsetGuard half already ships with no readout at all.
- Any readout-axis round must be judged on the set-v3 block scorecard as well as the AUC. v11b is the counterexample: +0.145 device-session DRR AUC (p<0.001) bought with roughly half the anchored far suppression. Report both or the round will look like a win.

**Caveats**

- v11b ep31 was stopped mid-cosine by hand and was trained on the pre-2026-08-18 synthesis distribution; v8 is also old-distribution, v16 is new. The readability AUC is computed on real recordings only, so the distribution boundary does not invalidate this comparison, but v11b's absolute level is not a trough number.
- The QVF side of the field set is tiny: 9 keep and 6 suppress clips across 4 recording groups, 3 sessions. Paired half-width ±0.14 on clip scope, and QVF session AUC CIs span [0.06, 0.97]. Nothing smaller than a ~0.15 move is resolvable, and QVF session numbers cannot gate anything until the row count grows.
- The linear probe is fitted per frame and frames inside a clip are strongly correlated, so the 0.986-0.999 leave-one-group-out figures are optimistic as an absolute; the load-bearing number is the cross-chain one (device-fit -> QVF, 0.68-0.83), which no fitting choice can inflate in the model's favour.
- The offline chain probe covers one compressor family (three thresholds/ratios plus a hard-knee point), one broadcast EQ curve, one dark-tilt control and three waveshaper strengths, at a single normalisation level, on 47 clips, medians only. Codec, packet loss, and noise gating were not applied. So 'no modelled stage reproduces the inversion' is a statement about these stages, not about every chain factor.
- The activity mask and keep/suppress spans come from the set-v3 windows.json labels; per the span-energy lesson those spans contain speech plus noise plus reverb, so the AUC inherits any label imprecision. It is the same mask for every condition and checkpoint, so comparisons are clean even where the absolute level is not.
- I did not build a Dawn cache for v11b and ran no ASR, no field block scorecard and no training. Everything here is readout measurement; the deployment consequences are quoted from v11b_VERDICT.md, not re-measured.

## D: Does the model show the same anchor behaviour on SYNTHETIC data as on real recordings — (i) needs ~1 s of a talker before it suppresses others, (ii) forgets the anchor after ~5-10 s of quiet, (iii) deletes a NEW near talker who follows a different anchor?

All three reproduce on synthetic data (wer_set_moderate_test, n=200, paired per utterance), on both checkpoint families, with the same time constants as the real recordings — so the training data/objective produce the anchor, and can change it. (i) On a lone interferer (mix−ref, user absent) the anchor-length sweep on the first second of the interferer's speech goes 0.25 s −0.48, 0.5 s −3.67, 1 s −12.61, 2 s −13.85, 3 s −13.62 dB (v8; v16 −0.70/−4.14/−9.72/−9.50/−10.75) against the field's 1 s −13.14 / 2 s −19.04 — same knee at 1 s, same saturation at 2 s, and the same three nulls: room-tone analogue −1.39/−0.55, bystander-only −0.09/−0.04 (n.s.), and a FAR talker anchor +0.85/+1.65 (slightly protective). (ii) With a gap-only control subtracted (a long quiet prefix is itself a suppress bias) the anchor decays −8.23 → −4.00 → −2.81 → −0.89 dB at 2/5/10/20 s (v8; v16 −7.93/−4.57/−2.01/−0.70), i.e. half-life 4–5 s and ~90 % gone by 20 s, versus the field's half-gone-at-5-s / gone-by-10-s — the same mechanism with a roughly 2× longer constant. (iii) A 2–3 s anchor from a different talker deletes the next near talker by −1.71/−1.47 dB (v8) and −1.25/−1.33 (v16) over the first second of that talker's speech, p≈1e-25, 176/24 utterances down, while the SAME talker's anchor costs nothing (+0.01, p=0.82); Δ after 2 s is only −0.10 dB, which is why the whole-span figure is a mere −0.40 dB. That resolves the apparent size gap with the field, whose near clips are short enough that its −1.8 dB whole-span number IS an onset number. New fact from splitting the confound: an anchor by the SAME speaker in a DIFFERENT room already costs −0.44/−0.40 dB, about 30 % of the different-talker effect, so the template is part talker-identity and part channel signature. The one real divergence is severity, not mechanism: synthetic cold start already suppresses a lone interferer by −6.4/−6.3 dB (103–106/200 clips pass ≤−6 dB) where the real chain sits at −1.06/−1.71 dB with 4/20 passing.

**Findings**

- **(i) REPRODUCED on synthetic: suppression of a lone interferer needs ~1 s of a near talker and saturates at 2 s, matching the field curve almost quantitatively.** — bgonly arm, Δ vs `none` over the first 1 s of interferer speech, dB. v8: 0.25 s −0.48, 0.5 s −3.67, 1 s −12.61, 2 s −13.85, 3 s −13.62. v16 ep19: −0.70 / −4.14 / −9.72 / −9.50 / −10.75. Field (reference_matrix §3): 0.25/0.5 s ≈ 0, 1 s −13.14, 2 s −19.04, 3 s −19.25. Same `self`-anchor sweep on v8: −0.57 / −2.43 / −8.04 / −13.82. _(TABLE_bgonly.md, 200 utts, paired Wilcoxon p 6e-19 at 1 s; conditions built as prefixes on (mix − ref))_
- **The three field nulls reproduce exactly: room tone, bystander noise and a FAR talker do not arm suppression — only a near talker does.** — bgonly Δon1, dB (v8 / v16): quiet-floor analogue −1.39 / −0.55; bystanders-only (ref-inactive mix frames, n=126) −0.09 (p 0.92) / −0.04 (p 0.75); the interferers themselves as a 3 s anchor +0.85 / +1.65 (protective, p 1.4e-12). Field: true floor −0.04 / +0.03 n.s., far speech −2.75 vs near speech −15.12. _(TABLE_bgonly.md rows quiet3 / bg3 / bgsub3)_
- **(ii) REPRODUCED: the anchor is forgotten with a few-second time constant, once a gap-only control is subtracted. The gap's content is irrelevant (quiet vs background agree), as in the field.** — Δdecay − Δgap paired per utterance, bgonly, dB. v8 quiet gap: 2 s −8.23, 5 s −4.00, 10 s −2.81, 20 s −0.89 (p 2e-24 … 7e-17). v16: −7.93 / −4.57 / −2.01 / −0.70. Background gap v8: −7.10 / −3.97 / −1.55 / −0.48. Uncorrected gap-only controls are only −0.19..−1.06. Field: 2 s −14.56, 5 s −9.83, 10 s −0.92, 20 s −0.25. _(TABLE_bgonly.md 'decay, corrected for the gap-only control' section; gapq{N}/gapbg{N} conditions added specifically because silence3 alone is a −4.3/−6.9 dB suppress bias)_
- **(iii) REPRODUCED and localised to the onset: a different-talker anchor deletes the next near talker, the same talker's anchor does not. The sign contrast is the mechanism.** — utt arm Δon1 (first 1 s of the user), dB, v8 / v16: self3 +0.01 (p 0.82) / +0.04; other 1 s −1.07 / −0.84; other 2 s −1.71 / −1.25; other 3 s −1.47 / −1.33 (p 3e-25 / 2.8e-24). Δlate (after 2 s) only −0.10 / −0.12. Whole-span other3 −0.40 / −0.44, 176/24 and 173/27 utts down. `del < −3 dB` count 126 → 142 (v8), 131 → 143 (v16) of 200. SI-SDRi Δ: self3 +0.43, other3 −0.60. _(TABLE_utt.md; onset windows db_on1/db_on2/db_late added because the whole-span median hides the effect)_
- **The synthetic and field magnitudes for (iii) agree once measurement windows are matched — the field's whole-span number is effectively an onset number because its near clips are short.** — Field near clips: median −0.18 → −2.01 dB with a 2 s anchor (Δ ≈ −1.8), violations 0 → 4–6 of 17. Synthetic Δon1 at 2 s: −1.71 (v8) / −1.25 (v16). Synthetic whole-span at 2 s: only −0.40, over ~9 s utterances. _(TABLE_utt.md Δon1 vs Δlate columns compared with reference_matrix_README §3 table)_
- **NEW: the recent-talker template is part speaker identity and part channel signature — changing only the room, with the same speaker, already costs ~30 % of the wrong-anchor deletion. Byte-identity memorisation is ruled out.** — ident arm Δon1, dB, v8 (1 s / 3 s) then v16: self +0.02/+0.01, +0.04/+0.04 (null); selflate (same talker+room, later excerpt) −0.05/−0.04, −0.05/−0.01 (n.s.); samespk (same talker, different room draw) −0.52/−0.44 (p 3.3e-19 / 1.2e-14), −0.45/−0.40; diffspk −1.07/−1.47, −0.84/−1.33. _(TABLE_ident.md; 37 of 38 speakers have >=2 utterances in the set, each with its own room draw)_
- **The relative-distance rule is real but weak and does not explain the deletion: a nearer anchor deletes more, yet the deletion stays highly significant even when the anchor was farther than the user.** — Spearman rho(anchor_dist − user_dist, Δon1) = +0.149 (p 0.035) v8, +0.139 (p 0.05) v16. Terciles v8: anchor nearer (median d −0.24 m) −1.72 dB, similar −1.49, anchor farther (+0.24 m) −1.02 — all p <= 7e-10. v16: −1.77 / −1.28 / −0.78. _(TABLE_strat.md, built from the manifest's near_dist and the logged partner id)_
- **Digital silence is not a neutral prefix on synthetic data either — it is the most destructive onset condition measured, reproducing the field warning.** — bgonly Δon1: −5.90 dB (v8), −19.22 dB (v16). utt-arm Δon1: −0.28 (v8), −2.33 (v16) — larger than any real anchor on v16. Field: digital silence −2.58/−1.92 on the device chain, catastrophic on QVF. _(TABLE_bgonly.md / TABLE_utt.md silence3 rows)_
- **The one place synthetic and real diverge is cold-start SEVERITY, not the mechanism: the training distribution's lone-interferer case is already half-solved with no anchor at all.** — bgonly `none` absolute: −6.40 dB (v8) / −6.25 (v16), with 106/200 and 103/200 clips already passing <= −6 dB. Real cold far: −1.06 (v8) / −1.71 (v16), 4/20 passing. _(TABLE_bgonly.md `none` row vs reference_matrix_README §2 block table)_
- **Both checkpoint families agree row for row on every condition, so the mechanism is not an artifact of one training run or one synthesis distribution (v8 predates the 2026-08-18 linearity change, v16 follows it).** — e.g. bgonly other3 Δ −9.67 (v8) vs −9.89 (v16); decayq5 corrected −4.00 vs −4.57; utt other3 whole-span −0.40 vs −0.44; ident samespk3 −0.44 vs −0.40. An independent first pass without onset/gap-control columns (out_v1/) agrees to within 0.1–0.3 dB. _(out_v1/ and out2/ row files, both models, all three arms)_

**Implications for training**

- The objective CAN be changed, because the behaviour is in-distribution. Onset deletion and wrong-anchor deletion are now measurable on 200 synthetic utterances in ~10 min per checkpoint with no real recordings, no field set and no ASR — usable as a training-time metric or early-stopping signal, which the field set cannot be (FIELD_BLOCK requires 5-checkpoint blocks).
- Weight the loss on the first 1–2 s of each talker turn. Δlate is ~0 dB in every condition while Δon1 is −1.5..−1.7 dB; all the damage is at onset. The failing case is exactly a concatenation row 'talker A 3 s -> talker B' scored on B's first second, and it is buildable from wer_set_moderate_test today (partner utterances by a different speaker already wired in the probe).
- A persistent 'user' representation cannot be built on channel cues: the same speaker in a different room already loses ~30 % of the protection (samespk −0.44 dB vs self +0.01), and that is precisely the deployment case (new session, user moved). Axis 5 of v12_candidate_axes needs a target invariant to the room draw yet sensitive to the talker — the probe's self/selflate/samespk/diffspk ladder is the readout for whether a v19 run achieves that.
- Never use digital silence as the neutral prefix in a training or eval row: −5.9 dB (v8) / −19.2 dB (v16) suppress bias on the lone-interferer arm and −2.33 dB onset deletion on v16's keep side. Any curriculum that pads with zeros is teaching 'quiet then sound = suppress'.
- Do not expect an anchor fix on this set to fix the real-chain cold start. Synthetic cold start is already −6.3 dB while the real chain is −1.1 dB; the training distribution reproduces the mechanism but never presents the hard case. Cold-start severity has to come from a different axis (reference_matrix §6.3 and eq_probe attribute the QVF wall to the chain), so the v19 gate must keep the field cold-far column separate from this synthetic one.
- Because (ii) and (iii) share one clock — the wrong-anchor deletion decays with the same few-second constant and closes by 20 s (p=0.24 v8 / 0.93 v16) — any training change that lengthens the anchor's memory to survive silence will, on this architecture, also lengthen the window in which the next talker gets deleted. reference_matrix §6.2's warning is confirmed on synthetic data, so a v19 design must break the coupling explicitly rather than just extending the state's memory.
- The OnsetGuard pna clause has a training-side counterpart that is now measurable: the guard removes half the keep violations by refusing to attenuate before a 1 s talker is confirmed, and this probe shows the model's own onset behaviour is the thing to retrain. Report Δon1 alongside the guard's numbers so 'the model no longer needs the guard' becomes a checkable claim.

**Caveats**

- The primary metric is an energy proxy, not ASR. No WER, no listening test, no harmonic peak-valley check was run, so per the span-energy-conflation lesson these numbers cannot distinguish removed speech from removed noise or reverb tail. Treat magnitudes as proxies and the sign contrasts (self vs other) as the robust part.
- del_db at the `none` baseline is −3.6..−3.7 dB and is NOT deletion: over ref-active frames the mix also contains the interferers the model is supposed to remove. Only the paired Δ vs `none` is interpretable as deletion. Same for the bgonly arm's absolute column.
- One checkpoint per family (v8, v16 ep19), not a 5-checkpoint block. Per-utterance paired differences within a checkpoint are valid (the reference_matrix §5 argument) and n=200 gives p<1e-20 on the key rows, but every absolute column is a single-point reading and the v8-vs-v16 magnitude differences (e.g. 1 s anchor −12.6 vs −9.7) are within the range that FIELD_BLOCK says single checkpoints cannot resolve.
- The 'quiet' gap is the quietest 30 % of (mix − ref) frames — low-level noise, not true room tone. There is no true-floor material in a synthetic set, and digital silence is disqualified as the control because it is itself a large suppress bias. The gap-only controls bound this, but the decay constant is measured against a noise floor, not a room floor.
- The samespk anchor changes the utterance and its level as well as the room, so it is not a pure channel swap; it bounds the channel contribution from above. Conversely diffspk changes talker and room together. The ordering self(null) < samespk < diffspk is clean, the 30 % split is approximate.
- The bg3 and decaybg/gapbg conditions run on n=126, not 200: 74 of 200 utterances have under 0.3 s of ref-inactive frames (the same fallback count reference_matrix §5 reports), so those rows are a biased subset of utterances with more silence.
- v8 predates the 2026-08-18 synthesis linearity change while wer_set_moderate_test and v16 follow it, so v8 is being probed slightly out of its training distribution. The two families agreeing on every row argues the mechanism is not distribution-specific, but v8's absolute numbers carry that caveat.
- The synthetic 'interferer' in the bgonly arm is RIR-convolved speech plus noise, not a real far-field recording; the closed RIR axis means this arm cannot speak to real far-field transfer, only to what the training distribution teaches.
- Anchor material is built by concatenating 20 ms frames selected by a reference-energy VAD, which introduces splice discontinuities absent from real prefixes. selflate being null suggests splicing alone is not driving the effect, but it was not tested directly.
