# Proposals and verdicts


## v19a — Offset-free proximity readout (ProxHead + within-row contrast + nonlinear chain consistency)

_Angle:_ Representation first: make the distance/DRR readout invariant to the capture chain by rebuilding it as a per-frame, difference-only (offset-free) proximity score and requiring it to preserve within-row contrasts across two chain renderings — with readability AUC on the QVF field clips as the in-loop metric and the device chain held at >=0.95. Onset/anchor is explicitly a later round on top.

The DistHead is asked to predict absolute metres from a whole-clip mean pool, and that is exactly the kind of readout this project has already watched die twice: an absolute calibration that a new capture chain shifts. The measured evidence says the features are fine and the head is the problem — a logistic probe on the *same* cached bottleneck window, fitted on device recordings only, reads QVF near-vs-far at 0.827 (v16) while the trained DistHead on the identical features reads 0.255, inverted, on 12 of 12 QVF group x checkpoint cells. So v19a stops trying to fix the input distribution (v11b's compressor is measurably inert on this readout, |dAUC| <= 0.013) and rebuilds the readout so that a per-chain offset cannot change its answer: a per-frame ProxHead that is LayerNorm'd over channels, supervised only through the difference between the row's near-active frames and its far-only frames, thresholded at the row's own midpoint, and penalised whenever a second rendering of the same row through a different — including nonlinear — chain changes that difference. Nothing in the suppression objective is touched, no data row changes, and the head's output layer is zero-initialised so the run begins at exactly v16 ep19's function. The prize is a readout the shipped AnchorGate's relative-DRR arm can actually read off-rig, which is the arm that cut Dawn deletion 0.230 -> 0.096; the round's own claim is only about the representation.

**Objective**

ARCHITECTURE (puresound/nnet/lobe/heads.py, new class next to DistHead; DistHead untouched).
Bottleneck x is [N, C, F, T] (dpcrn.py:292; C = channels[-1]).

  ProxHead.forward(x):
      h = x.mean(dim=2)                                  # [N,C,T] — pool F ONLY (matches the agcache `feat` layout, so offline probes and the trained head read the same vector)
      h = LayerNorm(C, elementwise_affine=False)(h^T)^T  # per-frame channel offset+scale removed
      h = SiLU(Conv1d(C -> 128, k=1)(h))
      h = SiLU(Conv1d(128 -> 128, k=9, groups=128)(left_pad(h, 8)))   # causal, 90 ms RF
      s = Conv1d(128 -> 2, k=1)(h)                       # [N,2,T]; s[:,0]=proximity, s[:,1]=DRR-contrast
      nn.init.zeros_(out.weight); nn.init.zeros_(out.bias)

Backbone sets self.last_prox_logits = s (mirrors last_dist_preds, dpcrn.py:292-295); a new
provider "prox_logits" is added to siso._loss_providers (siso.py:349-357).

FRAME MASKS — from providers that already exist; no dataset change:
      near = vad_target > 0.5                                  # 100 fps, ns.py:641
      far  = (background_vad_target > 0.5) & ~near              # 100 fps, ns.py:643-645, already a provider
  row eligible iff batch['n_interferers'] >= 1 AND |near| >= 10 frames AND |far| >= 10 frames.
  (Real-near rows are excluded by construction: audit measured their target active fraction at
  median 0.98 with an 18 dB higher noise floor, so their "far-only" frames are not far-only.)

LOSS 1 — L_rank (row-level ordering; invariant to s -> s + b):
      mu_n = mean(s0 | near); mu_f = mean(s0 | far)
      L_rank = mean_rows softplus((mu_f - mu_n + m) / tau),      m = 2.0, tau = 1.0

LOSS 2 — L_frame (self-calibration made differentiable: the threshold is the row's OWN midpoint):
      thr = 0.5 * (mu_n.detach() + mu_f.detach())               # [N,1]
      L_frame = BCEWithLogits((s0 - thr)/tau, y = 1 on near, 0 on far), averaged over near|far
  Same construction on s1 against the row's DRR contrast sign where foreground_drr and
  strongest_interferer_drr are both finite (synthetic rows only; NaN-masked as DistHead already is).

LOSS 3 — L_var (anti-collapse; a constant head satisfies the consistency terms for free):
      L_var = mean_rows relu(0.5 - std_over_frames(s0))

LOSS 4 — L_ccprox (chain consistency ON THE READOUT, in training_step beside the existing
mask-consistency block, siso.py:381-403, because it needs the second forward):
      c  = mu_n(s)  - mu_f(s)                                   # clean view (teacher, detached)
      c' = mu_n(s') - mu_f(s')                                  # perturbed view
      center(s) = s - mean(s | near|far)
      L_ccprox = mean |c' - c.detach()| + mean |center(s') - center(s).detach()|
  Both terms are exactly invariant to an additive per-row/per-chain offset, so the loss cannot be
  satisfied by re-calibrating a bias — only by making the contrast itself chain-independent.

PERTURBATION GROUPS (_random_channel_perturb, siso.py:262-286, grows a `group` argument):
  - linear (existing): cosine-series EQ +-6 dB over 4 orders + gain +-4 dB. H(f) real positive
    => ideal complex ratio mask unchanged => BOTH the existing mask-consistency L1 and L_ccprox apply.
  - nonlinear (new; one drawn per firing): (a) sign(x)*|x|^p, p ~ U(0.4, 1.0), RMS restored — the
    ONLY operator measured to move this readout (device dist AUC 0.989 -> 0.880 at p 0.4; far-talker
    DRR walk -0.55 -> +5.00 dB); (b) envelope compressor with the gain curve actually multiplied in
    (device_chain._compressor, NOT compressor_gain's returned curve — the eq_probe bug); (c) downward
    expander / noise gate, threshold = row_rms - U(10,25) dB, q ~ U(1.5,3); (d) broadcast EQ + dark tilt.
    MASK CONSISTENCY IS DISABLED ON NONLINEAR VIEWS — a nonlinear chain genuinely changes the ideal
    mask, so demanding mask equality there would train the model to ignore a real signal. Only
    L_ccprox applies. This is the one place the existing code cannot be reused unchanged.
  Firing schedule stays rank-synchronised on batch_idx % period (a per-rank random draw desyncs the
  SyncBatchNorm all-gathers and deadlocks DDP), max_rows = 4.

TOTAL OBJECTIVE:
      L = L_v16(7 terms, unchanged except DistHeadRegressionLoss 0.3 -> 0.15)
          + w_prox * (L_rank + L_frame) + 0.1 * L_var + 1.0 * L_ccprox
      w_prox ramps 0 -> 0.3 linearly over the first 2 epochs (zero-init output + ramp => the run
      starts at exactly v16 ep19's function, the zero-init parallel method kept from the v14 round).
DistHeadRegressionLoss stays at 0.15 as the device-path calibration anchor — it is what produced
the 0.989 device AUC the guard's device arm reads. NO suppression term is modified: v18
(inactive_mode=relative) is the standing warning that relaxing the suppression floors makes every
suppression number shallower (cold-far +0.53 dB, p 0.017).

**Data changes**

- NONE required, deliberately. The round is data-neutral so its verdict is attributable to the readout objective alone. Both frame-label sources (vad_target for near, background_vad_target for far speech, both 100 fps) are already emitted by the dataset (ns.py:623-645) and already loss providers (siso.py:349-357).
- augmentation_compressor stays OFF (v11b's only unique knob; measured inert on this readout at |dAUC| <= 0.013, and v11b's suppression cost is confounded with v11a's presence heads — importing it would import an unattributed confound).
- augmentation_codec stays OFF in arm A. It goes back on the candidate list only because eq_probe Verdict 2 is withdrawn (that probe fed the model a DC signal, std 2.3e-10), and it belongs to arm B if arm A passes.
- Pre-flight measurement, not a change: one CPU pass of the v16 dataloader (reuse the audit's sample_rows.py) to report the ELIGIBLE-ROW SHARE under the rule n_interferers >= 1 AND >= 10 near frames AND >= 10 far-only frames, broken down by row type and length bucket. The audit measured synthetic target active fraction at median 0.80 (so ~20% near-inactive frames exist) and real-near at 0.98; if eligible rows fall below ~50% of rows or are dominated by one row type, relax to >= 5 frames or add far-only frames via overlap gating BEFORE training, not after.
- Explicitly NOT in this round: lead-in / bystander-first / long-gap / re-entry row generators. Onset deletion is absent from the synthetic median (-0.25 to +0.30 dB foreground-projection excess on both WER sets, both checkpoints), so a loss change has nothing to push on there; that is a separate data round and this proposal must not be sold as an onset fix.

**Architecture changes**

- puresound/nnet/lobe/heads.py: new ProxHead (per-frame, F-only pool, affine-free LayerNorm over C, 1x1 -> causal depthwise k=9 -> 1x1 with 2 outputs, output layer zero-initialised) + ProxHeadConfig(StrictConfig) mirroring DistHeadConfig at heads.py:68-73. DistHead and DistHeadRegressionLoss are left byte-identical — the device path that already reads 0.989 must not be disturbed.
- puresound/nnet/dpcrn.py: constructor arg prox_head at :180, build at :240 (ProxHead.from_config(..., enc_channels=channels[-1])), call and stash last_prox_logits at :292-295 alongside last_dist_preds. Guarded by the config block so every existing recipe is unchanged.
- puresound/system/siso.py: (a) new provider 'prox_logits' in _loss_providers; (b) _random_channel_perturb gains a `group` argument and the nonlinear operator family; (c) training_step stashes the clean-view prox logits (same pattern as last_mask) and runs the perturbed-view consistency, with the mask-consistency L1 gated to linear views only; (d) new on_validation_epoch_end that scores the frozen 47-clip field list and logs auc_prox_qvf_clip / auc_prox_device_clip / auc_dist_device_clip.
- puresound/nnet/loss/prox.py (new): ProxContrastLoss with required_inputs = ('prox_logits','vad_target','background_vad_target','batch'), holding L_rank + L_frame + L_var. Registered in puresound/nnet/loss/__init__.py __all__ so recipes can name it.
- puresound/system/field_readout.py (new, small): loads the frozen clip list + keep/suppress spans from benchmarks/field_test_vector windows.json and computes the AUCs, importing the AUC code path from benchmarks/probes/anchor_gate_sim.py rather than duplicating it. Path-guarded and optional — test_vec/ is private and must never become a required training path.
- NOT changed: the dead FiLM/dvec_dim self-conditioning path, the presence/VAD heads, the mask type, the inter path, and every suppression loss. This round adds one training-only head and one regulariser.

**Expected effects**

- The chain shift is not additive/scale in feature space but rotational — centering and difference-only supervision then buy nothing and QVF AUC stays <= 0.40. This is exactly what the 48 h probe tests before any GPU is spent.: QVF clip-scope proximity readability AUC rises from 0.255 (v16 ep19 DistHead) to >= 0.50, target >= 0.75; and the robust statistic — QVF groups with AUC >= 0.35 — goes from 0/4 to >= 3/4. — _A logistic probe on the SAME cached pooled bottleneck features, fitted on device recordings only, already reads QVF at 0.827 (v16) / 0.789 (v8), and QVF-fitted leave-one-group-out reads 0.986-0.999. The whole 0.57 AUC gap is in the head's parameters, and the proposed head removes the two structural reasons it can drift: the absolute-metres target and the whole-clip pool. 0.827 is the measured ceiling of a LINEAR read of v16's existing features across chains, so 0.50-0.83 is the honest band._
- The per-frame head pools far less than the whole-clip mean, so its variance on 1 s windows is higher and device clip AUC falls below 0.95; or v11b's +0.145 came from v11a's presence heads (the confound) and nothing moves at all.: Device clip-scope distance AUC stays >= 0.95 (v16 DistHead: 0.989) and device session DRR AUC rises from 0.757 toward >= 0.85. — _DistHeadRegressionLoss is retained at 0.15 as the calibration anchor that produced 0.989, and the within-row ranking objective is a strictly stronger in-domain constraint (device-fit in-sample probe = 1.000, LOGO 0.999-1.000). On the DRR side, v11b moved device session DRR AUC +0.145 (p<0.001) with input-side compression alone; L_ccprox is a more direct pressure on the same quantity._
- The near/far contrast gradient competes with separation the way the v11 family's auxiliary heads did (v11: synthetic far suppression down 3-5 dB), and the block median comes back >2 dB shallower — the pre-registered kill.: Anchored / session far suppression on the field block stays inside the v16 ep19 block band (block median -9.73 dB), i.e. NOT v11b's roughly halved depth (-12.35/-11.29/-9.25 -> -6.64/-5.95/-6.02). — _Unlike v11b this round changes no mixture distribution and no suppression term; the new gradient into the bottleneck enters through a zero-initialised head whose weight ramps 0 -> 0.3 over 2 epochs, plus a consistency term on a 4-row sub-batch at 30% of steps. v18 is the evidence that the suppression floors do load-bearing work, and they are untouched._
- The contrast loss shifts keep-side behaviour anyway and Dawn deletion rises past 0.227 — a guardrail kill, since a readout win bought with deletion is the v11b mistake repeated.: No first-order improvement in onset or wrong-anchor deletion. Dawn deletion and moderate-set WER deltas stay within noise of v16 ep19 (Dawn deletion <= 0.207 + 0.02). — _Onset deletion is a real-recording phenomenon absent from the synthetic training-distribution proxies in the median (-0.25 to +0.30 dB foreground-projection excess on 200+200 utterances, both checkpoints), so a loss-only change has no gradient to shape. The payoff for onset here is a PRECONDITION, not a fix: the AnchorGate arm that cut Dawn deletion 0.230 -> 0.096 (v8) and 0.207 -> 0.094 (v16) is the relative-DRR arm, and it currently cannot be trusted off-rig because QVF distance is inverted (0.265) and QVF DRR only 0.799._
- Nothing moves, because the deletion is decided by the mask path and the aux head's gradient never reaches the decision — which is the honest prior, hence not a gate.: Second-order, reported but not gated: the synthetic wrong-anchor onset metric Delta_on1 improves by >= 0.5 dB from v16 ep19's -1.25 dB (2 s different-talker anchor) or does not move. — _L_frame puts explicit per-frame near/far gradient on exactly the frames where the anchor_synthetic probe localised the damage (Delta_on1 -1.25 to -1.71 dB while Delta_late is -0.10 to -0.12), and that probe runs on 200 synthetic utterances in ~10 min per checkpoint with no ASR._
- The waveshaper changes the direct/reverberant energy ratio physically, not just the readout's calibration, in which case invariance to it is unlearnable and forcing it costs real distance sensitivity — visible as device AUC falling instead of holding.: The readout survives the |x|^p waveshaper in-silico: applying p = 0.4 to device clips leaves the new head's device AUC >= 0.95 where the DistHead falls 0.989 -> 0.880. — _That operator is in the nonlinear perturbation group and L_ccprox penalises exactly the quantity it moves (the DRR contrast walk -0.55 -> +5.00 dB). comp_readability2.py already measures this and can be re-run per checkpoint._

**Cost:** Measured from the v16 checkpoint timestamps (ep0 08:51 -> ep19 20:56 on 2 GPUs): 38.2 min/epoch = 1.27 GPU-hours/epoch = 1.06 GPU-days per 20-epoch cosine cycle. The extra nonlinear-view forward is 4 rows at 30% of steps on top of the second forward v16 already runs, so ~+3-5% wall => 1.1 GPU-days per arm. Plan: arm A (full objective) + arm B-ablation (L_ccprox off, L_rank/L_frame only, to attribute any win to invariance vs to the difference-only readout) = 2.2 GPU-days; optional 40-epoch continuation if the ep19 trough passes, +1.1. Evaluation: field+Dawn agcache for a 5-checkpoint block at ~2 GPU-hours per checkpoint = 0.4 GPU-days (the chain_readability agent built one full 499-record field cache this way). TOTAL ~3.7 GPU-days on the 2-GPU box, i.e. ~2 calendar days of training per arm. Engineering: 1.5 d ProxHead + ProxContrastLoss, 1.0 d validation hook + field_readout module, 0.5 d nonlinear perturbation group and the linear/nonlinear gating of the mask-consistency term, 1.0 d tests (head shape/causality/zero-init; loss invariance under s -> s + b to 1e-6; provider-table test update; nonlinear view does not enter mask consistency), 0.5 d the 48 h offline probe = 4.5 engineering days. Warm start: --pretrained_ckpt_path /work/any_exp_link/puresound_exp/dpcrn_v16_lengthmix/lightning_logs/version_0/checkpoints/epoch=19-step=10000.ckpt (runner.py:259 loads params only with strict=False, so the new ProxHead params are reported as "kept at init"), new config config/exp/train_dpcrn_v19a_chaininv.yaml, fresh CosineAnnealingWarmRestarts T_0=20 cycle, judged at its own trough (ep19). No ladder re-run: the eight-stage curriculum is load-bearing (last stage from scratch reaches -1.65 dB real far suppression vs v8's -9.83), which is precisely why the warm start is mandatory and v11b (pre-2026-08-18 distribution, hand-stopped mid-cosine, cross-chain probe 0.680 vs v16's 0.827) is rejected as the base.

**Risks**

- Head collapse: a constant s satisfies both consistency terms for free. Mitigated by L_var (relu(0.5 - std_t(s))) and by logging std_t(s0) every validation epoch; if std drifts below 0.5 the run is invalid, not merely worse.
- Auxiliary-gradient tax on separation. This is the v11-family failure mode and v11b's measured price (roughly half the anchored far suppression). Mitigated by zero-init output, a 2-epoch weight ramp, max_rows=4, and a pre-registered kill at >2 dB shallower block median — but the risk is real and is the main reason for the ablation arm.
- The inversion may not be an additive/scale offset. If it is a rotation in feature space, difference-only supervision and channel LayerNorm cannot remove it. This is the proposal's single load-bearing assumption and the 48 h probe is designed to falsify it before any GPU spend.
- Metric resolution. QVF clip distance AUC has a paired half-width of +-0.14 at n = 9 keep / 6 suppress recordings and the session scope is unusable (CI [0.06, 0.97], n = 3). A 0.255 -> 0.45 move would not be resolvable. Mitigation: the primary gate is the per-group count (currently 12/12 below 0.20 — a count is far more robust than the pooled AUC at this n) plus a 5-checkpoint block, and the pass bar is set at 0.50 which is above the resolution floor.
- Nonlinear-view consistency could teach the wrong invariance. The |x|^p waveshaper physically changes the direct-to-reverberant ratio, so demanding readout invariance to it may cost genuine distance sensitivity. Bounded by the device-AUC guard (>= 0.95) and by keeping p >= 0.4 (the operating point already measured, not an extrapolation).
- Row-eligibility bias. Requiring >= 10 far-only frames may select rows with more silence — the audit already flagged that the bg3/decay cells ran on n = 126 of 200 for this reason. Measured before training (CPU dataloader pass), and the eligible-row share is reported by row type and length bucket in the round's pre-registration.
- Label hygiene: the audit found ~1% of rows where turn-taking gated the near talker to exact zero while the metadata still says target_present = 1.0. The eligibility rule therefore uses waveform-derived frame masks (vad_target / background_vad_target), never the target_present scalar — the same choice siso.py:305 already makes for inactive_labels.
- Privacy/plumbing: the in-loop metric reads test_vec/ (private recordings). It must stay an optional, path-guarded block; a committed recipe that hard-requires that path would break every other machine and put private audio on a required code path.
- Scope creep in the write-up. The round improves a readout; it does not fix onset deletion (absent in the synthetic median) and it does not touch the QVF suppression wall itself. If the verdict is written as an onset or deployment win it will contradict the onset_profile and reference_matrix findings.

**Go / no-go**

- PRIMARY: QVF clip-scope proximity readability AUC (new ProxHead output, s0) — benchmarks/probes/anchor_gate_cache.py field (config/exp/train_dpcrn_v19a_chaininv.yaml, one cache per checkpoint) -> anchor_gate_sim.py readability + the scratchpad per_group_auc.py, on a 5-checkpoint block (ep15-19), median over checkpoints — pass: >= 0.50 median over the block; >= 0.75 = on target (the level at which the device streaming readout already sits and the AnchorGate demonstrably works) — kill: < 0.40 (statistically indistinguishable from the current 0.255 given the +-0.14 half-width, i.e. no evidence of a fix)
- PRIMARY (robust form): number of QVF recording groups with clip AUC >= 0.35 — per_group_auc.py over the condition=none records of the v19a caches, 4 QVF recording groups (gym / plumbing / price / scenario3) — pass: >= 3 of 4 groups at >= 0.35 — kill: <= 1 of 4 groups at >= 0.35 (currently 0/4; 12/12 cells across three checkpoints are below 0.20)
- GUARD: device clip-scope distance AUC (DistHead, retained) and device clip-scope proximity AUC (ProxHead) — Same caches, device-chain records; anchor_gate_sim.py readability — pass: >= 0.95 for both (v16 DistHead baseline 0.989) — the task's explicit constraint that the device chain stays at 0.99-class readability — kill: < 0.95
- GUARD: device session-scope DRR readability AUC — Same caches, device session records; anchor_gate_sim.py readability — pass: >= 0.85 (v16 0.757; v11b reached 0.903 with input-side compression alone) — kill: < 0.75 (a regression below v16's own level)
- GUARD: anchored / session far-field suppression, block median (absolute residual and headroom, not attenuation alone) — The set-v3 field block protocol (FIELD_BLOCK.md): 5-checkpoint block, context pads, paired Wilcoxon vs the v16 ep19 block — pass: within the v16 ep19 block band (block median around -9.73 dB), no significant loss at p < 0.05 — kill: block median shallower than the v16 ep19 band by > 2.0 dB (v11b's ~50% loss would be an unambiguous kill)
- GUARD: Dawn Chorus ASR deletion rate — Azure STT on the Dawn Chorus set, deltas versus the unprocessed mixture and versus v16 ep19 (benchmarks/wer_sets/AZURE_2026-09.md protocol; compare deltas because the 10 s cap inflates absolute deletion) — pass: <= v16 ep19's 0.207 + 0.02 — kill: > v16 ep19 + 0.02
- GUARD: moderate-reverb WER delta (primary WER gate) — eval_wer.py on data_report/wer_set_moderate_test (200 utts, CI printed; BUT-OFFICE is explicitly NOT used — n=200 CI +-0.03 there gives it no resolution) — pass: not worse than v16 ep19 outside the CI — kill: worse than v16 ep19 beyond the printed CI
- GUARD: keep violations on field set v3 — The set-v3 keep-violation count in the field scorecard, cross-checked on any new violation with the harmonic peak-to-valley test (span energy conflates speech / noise / reverb, and user listening has already caught a 'violation' that was noise) — pass: <= v16 ep19's 20/148 — kill: > 20/148
- MONITOR (not a gate): in-loop auc_prox_qvf_clip / auc_prox_device_clip and the readout's frame-wise standard deviation — The new on_validation_epoch_end hook (47 frozen field cold-start clips), plus std_over_frames(s0) logged each epoch — pass: used only to spot divergence early; a single-checkpoint reading is never a verdict (+-0.14 half-width, n=3 sessions) — kill: std_t(s0) < 0.5 at any trough = collapsed head, run invalid regardless of AUC
- REPORTED (not gated): synthetic wrong-anchor onset Delta_on1 at a 2 s different-talker anchor — benchmarks/probes/anchor_synthetic_probe.py (utt arm, 200 utts, ~10 min/checkpoint, no ASR) — pass: improvement of >= 0.5 dB on v16 ep19's -1.25 dB would be a bonus — kill: no kill — reported, not gated

**Implementation**

- 1. egs/voice_isolate/benchmarks/probes/readout_surgery.py (NEW) — the 48 h killer probe, CPU only, on the existing /tmp/.../scratchpad/agcache/{v8,v16,v11b}/field/*.npz caches. Must run and pass BEFORE anything below.
- 2. puresound/nnet/lobe/heads.py — add ProxHeadConfig (mirroring DistHeadConfig at :68-73) and class ProxHead (F-only pool, affine-free LayerNorm over C, Conv1x1 -> causal depthwise k=9 -> Conv1x1 with 2 outputs, zero-init output). Do not touch DistHead.
- 3. puresound/nnet/dpcrn.py — prox_head arg at :180, ProxHead.from_config(..., enc_channels=channels[-1]) at :240, self.last_prox_logits = self.prox_head(x) at :292-295 beside last_dist_preds.
- 4. puresound/nnet/loss/prox.py (NEW) — ProxContrastLoss(required_inputs=('prox_logits','vad_target','background_vad_target','batch')) implementing L_rank + L_frame + L_var with the eligibility rule; NaN-masked DRR arm on s1 reusing DistHeadRegressionLoss's masking pattern.
- 5. puresound/nnet/loss/__init__.py — import and add 'ProxContrastLoss' to __all__ so recipes can name it via loss_func[].type.
- 6. puresound/system/siso.py — (a) add 'prox_logits' to _loss_providers (:349-357); (b) give _random_channel_perturb (:262-286) a `group` argument plus the nonlinear operator family (waveshaper / correctly-applied compressor gain / expander / broadcast EQ); (c) in training_step (:381-403) stash the clean-view prox logits the way last_mask is stashed, run one linear-view and one nonlinear-view perturbed forward on the rank-synced schedule, apply the mask-consistency L1 to the LINEAR view only and L_ccprox to both, and log both terms.
- 7. puresound/system/field_readout.py (NEW, small) — frozen 47-clip list + spans from benchmarks/field_test_vector windows.json; AUC computation imported from benchmarks/probes/anchor_gate_sim.py rather than duplicated; path-guarded so a machine without test_vec/ trains normally.
- 8. puresound/system/siso.py — on_validation_epoch_end logging auc_prox_qvf_clip / auc_prox_device_clip / auc_dist_device_clip / std_t(s0) (today validation_step at :426 logs losses only).
- 9. egs/voice_isolate/config/exp/train_dpcrn_v19a_chaininv.yaml (NEW) — copy of train_dpcrn_v16_lengthmix.yaml with backbone_args.prox_head enabled, the ProxContrastLoss entry (weighted 0.3, ramp 2 epochs), DistHeadRegressionLoss 0.3 -> 0.15, channel_consistency extended with the nonlinear group and mask_on_nonlinear: False, augmentation_compressor left OFF, and a header stating the ONE round knob and the pre-registered gates.
- 10. egs/voice_isolate/config/exp/train_dpcrn_v19a_ablate_nocc.yaml (NEW) — identical with L_ccprox weight 0, for attribution.
- 11. test/ — new tests: ProxHead output shape and strict causality (a future-only input change must not move s_t), zero-init means s == 0 at load, ProxContrastLoss invariance under prox_logits + b for random b (equal to < 1e-6), eligibility rule on a synthetic batch with no far-only frames (must contribute a graph-carrying zero, never a NaN, and never crash DDP — the standing lesson from the background-VAD None-target crash), nonlinear perturbation views never enter the mask-consistency term, provider-table test updated for 'prox_logits'.
- 12. Pre-flight, before launching: one CPU dataloader pass (the audit's sample_rows.py) reporting the eligible-row share by row type and length bucket, and a single-GPU 20-step smoke run to confirm the extra forward's memory headroom on the 22 GB cards (v16's measured peaks: 3s x20 / 6s x12 19.2 / 12s x6 19.0 / 30s x2 16.9 GiB — the 6 s and 12 s buckets have almost no headroom, so max_rows must stay at 4).
- 13. Launch: cd egs/voice_isolate && uv run python main.py --config config/exp/train_dpcrn_v19a_chaininv.yaml --pretrained_ckpt_path /work/any_exp_link/puresound_exp/dpcrn_v16_lengthmix/lightning_logs/version_0/checkpoints/epoch=19-step=10000.ckpt (params only, strict=False — runner.py:259).

**48 h kill experiment:** READOUT SURGERY ON FROZEN FEATURES — CPU only, hours, on data that already exists; it kills the proposal if the premise is wrong.

Script: egs/voice_isolate/benchmarks/probes/readout_surgery.py, reading /tmp/claude-1001/.../scratchpad/agcache/{v16,v8,v11b}/field/*.npz. Each record already contains `feat` [C, T] (the bottleneck pooled over frequency, 100 fps) plus the keep/suppress spans and the recording group — exactly the vector the proposed ProxHead consumes, so the probe tests the proposed head's construction and not a proxy for it.

Build near-frame / far-frame samples from the condition=none records using the set-v3 spans (the same masks per_group_auc.py already uses). Then fit five readouts on DEVICE recordings only and evaluate leave-chain-out on QVF, plus device leave-one-group-out:
  R0 logistic on the raw pooled 1 s window                      [measured baseline: 0.827 v16, 0.789 v8]
  R1 R0 on per-clip mean-centered features (x - mean_t(x))
  R2 R0 on per-clip standardized features (centered, unit variance per channel)
  R3 R0 on affine-free LayerNorm-over-C features                 [the exact module change]
  R4 a pairwise-ranking (RankNet) linear readout fitted on within-clip near/far frame pairs and scored against each clip's own midpoint  [the exact L_rank + L_frame objective]
Report AUC for every cell, and re-score the DistHead itself as the control (must reproduce 0.255 / 0.265 / 0.258).

Second half, using comp_readability2.py (which correctly multiplies the gain curve in): apply the |x|^p waveshaper at p = 0.8 / 0.6 / 0.4 to the DEVICE clips, recompute `feat`, and score R0-R4 on those. The DistHead falls 0.989 -> 0.880 at p 0.4; a readout that is invariant by construction must hold >= 0.95.

KILL RULES (pre-registered, either one kills the round before any GPU is spent):
  (a) If the best of R1-R4 neither beats R0's cross-chain 0.827 nor reaches >= 0.90 cross-chain on QVF, then the chain shift is not an additive/scale offset in feature space and no amount of centering or difference-only supervision can fix it — the proposal's single load-bearing assumption is false. Do not train; the lever is elsewhere (room/mic geometry, codec, or the real end-to-end axis).
  (b) If R1-R4 do not hold >= 0.95 device AUC under the p = 0.4 waveshaper, then the operator that provably moves the readout moves the underlying physics too, invariance to it is unlearnable, and the nonlinear consistency term would trade real distance sensitivity for a metric.
PASS: at least one of R1-R4 reaches >= 0.90 cross-chain on QVF AND >= 0.95 device under p = 0.4 — the winning variant then fixes the head's normalisation choice (centering vs standardisation vs LayerNorm) and the reading protocol, so the GPU round starts with a pre-tested module rather than a guess. Also record the ranking of the three checkpoints' features under the winning readout, since it re-checks the v16-over-v11b warm-start choice (v16 0.827 vs v11b 0.680 on R0) on the actual objective.


## v19-RETURN — re-entry rows, bystander-first rows, and an onset-weighted anti-deletion term (warm start v16 ep19)

_Angle:_ DATA + LOSS ONLY, no architecture change: manufacture the three row shapes whose absence the audit measured (long-gap re-entry, bystander-first onset, anchor-free lead-in), then put loss mass exactly on the frames where deletion is measured (the first second after each foreground onset), and restore the intelligibility term's supervision at the row opening.

The model deletes the first second of a talker who arrives without an anchor, or after somebody else's anchor. v19-RETURN says: stop training on "two people already talking from frame 0" and start training on "the decision has to be re-made". Three data changes manufacture the failure in-distribution — (D1) a long quiet gap inside long rows followed by the SAME near talker re-entering, with the gap filled by the row's own room tone rather than digital silence; (D2) enough turn-taking that a bystander holds the floor for seconds before the near talker's first word; (D3) a short anchor-free or bystander-only lead-in with the capture floor forced on, so a lead is never digital silence. One loss change puts weight where the damage is: OverSuppressionLoss becomes onset-weighted, w = 1 + β·m_onset with the denominator left as the plain element count, so every non-onset bin keeps exactly the gradient it had in v16 and the first second after every onset gets (1+β)× it; and the two ASRFeatureLoss crops, which today supervise t=0 with probability 0.000 on 12 s and 30 s rows, are anchored at the row start part of the time. Nothing about the suppression floors is relaxed (v18's verdict stands), no target-absent rate is raised (that explosion is on record), and no module gains a parameter. Warm start from v16 ep19 for one 20-epoch cosine cycle; the round succeeds only if the shipped OnsetGuard becomes unnecessary — measured with the guard OFF.

**Objective**

## The one new loss

`OnsetWeightedOverSuppressionLoss` (new class in `puresound/nnet/loss/stft_loss.py`, replaces the `OverSuppressionLoss` entry in the recipe at the same `weighted: 3.0`).

Let T = |STFT(target)|, E = |STFT(enhanced)| on the existing grid (fft 512, hop 128, win 512 -> 125 fps), p = 0.5, r[b,f,t] = ReLU(T^p - E^p)^2, and m_onset[b,t] in {0,1} the onset mask resampled from the VAD grid (hop 160, 100 fps) by nearest-neighbour index mapping idx(t_stft) = round(t_stft * 128 / 160).

    w[b,t] = 1 + beta * m_onset[b,t]
    L_ons  = sum_{b,f,t} w[b,t] * r[b,f,t]  /  N          where N = numel(r)

The denominator is the plain element count, i.e. exactly what `torch.mean` uses today. That is the load-bearing design choice: normalising by sum(w) would buy onset pressure with steady-state pressure, and the diagnosis says steady state is the half that is already fine (d_late ~= -0.10 dB in every synthetic condition, +0.30 dB on indomain). So non-onset bins keep v16's gradient bit-for-bit and onset bins get (1+beta)x. With beta = 7 and the measured onset frame fraction (~0.11 of all frames after D1-D3 raise it from the current 0.10), the term's total value rises ~1.8x -- the one deliberate loss-balance change of the round, and the intended direction, since this is an anti-deletion round. beta in {3, 7} is the only tuned scalar; picked in the 48 h probe, never on field.

beta = 0 must be bit-identical to `OverSuppressionLoss` (unit test).

## The onset mask (data-side, emitted per row)

`sample["onset_weight"]`, a [T_vad] float mask built in `ns.py` right after `vad_reference = target_speech.clone()` (line 583, i.e. post speed-perturb / post reverb so indices line up with `vad_target`):

    a[t] = foreground activity, from EITHER
           (i) gating.near_frames -- the exact near envelope of the turn script,
               ground truth, available whenever turn-taking/re-entry fired; OR
           (ii) a STRICT energy detector on vad_reference: frame 400 / hop 160,
                > -25 dB re the row's own peak frame.
    for each maximal run [s, e) of a == 1:
        preceded_by_gap = (s >= G) and (a[s-G : s].sum() == 0)      # G = 1.0 s
        is_row_head     = (s <= H)                                  # H = 0.2 s
        if preceded_by_gap or is_row_head:
            m_onset[s : min(s + L, e)] = 1                          # L = 1.0 s

Why the strict -25 dB detector and not the pipeline's `vad_target`: the audit measured real-near KEEP targets whose 5th-percentile frame sits only -38.9 dB below their peak (they are the recording, room tone included), so the shipped -40 dB labeler reads them 0.98 active and would find no onsets at all on exactly the rows that carry real near speech. The audit's own conclusion was that -25 dB is the safer read on those rows.

L = 1.0 s and G = 1.0 s are not free parameters: the field anchor curve knees at 1 s and saturates at 2 s (0.25 s -0.48, 0.5 s -3.67, 1 s -12.61, 2 s -13.85 dB, v8, lone-interferer arm), and the deletion is confined to the first second (Delta_on1 -1.71 dB vs Delta_late -0.10 dB).

## D1 -- long-gap re-entry rows

New guarded branch in `OverlapGating.apply`, placed BEFORE the turn-taking branch:

    if (allow_turn_taking and cfg.reentry_prob > 0.0
            and row_seconds >= cfg.reentry_min_row_seconds        # 9.0
            and torch.rand(1).item() < cfg.reentry_prob):         # 0.7
        return self._turn_taking(..., force_quiet_gap=True)

`_turn_script` gains `force_quiet_gap`: after the FIRST turn it inserts gap_f frames in which NEITHER near_mask NOR far_mask is set, gap_s ~ U(3.0, min(10.0, 0.45 * row_seconds)), and the turn after the gap is a NEAR turn (so `far_first` alone decides whether a bystander held the floor before the gap). Because `NoiseStage` runs after gating in `ns.py`, the gap is filled with the row's own recorded noise / room coloring / capture floor -- never digital silence. That is deliberate and measured: digital silence is a -5.9 dB (v8) / -19.2 dB (v16) suppress bias on the lone-interferer arm and a -2.33 dB onset deletion on v16's keep side, so it is a stressor and a probe, never a neutral lead.

Target during the gap: turn-taking gates the target and its contribution to the mixture with the SAME envelope, so the target is ~0 through the gap while the mixture carries the floor. The suppression terms therefore demand "output the floor as zero" during the gap and the keep terms demand "hand back the talker" one frame later -- that IS the decision we want re-made, and it needs no target-absent row and no floor relaxation to express.

Realised row share (rows, not batches -- the audit showed batch probabilities understate short buckets ~2x because n_spk travels with the bucket): P(row >= 9 s) = 0.389 under the new schedule x P(row has interferers) ~= 0.78 x 0.7 = **0.212 of rows**, against 0.003 today (4 of 1206) and 0.000 on the 3 s bucket.

## D2 -- bystander-first (wrong-anchor) rows

`overlap_control.turn_taking_prob: 0.0 -> 0.35`. Today turn-taking reaches only 11.7% of rows via the realfar 0.3 / realnear 0.5 per-row overrides, and it is the sole generator of the far-first shape, which covers 6.0% of rows at >= 1 s. `far_first_prob` stays 0.5. Combined with D1 and D3 the far-speech->near-onset shape reaches ~0.30 of rows (0.106 from re-entry rows with far_first, ~0.12 from plain turn-taking rows, ~0.075 from bystander leads), i.e. ~5x today's rate. This is the higher-value target: on Dawn the onset after >= 1 s of real background is -7.65 dB (v8) / -6.07 (v16) against -4.22 / -4.61 for the true cold onset, and it is the only condition in which field onset deletion is visible at all (near_speech dt -3.86 dB).

## D3 -- anchor-free / bystander-only lead-in, with the floor forced

Reuse the v17 `augmentation_row_initial_ambient` block (`ns.py:540-569`, currently off) with three changes that separate it from v17's negative verdict:
* `lead_seconds_range: [0.5, 3.0]` (v17 used [1.0, 4.0]) -- the anchor knee is at 1 s and saturation at 2 s, so the decision boundary must fall INSIDE the lead range;
* `bystander_lead_prob: 0.5` -- on that share of lead rows only the TARGET is masked over the lead, interferers keep running, so the lead is a bystander-first prefix rather than room tone;
* `require_capture_floor: True` -- `NoiseStage.apply(..., force_floor=True)` on any row that got a lead. Without it, P(no recorded noise AND no capture floor) = 0.2 * 0.5 = 0.10 of lead rows would open on digital silence, i.e. on the one prefix that is a large suppress bias. Draw first, then `if force_floor or draw < prob`, so the RNG stream is unchanged.
* and, unlike v17, these rows now carry loss mass at the onset (m_onset fires on the row's first active run after the lead). v17's own verdict is that the lead ALONE is negative; this proposal never runs it alone.

`prob: 0.15` of all rows.

## Label-correctness fix (prerequisite, not an experiment)

The audit measured 1.0% of rows -- all on the 3 s bucket -- where the far-first turn gates the near talker to EXACTLY zero while the metadata still says `target_present = 1.0`: SDR / MR-STFT then treat the row as lone-far (they derive the mask from the waveform) while `ResidualReferenceLoss(target_present_only=True)` still includes it. D1/D2 raise the turn-taking rate, so this is fixed first, in `ns.py` immediately after the gating block:

    if not target_absent and target_speech.abs().amax() == 0:
        target_absent = True      # the row IS lone-far; label it so

No RNG draw, so a seeded stream cannot move.

## Loss-side change #2 -- give the intelligibility term the row opening

`asr_feature.py:85-91`, one new argument `onset_crop_prob: float = 0.5`: with that probability `start = 0` instead of `randint`. Today the two ASRFeatureLoss terms carry 2.0 of the 7.0 total loss weight and supervise t=0 with probability 0.000 on a 12 s row and 0.000 on a 30 s row (t=1 s: 0.167 / 0.042, against 0.250 mid-row). The round adds anti-deletion pressure exactly where the only intelligibility-aware term is blind; that has to be fixed in the same arm or the round is testing a half-instrumented objective.

## Explicitly NOT in this proposal

* No target-absent rate change. `augmentation_target_absent.used` stays False and `lone_far_prob` stays 0.15. Silence rows as the persistence teacher are on record as a Dawn WER explosion (0.392 -> 0.626).
* No loss-floor relaxation. `stft(relative_floor)` stays off, `SDRLoss.inactive_mode` stays `absolute`, VAD `eps_mode` stays `absolute`. v18 measured that relaxing them makes every suppression number shallower (cold-far +0.53 dB, p=0.017).
* No change to the real-near target definition. The audit is right that a real-near target carrying its own -38.9 dB floor conflicts with the lone-far rows' "kill to the floor", and that a long silence on such a row teaches the model to reproduce that recording's room tone. Inside a re-entry gap the conflict is resolved by construction (the near envelope gates the real target to zero too). Outside it, the conflict stays, and I am leaving it: v8's real far suppression is load-bearing and comes from these rows, so re-defining their target is a second axis, not this one. Named as a risk, with the knob (`realnear_gate_inactive_target`) added OFF so the decision is on the record rather than discovered.
* No architecture change of any kind.

**Data changes**

- D1 re-entry rows: new guarded branch in `OverlapGating.apply` + `force_quiet_gap` in `_turn_script`. One quiet gap of U(3.0, min(10.0, 0.45*row_seconds)) s after the first turn in which NEITHER near nor far mask is set; the turn after the gap is a near turn. New config fields on `OverlapControlConfig`: `reentry_prob=0.7`, `reentry_min_row_seconds=9.0`, `reentry_gap_seconds=(3.0,10.0)`, `reentry_gap_max_row_fraction=0.45`. Gap content = the row's own noise / room coloring / capture floor, because `NoiseStage` runs after gating -- never digital silence. Realised: 0.212 of rows, vs 0.003 today.
- D2 bystander-first rows: `augmentation_speech.overlap_control.turn_taking_prob: 0.0 -> 0.35` so turn-taking reaches synthetic rows and not only the 11.7% of rows carrying the realfar 0.3 / realnear 0.5 overrides. `far_first_prob` unchanged at 0.5. Raises the 'far speech >= 1 s before the near onset' shape from 0.060 to ~0.30 of rows.
- D3 lead-in rows: enable `augmentation_row_initial_ambient` with `prob=0.15`, `lead_seconds_range=[0.5,3.0]`, `fade_ms=50`, and two new fields: `bystander_lead_prob=0.5` (mask only the target over the lead, leave the interferers running) and `require_capture_floor=True` (forces `NoiseStage._capture_floor` on lead rows, closing the 10% of lead rows that would otherwise open on digital silence).
- Row-length reweighting stated in ROWS, not batches: `trainer.length_schedule` probs 0.25/0.30/0.25/0.20 -> 0.10/0.20/0.35/0.35 for the 3/6/12/30 s buckets (n_spk 20/12/6/2 unchanged). Realised row shares move from 49.8/37.8/9.9/2.5% to 27.8/33.3/29.2/9.7%, so 38.9% of rows are >= 12 s and can hold a >= 3 s gap. Audio-seconds per batch is IDENTICAL (66.6 s in both schedules: 0.10*60+0.20*72+0.35*72+0.35*60 = 0.25*60+0.30*72+0.25*72+0.20*60), so epoch wall time is unchanged by construction.
- New per-frame label `onset_weight` [T_vad] emitted by `ns.py::_build_onset_weight` and pad-stacked by `VoiceIsolationCollateFunc`; plus a scalar `onset_frame_fraction` added to `VOICE_ISOLATION_SCALAR_KEYS` so the realised onset mass is LOGGED per batch instead of assumed. `GatingResult` gains `near_frames` so the mask can use the turn script's exact envelope instead of re-detecting activity on a target whose own noise floor makes the -40 dB labeler read 0.98 active.
- Label-correctness fix in `ns.py`: a row whose gated target is exactly zero is relabelled `target_absent=True` / `target_present=0.0`. Fixes the audit's measured 1.0% of rows that SDR/MR-STFT scored as lone-far while ResidualReferenceLoss(target_present_only) still counted them as present. No RNG draw.
- RNG contract, tested: every new draw sits inside `if knob > 0.0`, so `reentry_prob=0.0` + `turn_taking_prob=0.0` + `row_initial_ambient.used=False` regenerates v16's seeded rows bit-for-bit; `require_capture_floor` draws first and then ORs, so forcing the floor does not shift the stream.

**Architecture changes**

- None. No module, head, parameter, buffer or forward-path change. The backbone, DistHead, VAD heads and the streaming export are untouched, so the checkpoint loads into `config/infer_dpcrn.yaml` and `streaming_onnx.py` unchanged and `--pretrained_ckpt_path` reports zero missing / zero unexpected keys (a preflight assertion, `scripts/preflight_ckpt_recipe.py`).
- The only new code object is a loss class, `OnsetWeightedOverSuppressionLoss`, which lives entirely in `puresound/nnet/loss/stft_loss.py` and reads `batch['onset_weight']` through the existing provider table (`siso.py::_loss_providers` already offers `batch`; no provider is added).
- One existing loss gains one argument: `ASRFeatureLoss(onset_crop_prob=0.5)`. No parameters, no shape change.

**Expected effects**

- Cold onset deletion on real recordings: the model attenuates the first ~1 s of the very first talker, before any anchor exists.: Dawn Chorus foreground-projection onset excess @0.5 s, condition `none`, improves from -3.90 dB (v16 ep19) to -1.5..-2.0 dB in the median, and the p5 tail from -75.6 dB to better than -40 dB. The cold-onset subset (Dawn onset < 0.1 s, n=146) improves from -4.61 to about -2 dB. — _The failure is currently NOT in the training distribution -- the same measure reads -0.41 dB (moderate) and +0.21 dB (indomain) on the two synthetic proxies, and 90.7% of rows start with the target talking within 0.5 s -- so there is nothing for the loss to push on. D3 puts an anchor-free lead in 15% of rows and D1 puts an interior anchor-free re-entry in 21%; the onset weight then puts (1+beta)x the anti-deletion gradient on exactly the frames the metric reads. The diagnosis's own implication list names this construction ('a lead-in knob that prepends 0.5-3 s of ... the loss weighted normally over the first second') as the round-1 candidate._
- Wrong-anchor deletion: a near talker who follows a DIFFERENT talker's 2-3 s anchor is deleted over their first second.: Synthetic Delta_on1 (anchor_synthetic probe, utt arm, other2/other3 anchors, n=200) moves from -1.25 / -1.33 dB (v16) to |Delta_on1| <= 0.5 dB, with the same-talker null preserved (self3 within +-0.15 dB of 0). The sign contrast self(null) < samespk < diffspk should survive; the samespk channel component (-0.40 dB) is expected to shrink less than the diffspk component, because nothing in this round is about channel identity. — _Unlike the cold case, this failure IS in-distribution and measurable at p ~ 3e-25 on 200 utterances -- so there is gradient. What is wrong today is where the gradient goes: onset frames are 14.1% of active frames and 22.5% of active energy, and every v16 term pools uniformly over time (SDR whole-row, MR-STFT/OverSuppression torch.mean, DistHead global pool). D2 multiplies the row shape ~5x and the onset weight moves the anti-deletion term's mass onto the frames where Delta_on1 is measured, while Delta_late (~-0.10 dB) stays supervised at exactly its v16 strength._
- The anchor is forgotten after ~5 s of quiet (field: half gone at 5 s, dead at 10 s; synthetic: half-life 4-5 s, ~90% gone at 20 s), so a talker returning after a pause is treated as new and attenuated.: The corrected decay curve should FLATTEN on the keep side, i.e. the returning near talker's first second is no longer attenuated, WITHOUT the suppression decay necessarily flattening. Concretely: on the anchor_synthetic bgonly arm the gap-corrected decay (-7.93 / -4.57 / -2.01 / -0.70 dB at 2/5/10/20 s, v16) is allowed to stay as it is -- I am not trying to lengthen the memory -- while the utt arm's post-gap Delta_on1 goes to ~0. — _reference_matrix section 6.2 warns, and the synthetic probe confirms with p=0.24 (v8) / 0.93 (v16) at 20 s, that persistence and wrong-anchor deletion share one clock: lengthening the state's memory lengthens the window in which the next talker is deleted. So this round deliberately does NOT extend memory. It trains the model to RE-MAKE the decision cheaply after a gap, which is what a 21%-of-rows re-entry shape supervises and what 0.3% of rows (4 of 1206, none on the 3 s bucket) cannot._
- ASR deletion on real recordings, which is the deployment-visible form of the two onset failures.: Dawn Chorus deletion, guard OFF, from 0.207 (v16 ep19, `none`) toward the guarded arm's 0.111 -- I claim <= 0.15 as the honest expectation and <= 0.12 as the target. WER from 0.297 toward 0.19-0.22. The bystander penalty (+0.028 on v16) should shrink, since the wrong-anchor clause is what produced it. — _The AnchorGate measurement prices the prize: the pna clause alone removes half the field keep violations (v16 20/148 -> 7) and with the relative-DRR arm cuts Dawn deletion 0.207 -> 0.094 and WER 0.297 -> 0.188, every arm p < 1e-26 paired. That is ~0.11-0.13 absolute deletion attributable to onset + wrong-anchor deletion. A trained fix should recover most of it without the guard's -22.3 -> -10.0 dB suppression price, because the guard is a blunt 'do not attenuate at all' rule while the loss can learn 'attenuate the bystander, keep the new arrival'._
- Suppression depth is the thing this round can break (the guard's price, paid in training instead of inference).: Sessions suppression block median should stay inside the v16 ep19 block band (-9.73 +-1.41 dB); I expect -9 to -10.5 dB, i.e. a lateral move, and I expect the ambient cold-far column (-12.65 +-2.50) to hold within 2 dB. If it lands shallower than -8.3 dB the anti-deletion pressure has eaten suppression and the arm is not deployable whatever the onset numbers say. — _Two independent priors point the same way. (a) v11b bought +0.145 device-session DRR AUC (p<0.001) with roughly half the anchored far suppression (-12.35/-11.29/-9.25 -> -6.64/-5.95/-6.02) -- a readout win reported without its cost. (b) v18 showed that weakening the 'kill to the floor' incentive makes every suppression number shallower. This round leaves all suppress-side terms and floors untouched and only ADDS keep-side mass, which is the least-bad version of the trade, but the trade is real: OverSuppression's total value rises ~1.8x._
- The QVF cross-chain wall (distance readout inverted, AUC 0.255-0.265).: NOTHING. I expect no movement on the QVF distance AUC and no cross-chain transfer. QVF keep clips may improve slightly to the extent their damage is onset damage, and QVF suppress clips should not get better. — _chain_readability measured that the features already carry the cue (device-fitted linear probe reads QVF at 0.789) and that the DistHead's learned mapping is what inverts, that v11b's compression axis did not move it (paired delta -0.006, p=0.893), and that the compressor stage the previous round guessed at is inert on this readout (|dAUC| <= 0.013). It is a readout-objective problem, not a row-shape problem. Claiming otherwise would be the round's easiest way to look like a win and then fail the block scorecard._

**Cost:** GPU: one 20-epoch cosine cycle warm-started from v16 ep19 on the same 2-GPU setup. v16's own checkpoint timestamps give 38 min/epoch (epoch=0 at 08:51, epoch=19 at 20:56 on 2026-08-30 = 12 h 05 min for 20 epochs), and the new length_schedule is cost-neutral by construction -- audio-seconds per batch is 66.6 s in both schedules -- so budget 12.5-14 h wall = **1.05-1.2 GPU-days** for the main run. The 48 h killer experiment is a 6-epoch version of the same run: 3.8 h wall = **0.32 GPU-days**. Full nine-gate evaluation on a 5-checkpoint block (ep15-19): field block + coldstart_v2 + s2f keep sweep + Dawn (with ASR) + two WER sets, ~5 checkpoint-passes of `run_full_benchmark.sh` -> budget **0.5 GPU-days** plus Azure STT cost on 4 WER sets. Fresh agcache builds for the onset/anchor probes: 499 field records + 450 Dawn utterances per checkpoint, ~25 min each on one GPU -> 0.05 GPU-days. **Total ~2.1 GPU-days.**

Engineering: 3.5 days. Day 1 -- config fields + `_turn_script` re-entry branch + `GatingResult.near_frames` + RNG bit-exactness tests. Day 2 -- `_build_onset_weight`, collate, `onset_frame_fraction`, the target_present relabel, `force_floor`, the D3 lead extensions, and the temporal columns in `scripts/check_training_data.py`. Day 3 -- the loss class + its beta=0 identity test + `ASRFeatureLoss.onset_crop_prob` + recipe config + `preflight_ckpt_recipe.py`. Half a day -- the 48 h probe's report script (reuse `onset_profile/onset_real.py` and `anchor_synthetic/anchor_synthetic_probe.py` verbatim; both already exist and need only a `--tag` for the new checkpoint).

Warm-start plan, exactly: `uv run python main.py --config config/exp/train_dpcrn_v19_return.yaml --pretrained_ckpt_path /work/any_exp_link/puresound_exp/dpcrn_v16_lengthmix/lightning_logs/version_0/checkpoints/epoch=19-step=10000.ckpt` -- WEIGHTS ONLY (runner.py:259-265, strict=False), not `--ckpt_path`, so the cosine restarts and ep19 of the new run is a trough comparable to v16 ep19. `max_epochs: 20`, `T_0: 20`, `warmup_step: 250`, `eta_min: 1e-5`, `work_folder: exp/dpcrn_v19_return`. Never train the last stage from scratch: the ladder is load-bearing (last-stage-only from zero reaches -1.65 dB real far suppression against v8's -9.83), and the architecture is unchanged so the eight-stage ladder does not need re-running. Because no parameter is added, the preflight must report 0 missing / 0 unexpected keys; if it does not, stop -- something in the config touched the model.

**Risks**

- The trade this round can lose: keep-side mass rises ~1.8x on the OverSuppression term while every suppress-side term stays fixed, so the model can satisfy the new objective by becoming more passthrough everywhere. That is the v14 shape of failure -- a free `dry_blend 0.85` knob beat the whole round -- and the honest guard against it is gate 4 (sessions suppression band) plus a pre-registered comparison against v16 ep19 + OnsetGuard, not against bare v16.
- Persistence and wrong-anchor deletion share one clock (reference_matrix 6.2, confirmed synthetically: the wrong-anchor deletion closes at 20 s with p=0.24/0.93, the same constant as the memory decay). If the model's way of passing the onset gate is to lengthen its memory, gate 2 improves while the memory-decay curve flattens and the wrong-anchor window gets LONGER on longer contexts than the probe measures. Mitigation: report the gap-corrected decay curve (bgonly arm, 2/5/10/20 s) beside Delta_on1 in every readout, and treat a flattening suppression decay as a red flag, not a win.
- Real-near KEEP targets carry their own room tone (p5 frame level -38.9 dB re peak vs -56.5 dB synthetic). Outside a re-entry gap that conflicts with the lone-far rows' 'kill to the floor', and the onset detector on those rows depends on the -25 dB threshold being above their floor. If it is not, the mask fires on room tone and the round adds anti-deletion pressure to noise. Detectable in the 48 h part (a): report `onset_frame_fraction` split by row type and require real-near rows to be within 2x the synthetic rows' value.
- Splice realism: a re-entry gap on a real-near row means a genuine recording is envelope-gated to zero for 3-10 s and back. The 400-sample (25 ms) Hann fade removes the click, but the room tail is cut mid-decay and the row now contains a discontinuity real recordings do not. The synthetic anchor probe's `selflate` null (-0.05 dB, n.s.) argues splicing alone is not a cue the model keys on, but that was 20 ms frame splicing, not a 5 s hole.
- Raising turn_taking_prob to 0.35 with far_first_prob 0.5 reduces near-speech minutes per epoch, which could shallow keep quality for reasons unrelated to onsets. Measured guard in part (a): the per-row target-active fraction must stay >= 0.5x the v16 value (v16: 0.80 synthetic / 0.98 real-near).
- The round bundles four coupled changes (D1, D2, D3, L1+L1b) and therefore cannot attribute its own result. That is deliberate, and each component alone is measured-dead: the v17 lead alone is negative on cold-far (+0.35 dB, p=0.020); loss reweighting alone has no gradient on the cold failure (the diagnosis's central finding); more turn-taking alone already exists at 6% of rows with Delta_late ~= 0, so pooling dilutes it; and anchoring the ASR crop alone touches no row shape. If the bundle passes, the ablation round is the next 2 GPU-days, not this one.
- `m_onset` is an energy-based mask and the gate metric is an energy projection, so per the span-energy lesson neither can distinguish removed speech from removed noise or reverb. The proposal answers this with instruments, not with hope: gates 5 and 6 are ASR (Azure), and a harmonic peak/valley listening check on the field clips that move most is part of the verdict, not optional.
- Two loss-balance perturbations land in the same arm (OverSuppression's total value up ~1.8x; the ASR crop's coverage of t=0 up from 0.000 to 0.500 on long rows), so the total loss's composition changes even though no weight in the config changed except one term's type. Log every term separately (`self.verbose` already emits `train_step_loss_i`) and compare the per-term curves with v16's via `scripts/dump_loss_curves.py` before reading any field number.
- Not measured anywhere in this design: whether the model CAN represent 'a talker is arriving' without a persistent identity slot. If it cannot, the ceiling on gate 2 is above zero for architectural reasons and no data/loss round reaches it. The 48 h experiment's kill rule is written to fire in exactly that case.

**Go / no-go**

- PRIMARY 1 -- Dawn Chorus foreground-projection onset excess @0.5 s, condition `none`, n~450, paired Wilcoxon vs v16 ep19 (same utterances) — `benchmarks/probes/anchor_gate_cache.py dawn` for the new checkpoint, then `v19_plan/onset_profile/onset_real.py` (already written, reads the cache, no model calls). Metric = 10log10((<enh,ref>/<ref,ref>)^2) over the first 0.5 s of foreground activity minus the same over the remainder of the span. — pass: median >= -2.0 dB (from v16's -3.90) AND p5 tail >= -40 dB (from -75.6) AND p < 0.05 for the improvement — kill: median worse than -3.4 dB (i.e. < 0.5 dB of improvement), or p > 0.05, or the p5 tail no better than -60 dB
- PRIMARY 2 -- synthetic wrong-anchor deletion Delta_on1 (utt arm, other2 and other3 anchors, first 1 s of the user), n=200, paired per utterance — `v19_plan/anchor_synthetic/anchor_synthetic_probe.py --arm utt` on wer_set_moderate_test, ~10 min per checkpoint on one GPU, no ASR and no field set — pass: |Delta_on1| <= 0.5 dB for both other2 and other3 (from v16's -1.25 / -1.33) AND the same-talker null preserved (self3 within +-0.15 dB of 0) AND the three field nulls preserved in sign (bystanders-only n.s., far-talker anchor >= 0) — kill: |Delta_on1| > 1.0 dB on either anchor length, or self3 moves below -0.3 dB (the model has started deleting after its OWN anchor)
- PRIMARY 3 -- field set v3 KEEP violations with the OnsetGuard OFF, 5-checkpoint block mean (ep15-19), all 148 keep spans — `scripts/eval_field_block.py` over set v3 with BLOCK_CKPTS, dry_blend 1.0, guard disabled; compared with `compare` (per-clip paired Wilcoxon per group + the >=1 dB severity table, per FIELD_BLOCK.md) — pass: <= 13 / 148 (the number the guarded v8 arm achieves) with no keep clip deepening by >= 3 dB and no chain-selective damage in the severity table — kill: > 20 / 148 (i.e. no better than unguarded v16's 20) or any single keep clip deepening past -12 dB
- GUARDRAIL 4 -- sessions suppression, field set v3, block median (the round's price) — `scripts/eval_field_block.py`, sessions group, block mean per clip with per-checkpoint spread; paired Wilcoxon vs the v16 ep19 block — pass: <= -8.3 dB (inside v16 ep19's block band of -9.73 +-1.41) AND cold-far device `ambient` no shallower than -10.6 dB (v16 -12.65, allowance 2 dB) — kill: shallower than -7.5 dB on sessions, or shallower than -9.0 dB on ambient cold-far -- at that point the round has re-bought the AnchorGate's suppression price in the weights and has no advantage over shipping the guard
- GUARDRAIL 5 -- Dawn Chorus ASR deletion and WER, guard OFF, condition `none` and `background` — `scripts/eval_dawn_chorus.py --ckpt <v19 ep19> --dry-blend 1.0`, same recognizer and settings as the recorded v8/v16 numbers (deltas only, never cross-recognizer) — pass: deletion <= 0.15 in `none` (v16 unguarded 0.207; guarded arm 0.111) and no worse than v16 in `background`; WER <= 0.25 (v16 0.297) — kill: deletion > 0.25 or WER > 0.33 -- the round made the deployment-visible failure worse
- GUARDRAIL 6 -- moderate-reverb WER set, Azure, delta (enh - mix) with CI; primary WER gate per the BUT-OFFICE resolution lesson — `scripts/eval_wer.py --set-dir data_report/wer_set_moderate_test --asr azure` (prints CI; compare deltas because the 10 s cap inflates absolute deletion), against the v16 ep19 numbers in `benchmarks/wer_sets/AZURE_2026-09.md` — pass: delta not worse than v16 ep19 by more than +0.02 absolute WER, and the deletion component not worse by more than +0.02 — kill: delta worse than v16 by > 0.04 absolute WER outside the CI. BUT-OFFICE is NOT a gate (n=200, CI +-0.03, no resolution).
- GUARDRAIL 7 -- s2f keep-robustness sweep (native / 20 / 15 / 12 / 8 dB added noise) — `scripts/eval_s2f_keep_probe.py`, judged against the v16 ep19 baseline curve -0.25 / -0.31 / -0.38 / -0.55 / -1.02 (median) — pass: every point within +0.3 dB of the baseline curve; no new cliff (a cliff = any point > 1.5x the baseline point) — kill: any point worse than the baseline by > 0.8 dB, or a new cliff on a clip that had none
- GUARDRAIL 8 -- data-shape realisation (must be checked BEFORE any GPU time, and re-checked on the real run's first epoch) — `scripts/check_training_data.py` extended with the audit's temporal columns (onset, interferer-before-onset, longest target-free gap, re-entry rate, target-absent provenance, lead floor level), plus the logged per-batch `onset_frame_fraction` — pass: re-entry after >= 3 s of target-free gap on >= 15% of rows (today 0.3%); far speech >= 1 s before the near onset on >= 20% of rows (today 6.0%); lead rows 12-18% with 0% digital-silence leads (every lead's floor > -70 dBFS); per-row target-active fraction >= 0.5x v16; rows relabelled target_absent by the zero-target fix reported and < 3% — kill: re-entry < 10% of rows, or any digital-silence lead, or target-active fraction < 0.4x v16 -- the row shape and the length budget are then incompatible and the round must be redesigned before it is trained

**Implementation**

- 1. `puresound/config/augmentation.py` -- `OverlapControlConfig` (line 47): add `reentry_prob: Probability = 0.0`, `reentry_min_row_seconds: NonNegativeFloat = 9.0`, `reentry_gap_seconds: FloatRange = (3.0, 10.0)`, `reentry_gap_max_row_fraction: float = 0.45`, with a validator that `reentry_gap_seconds[0] * 2 <= reentry_min_row_seconds`. `RowInitialAmbientAugmentation` (line 478): add `bystander_lead_prob: Probability = 0.0` and `require_capture_floor: StrictBool = True`; widen the `lead_seconds_range` validator's docstring to say why 0.5 is now allowed.
- 2. `puresound/task/overlap_gating.py` -- `GatingResult`: append `near_frames: Optional[torch.Tensor] = None`. `OverlapGating.apply`: take `row_seconds: float`, add the guarded re-entry branch BEFORE the turn-taking branch, and thread `force_quiet_gap` into `_turn_taking` -> `_turn_script`. `_turn_script`: take `row_seconds` and `force_quiet_gap`, insert the no-speaker gap after the first turn, make the post-gap turn a near turn. `_turn_taking` and `_bernoulli`: return `near_frames` (the near mask; for `_bernoulli`, `target_active`). Update the module docstring -- it currently lists TWO regimes and there will be three.
- 3. `puresound/task/ns.py` -- `__getitem__`: pass `row_seconds` (from `self._row_length_override / self.audio_sr`, which is already set at line 310-313) into `self.overlap_gating.apply` (line 386); capture `gating.near_frames`. Add the target_present relabel right after line 398. Extend the row-initial-ambient block (lines 540-569) with the bystander variant (mask only `target_speech` + `vad_reference`, leave `noisy_speech`'s interferer content) and record `lead_applied` for the noise stage. Call `NoiseStage.apply(..., force_floor=lead_applied and ria.require_capture_floor)` at line 574. Add `_build_onset_weight` and call it right after `vad_reference = target_speech.clone()` (line 583); emit `sample['onset_weight']` and the `onset_frame_fraction` scalar. Note that `_build_onset_weight` must run on the PRE-device-chain reference, like the VAD label, for the same reason.
- 4. `puresound/task/noise_stage.py` -- `apply(..., force_floor: bool = False)` and `_capture_floor(noisy, force: bool = False)`: draw first, then `if force or draw < prob`, so a forced floor does not shift the RNG stream (the stage's stated contract).
- 5. `puresound/task/voice_isolation.py` -- add `onset_frame_fraction` to `VOICE_ISOLATION_SCALAR_KEYS` (line 25) and emit it in `_emit_task_metadata`; `VoiceIsolationCollateFunc.__call__` (line 576): pad-stack `onset_weight` with zeros exactly as `vad_target` is handled in the parent collate.
- 6. `puresound/nnet/loss/stft_loss.py` -- add `OnsetWeightedOverSuppressionLoss` after `OverSuppressionLoss` (line 229), with `required_inputs = ('enhanced', 'target', 'batch')`, args `(p, fft_size, hop_size, win_length, beta, weight_key)`, the nearest-neighbour VAD->STFT index gather, and the plain-numel denominator. Reference implementation: /tmp/claude-1001/-home-milowu-A4Audio-PureSound/c52f3b49-8e8f-4f9f-a5ca-862771bd494c/scratchpad/v19_plan/design_data_loss/loss_sketch.py
- 7. `puresound/nnet/loss/__init__.py` -- import it and add it to `__all__` (that list is what `recipes.py::init_loss_func`'s `getattr(ploss, item.type)` resolves against).
- 8. `puresound/nnet/loss/asr_feature.py` -- `__init__(..., onset_crop_prob: float = 0.0)`; in `forward` (lines 85-91) `start = 0 if (crop is not None and length > crop and torch.rand(1).item() < self.onset_crop_prob) else randint(...)`. Keep the same crop for enhanced and target (the existing invariant).
- 9. `egs/voice_isolate/config/exp/train_dpcrn_v19_return.yaml` -- copy of `train_dpcrn_v16_lengthmix.yaml` with: `overlap_control.turn_taking_prob: 0.35` + the four reentry fields; a new `augmentation_row_initial_ambient` block (used/prob 0.15/lead 0.5-3.0/fade 50/bystander 0.5/require_capture_floor True); `length_schedule` probs 0.10/0.20/0.35/0.35; `OverSuppressionLoss` -> `OnsetWeightedOverSuppressionLoss` with `beta: 7.0` at the same `weighted: 3.0`; `onset_crop_prob: 0.5` on both `ASRFeatureLoss` entries; `work_folder: exp/dpcrn_v19_return`; `max_epochs: 20`. Header comment stating the round's ONE knob set and the warm-start command verbatim.

This is guardrail 8's instrument, so it belongs in the repo, not in a scratch script.
- 11. Tests. `test/test_task/` (new `test_reentry_rows.py`): with all new knobs at defaults, a seeded row is bit-identical to the pre-change dataset (golden tensor); with `reentry_prob=1.0` on a 12 s row, the near mask has a >= 3 s hole, the far mask does not fill it, and the gap's mixture energy is above -70 dBFS (never digital silence); the zero-target relabel fires. `test/test_losses/`: `OnsetWeightedOverSuppressionLoss(beta=0)` equals `OverSuppressionLoss` to 1e-7 on random tensors; the VAD->STFT index map is monotone and in range; a missing `onset_weight` falls back to the unweighted mean rather than crashing. Extend `test/test_row_initial_ambient.py` for the bystander lead and the forced floor. `test/test_length_schedule.py`: assert the realised row shares of the new schedule and that audio-seconds per batch is unchanged.

**48 h kill experiment:** Two parts, both inside 48 h, and part (b) is the one that can kill the proposal.

**(a) 0-6 h, CPU only, no GPU.** Land items 1-5 and 10 from the implementation list, then run the extended `scripts/check_training_data.py` over 1200 rows of the v19 config through the REAL dataloader (the audit's method: `egs.voice_isolate.main.init_dataloader`, seed 7, 104 consecutive batches) and over 1200 rows of the unchanged v16 config as the control. Guardrail 8's thresholds decide: re-entry after >= 3 s of target-free gap on >= 15% of rows (v16: 0.3%), far speech >= 1 s before the near onset on >= 20% of rows (v16: 6.0%), lead rows 12-18% with ZERO digital-silence leads, per-row target-active fraction >= 0.5x v16, and the seeded bit-exactness test passing with the knobs off. If the shape cannot be produced at >= 10% of rows without collapsing near-speech mass, the row shape and the length budget are incompatible and the proposal dies here for 6 CPU-hours.

**(b) 6-44 h, ~30 GPU-hours.** Warm-start the FULL arm from v16 ep19 (`--pretrained_ckpt_path`, weights only) for **6 epochs** (~3.8 h wall on 2 GPUs at v16's measured 38 min/epoch), twice: `beta = 7` and `beta = 3` (2 x 3.8 h = 7.6 h wall), plus one control arm with the data changes and `beta = 0` (3.8 h) so the 48 h test also says whether the loss half is doing anything. Then read ONLY the two fast metrics, no ASR and no field block:

* `anchor_synthetic_probe.py --arm utt` on the 200 moderate utterances, ~10 min per checkpoint -> Delta_on1 for other2 / other3, and the self3 null.
* `anchor_gate_cache.py dawn` + `onset_profile/onset_real.py` -> Dawn foreground-projection onset excess @0.5 s, n~450 (p < 1e-20 at that n, so a 1 dB move is unmissable), ~25 min per checkpoint.
* plus `scripts/eval_coldstart_v2.py` on the cold-start field subset, single checkpoint but same-run, purely as a blow-up detector.

**Pre-registered kill rule.** The proposal is wrong and is abandoned if, at ep6 on the better of the two beta arms, BOTH of these hold: (i) Delta_on1 for other2 has not moved at least 40% toward zero (i.e. is still worse than -0.75 dB, from v16's -1.25), AND (ii) the Dawn onset excess @0.5 s has not improved by >= 1.0 dB (i.e. is still worse than -2.90 dB, from v16's -3.90). Part (a) will already have proved the row shape is in the data at >= 15% of rows and the logged `onset_frame_fraction` will have proved the loss is looking at it, so a null on both metrics means the mechanism is not learnable from row shape plus reweighting -- there is no version of "train longer" that rescues an angle whose two target metrics are flat at the point where 30% of the cycle is spent. Independent immediate kill: if the cold-start field subset collapses shallower than -5 dB median suppression at ep6, the anti-deletion pressure has eaten the suppression and the arm is dead regardless of the onset numbers.

Why 6 epochs is enough to decide: this is an output-side behaviour, not a representation change (v17 and v18 both showed their sign inside the first third of their cycle, and v18's negative cold-far delta was significant at p=0.017 well before its trough), and the two metrics carry n=200 paired and n=450 paired with baseline effects of -1.25 dB (p~1e-24) and -3.90 dB (p~2e-21). What 6 epochs cannot decide is the PRICE -- suppression depth, ASR deletion, keep violations -- which is why passing (b) buys the remaining ~1.7 GPU-days for the full cycle and the 5-checkpoint block, and does not by itself buy a deploy.


## v19-FGMEM: explicit foreground memory (slow decay-free slot, self-enrolled, FiLM-conditioned)

_Angle:_ EXPLICIT FOREGROUND MEMORY: replace the inter-time LSTM's implicit recency template with a slow, decay-free foreground slot written under a relative confidence gate, read one frame late, and injected into the mask through a zero-initialised per-frame FiLM between the two DPRNN blocks; self-enrolled from the model's own bottleneck (no user enrollment), and trained on manufactured session rows where the user is not the first talker and re-enters after a multi-second gap.

Give the network the state it is currently faking. The only carrier of "who is the foreground" today is the inter-time LSTM, and the probes say exactly what that carrier implements: a recent-talker template that ~1 s of any same-chain speech arms, 2 s saturates, half forgets after 5 s of floor and fully forgets by 10 s (field), with the same clock reproduced on synthetic (gap-corrected decay -8.23/-4.00/-2.81/-0.89 dB at 2/5/10/20 s on v8; -7.93/-4.57/-2.01/-0.70 on v16). Because that carrier is a fading recency state, "remember the user across silence" and "delete the next talker" are one variable, which is why every attempt to lengthen memory made wrong-anchor deletion worse (reference_matrix 6.2, now confirmed on synthetic). FGMEM adds an explicit slow slot: a D=64 template accumulated as a confidence-weighted running mean with NO decay term at all (silence writes nothing and forgets nothing), read strictly causally, and fed to the mask via a zero-initialised per-frame FiLM so a v16 warm start starts bit-identical. Two mechanisms make the slot the USER and not merely the first talker: the write weight is a per-frame foreground-presence logit compared against the session's OWN running mean of that logit (relative, never an absolute threshold - self-calibration survived two chain/checkpoint transitions where absolute thresholds died twice), and accumulated evidence is discounted ONLY on contradiction (a confidently nearer, dissimilar talker), never on silence, which is the explicit break of the persistence/wrong-anchor coupling. Supervision is self-enrolment: the row's own near/far frame labels train the write gate, and the SAME row's far-exclusive frames are the hard negatives that force the template to encode the talker rather than the capture chain. Data supplies the shape the training set has never contained - bystander first, then 3-12 s of the row's own room floor (never digital silence), then the user re-enters - and a frame-weighted anti-suppression term puts loss mass on that re-entry second, which is where all the measured damage sits (Δon1 -1.7 dB vs Δlate -0.10 dB).

**Objective**

ARCHITECTURE (new file puresound/nnet/lobe/memory.py, own pydantic config + from_config, the heads.py pattern; streaming step/initial_stream_state contract copied from VADHead).

State, all fp32 (heads.py's own lesson: a 1 s tau at 100 fps has a=0.01 and vanishes in bf16):
  S_w in R[N] (decay-free evidence mass), S_wu in R[N,D] (decay-free weighted sum),
  C in R[N] (discountable mass), A in R[N,D] (discountable weighted sum),
  theta in R[N] (session's own running mean presence logit over speech frames), k in R[N].
  m_bar_t = S_wu,t / max(S_w,t, eps);  m_t = A_t / max(C_t, eps).

Per frame, from the block-1 bottleneck frequency-pooled h_t in R[N,128]:
  z_t     = normalize(W_u h_t)                                   # D=64 candidate template
  nu_t    = write_head(bottleneck)_t                             # VADHead-shaped presence logit
  s_t     = sigmoid(nu_t)
  k_t     = k_{t-1} + s_t
  theta_t = theta_{t-1} + s_t*(nu_t - theta_{t-1})/max(k_t,1)     # self-calibrated reference
  w_t     = s_t * sigmoid((nu_t - theta_{t-1})/tau + beta)        # RELATIVE write weight
  d_t     = s_t * sigmoid((nu_t - theta_{t-1})/tau) * relu(gamma - cos(z_t, m_bar_{t-1}))
  rho_t   = exp(-lambda * d_t)                                    # <=1, and ==1 in silence
  S_w,t   = S_w,t-1 + w_t                ; S_wu,t = S_wu,t-1 + w_t z_t
  C_t     = rho_t * C_{t-1} + w_t        ; A_t    = rho_t * A_{t-1} + w_t z_t
  read_t  = [ m_{t-1} , phi(C_{t-1}, k_{t-1}, cos(m_{t-1}, m_bar_{t-1})) ] in R^{D+4}
Defaults: D=64, tau=1.0, beta=0.0 (so a first talker with no reference writes at ~0.5), gamma=0.5, lambda=1.0, phi = (tanh(C/8), tanh(k/100), the cosine, 1[C>0]).

Parallel training path: S_w and S_wu are plain cumsums, so m_bar_{t-1} and hence rho_t are known for all t without a recurrence; A_t, C_t are then a LINEAR first-order recurrence with known coefficients and are computed by an exact chunked associative scan (chunk 128, cumprod of rho in fp32 with per-chunk renormalisation, rho clamped >= 1e-6). No Python loop over T, and train/stream arithmetic is identical because the streaming step maintains the same six tensors. This is precisely why d_t compares z_t against m_bar (cumsum) and not against m (recurrent): the latter is a genuine nonlinear recurrence and would cost a 3000-step loop per row.

Conditioning: new TemporalFiLM in puresound/nnet/lobe/trivial.py, beside FiLM (which is NOT modified - the dvec/TSE path stays frozen legacy):
  [gamma_t; beta_t] = W_f read_t  (W_f zero-initialised, Linear(D+4 -> 2*128))
  x[c,f,t] <- x[c,f,t] * (1 + gamma[c,t]) + beta[c,t]     # broadcast over the 64 bottleneck freq bins
Zero-init => exact identity at step 0, so the warm start carries no re-initialisation debt (the zero-init-parallel method kept from the negative v14 round).

Wiring, three lines in DPCRN.forward:
  x = self.dprnn_block1(x); mem = self.fg_memory(x); x = self.fg_film(x, mem["read"]); x = self.dprnn_block2(x)
read_dropout=0.15 per row zeroes the read during training, so the model must remain functional with an empty slot - which also makes the "memory off" ablation free at eval time (no second run).

LOSSES (added to the v16 list; every existing weight unchanged EXCEPT OverSuppressionLoss 3.0 -> 2.0, whose removed mass re-enters through the weighted twin, so total anti-suppression pressure is conserved; v18's lesson is "do not relax the floors", and this does not):
  L_write   (w 0.3): masked BCE(nu_t, near_active_t). Mask excludes real-near rows' gap frames and uses the -25 dB relative detector on real-near rows (see data caveat below).
  L_mem     (w 0.2): InfoNCE with kappa=0.1 between z_t and the row's memory read; positives = near-exclusive frames (near active, far inactive), hard negatives = the SAME row's far-exclusive frames (same capture chain, different talker) plus in-batch negatives.
    L_mem = -(1/|P|) sum_{t in P} log[ exp(cos(z_t, m_hat)/kappa) / (exp(cos(z_t,m_hat)/kappa) + sum_{u in Nrow ∪ Nbatch} exp(cos(z_u,m_hat)/kappa)) ], m_hat = read at the row's last near-active frame.
  L_keep_on (w 1.5): new class OnsetWeightedOverSuppressionLoss = mean over T-F bins of omega_t * relu(|T|^p - |E|^p)^2 with p=0.5 (the shipped floor kept), omega_t = 1 + (W-1)*1[t within the first 1 s after a near onset preceded by >= 1.5 s of near-inactive], W=3. Needs the STFT-hop-128 -> VAD-hop-160 index map floor(t*128/160).
Total loss weight goes 7.0 -> 8.0; nothing else is re-balanced, and that shift is documented in the recipe.

DATA (row generator, new third regime in puresound/task/overlap_gating.py beside _bernoulli and _turn_script):
  _session_script, drawn with session_prob on rows of length >= 6 s:
    with prob bystander_first_prob = 0.6:  far-solo turn of U(1.2, 4.0) s      # the wrong-anchor prefix
    gap: U(3.0, 6.0) s on 6 s rows, U(5.0, 12.0) s on 12/30 s rows             # near AND far gated to zero
    near re-entry turn: U(1.8, 5.0) s                                          # the scored window
    then alternate as _turn_script does
