  Invariants kept: the near label and the near contribution to the mixture are gated by the same envelope (the module's stated contract), and the gap is NEVER digital silence - gating runs before noise_stage.apply in ns.py __getitem__, so the gap carries the row's own noise, and on session rows absolute_floor.prob is forced to 1.0. Assert the near mask retains >= 0.8 s of active frames or fall back to _bernoulli (the audit's gated-to-zero-but-labelled-present bug).
  New emissions: per-frame keep_weight [T] on the 100 fps grid, plus scalars session_row, reentry_onset_frame, gap_seconds appended to VOICE_ISOLATION_SCALAR_KEYS.
  length_schedule is NOT touched, so v19 remains a one-axis change against v16.

**Data changes**

- New session-row regime `_session_script` in puresound/task/overlap_gating.py: optional far-solo opener U(1.2,4.0) s at prob 0.6, then a gap of U(3,6) s (6 s rows) / U(5,12) s (12 and 30 s rows) with near AND far gated to zero, then a near re-entry turn of U(1.8,5.0) s, then normal alternation. This is the row shape the audit measured as absent: re-entry after >=5 s of absence is 0.3% of v16 rows (4/1165) and 0.0% of the 3 s bucket, whose longest interior target gap ever observed was 1.71 s.
- Row share stated in ROWS, not batches (the audit's instruction): session_prob=0.35 applied only to rows of length >=6 s. Measured eligible share is 37.8% (6 s) + 9.9% (12 s) + 2.5% (30 s) = 50.2% of rows, so session rows land at ~17.6% of all rows. trainer.length_schedule is left exactly as v16 so the round stays one axis.
- The gap must carry the row's own floor, never digital zeros. Overlap gating runs before noise_stage.apply in ns.py __getitem__, so the gap inherits the row's noise automatically; on session rows augmentation_noise.absolute_floor.prob is forced to 1.0 so a dry row cannot produce a digitally silent gap. Grounding: digital silence measured -5.90 dB (v8) / -19.22 dB (v16) suppress bias on the lone-interferer arm and -2.33 dB onset deletion on v16's keep side, and it is the ONE synthetic prefix that already reproduces Dawn's tail (v16 sil1 p5 -70.2 vs Dawn -75.6). Silence stays a probe, never a training prefix.
- Guard against the audit's gated-to-zero bug: the session script asserts >=0.8 s of near-active frames survive the envelope and otherwise falls back to `_bernoulli`. In v16, 1.0% of rows (all in the 3 s bucket) had their near talker gated to exact zero by a far turn while metadata still reported target_present=1.0.
- Per-frame `keep_weight` tensor emitted by the dataset (100 fps, hop 160), = 1 + (W-1) on the first 1 s after any near onset preceded by >=1.5 s of near-inactive. This is the first per-frame weight tensor in the batch; the audit confirmed none exists and that the provider table exposes no weight channel.
- New scalars `session_row`, `reentry_onset_frame`, `gap_seconds` appended to VOICE_ISOLATION_SCALAR_KEYS in puresound/task/voice_isolation.py, so eval can bucket by row shape.
- Write-gate label fix for real-near rows: their targets carry the recording's own room tone (target 5th-percentile frame -38.9 dB vs -56.5 dB synthetic; energy-VAD active fraction 0.98 vs 0.80), so the energy label would teach the write gate 'real chain => user always present'. L_write uses the -25 dB relative detector on real-near rows and masks their gap frames out entirely.
- scripts/check_training_data.py grows the temporal columns the audit prototyped (onset, interferer-before-onset, longest target-free gap, re-entry rate, target-absent provenance, session-row share) so the realised row shares are verified before any GPU is spent, not after.

**Architecture changes**

- New puresound/nnet/lobe/memory.py: `ForegroundMemory` + `ForegroundMemoryConfig` (own from_config, the heads.py convention). Holds W_u (Linear 128->64), an internal VADHead-shaped `write_head` reading the block-1 bottleneck (hidden 64, ema_taus_s ~[1.0, 4.0], causal dwconv kernel_t 3), the six-tensor fp32 state, the exact chunked associative scan for the training path, and `initial_stream_state`/`step` mirroring VADHead's streaming contract.
- New `TemporalFiLM` in puresound/nnet/lobe/trivial.py: Linear(D+4 -> 2C) with zero-initialised weight and bias, applied per frame and broadcast over the 64 bottleneck frequency bins as x*(1+gamma)+beta. Existing `FiLM` and the dvec/TSE conditioning path are NOT touched (backbones are library assets; TSE/SV stay frozen legacy).
- puresound/nnet/dpcrn.py: `DPCRN.__init__` builds `self.fg_memory` and `self.fg_film` from a new `backbone_args.foreground_memory` block; `DPCRN.forward` gains three lines between dprnn_block1 and dprnn_block2 (memory forward -> TemporalFiLM -> block2) and stashes `self.last_fg_memory` (dict of read, z, w, nu, theta) as a side output next to last_vad_logits / last_dist_preds. `DPRNNblock2D` is unchanged.
- puresound/system/siso.py: one new entry in `_loss_providers` -- `"fg_memory": lambda: side("last_fg_memory")` -- and one in `"keep_weight": lambda: (batch or {}).get("keep_weight")` (or read through the existing `batch` provider; the batch route needs no table change). Mirrors dist_preds exactly.
- New losses: `ForegroundWriteGateLoss` and `ForegroundMemoryContrastLoss` in puresound/nnet/loss/memory.py (required_inputs = ("fg_memory", "vad_target", "batch")), and `OnsetWeightedOverSuppressionLoss` in puresound/nnet/loss/stft_loss.py (required_inputs = ("enhanced", "target", "batch")). The existing SDR / MR-STFT / OverSuppression classes mean-reduce internally, which is why the weighted term must be a new class rather than an edit -- the audit's finding.
- puresound/streaming/dpcrn.py: the export enumerates head states as 3-tuples (`_head_state_names`, `initial_state`, `state_from_tensors`, `_state_to_tuple`). Pack the memory state as three tensors -- [N,2,D] (A, S_wu), [N,4] (C, S_w, theta, k) -- and register `write_head` as an ordinary head so it keeps its own triple; add the FiLM hook inside `_forward_feature_frame`. scripts/streaming_onnx.py gains the new port names.
- Computed cost (not measured): W_u 8,256 + write_head 24,961 + TemporalFiLM 17,664 = 50,881 new parameters = +6.35% on the v16 backbone (measured 801,743 backbone params in the ep19 state dict), and about 51 k MAC/frame ~ 5.1 MMAC/s at 100 fps. No extra forward pass anywhere.
- New recipe egs/voice_isolate/config/exp/train_dpcrn_v19_fgmem.yaml: v16 verbatim plus backbone_args.foreground_memory, augmentation_speech.overlap_control.session_* keys, the three new loss entries, OverSuppressionLoss 3.0 -> 2.0, and absolute_floor.prob forced to 1.0 on session rows.

**Expected effects**

- The write gate learns 'whoever is loudest/most recent' anyway (nu correlates with recency through the LSTM that feeds it), so the slot reproduces the recency template with extra parameters and Δon1 does not move; or the demotion clause fires on the user's own onset (z_t dissimilar to a half-built template) and deletes the user harder than v16.: Wrong-anchor deletion on synthetic (anchor_synthetic utt arm, Δon1 with a 2 s different-talker anchor) at least halves: v16 -1.25 dB -> >= -0.63 dB, v8-equivalent -1.71 -> >= -0.85, with the same-talker null (+0.04) unchanged. — _The failure is already in-distribution and enormous in significance (p ~ 1e-25, 173/27 of 200 utterances down), so gradient exists. The mechanism it attacks is exactly the measured one: the anchor is a recency template, and the write gate replaces recency with a foreground-presence comparison that a bystander prefix fails (nu below the session's own theta), while the demotion clause lets the entering user discount the bystander's accumulated evidence. The ident ladder (self +0.01, selflate -0.04, samespk -0.44, diffspk -1.47) is the readout for whether the template became talker-identity rather than channel-signature._
- The coupling is not broken: persistence improves and wrong-anchor deletion gets worse in lockstep - the pre-registered kill combination. Or the write gate fires on room tone / stationary noise during the gap (a 3-12 s gap is 300-1200 frames of accumulation at even small w_t), so the template drifts toward the floor and the model treats the returning user as a foreground change.: The anchor survives silence: gap-corrected anchor decay at 10 s goes from -2.01 dB (v16) to <= -6.0 dB, and at 20 s from -0.70 to <= -4.0, WITHOUT the wrong-anchor number regressing. — _Persistence is architectural, not learned: with w_t ~ 0 in silence the state is literally unchanged, so there is no decay to fight. The data change supplies the only missing ingredient - rows where a decision must be re-made after the context has gone stale (v16 has 0.3% of such rows). The two clocks are decoupled by construction: silence multiplies rho by exactly 1 (no forgetting) while contradiction discounts evidence (fast re-assignment), which is the explicit break reference_matrix 6.2 demands and which anchor_synthetic confirmed is needed (the wrong-anchor deletion and the decay share one clock today, both closing by 20 s)._
- Zero movement on Dawn while the synthetic numbers improve - i.e. the manufactured row does not proxy the real prefix (the same 11-23x real/synthetic gap that onset_profile measured on the current distribution). That result would say the onset axis is a chain axis, not a data axis, and the shipped guard stays.: Real-recording onset deletion improves mainly on the bystander-first half: Dawn foreground-projection excess @0.5 s on the onset>=1 s subset from -6.07 dB (v16) to >= -3.0 dB; the true cold-onset subset (-4.61 dB) improves only modestly, target >= -3.1 dB. — _onset_profile's split says the bystander-first onset is 1.3-1.8x worse than the cold onset and is the condition the AnchorGate's pna clause fixes - and it is the condition the new session rows manufacture directly. The cold half is expected to move less because the synthetic distribution cannot teach it (median -0.25 to +0.30 dB there); what helps it is only the read's cold token (C~0 => 'nothing enrolled') being trained to mean KEEP on the bystander-first rows' opening._
- The keep-side weight (L_keep_on at W=3, plus the memory read) buys deletion relief exactly the way the guard does - by not suppressing - so deletion falls and far suppression falls with it. That is the v11b failure pattern (a real representation gain bought with roughly half the anchored far suppression) and it is a kill, not a pass.: Dawn Chorus ASR deletion with the OnsetGuard OFF: 0.207 (v16) -> <= 0.150, stretch <= 0.123 (what the guard alone achieves on v8), with WER 0.297 -> <= 0.24 and the bystander penalty (+0.028) not growing. — _The guard prices the prize: the pna clause alone removes half the keep violations (26->13 on v8, 20->7 on v16) and the relative-DRR arm takes Dawn deletion to 0.096/0.094, all arms p<1e-26. About 0.13 absolute deletion is onset plus wrong-anchor deletion, and a trained fix is supposed to collect it without paying the guard's suppression price. Making 'the model no longer needs the guard' checkable is the round's stated success criterion._
- Suppression collapses to the -6 dB range like v11b, or new keep violations appear on the 0D sentinel recording whose noise floor is 25 dB high (v8 already has 4). Span-energy conflation means a 'keep violation' may be noise not speech, so every flagged span goes through the harmonic peak-valley check before it counts.: Field-set behaviour: anchored session suppression stays inside the v16 ep19 5-checkpoint block band (>= -8.0 dB against the block's -9.73), keep violations on set v3 do not increase, and the anchored-condition onset deletion (near_speech dt -3.86 dB on v8, 0.5 s window) improves. — _The field set's cold `none` column cannot move here (both checkpoints are passthrough cold, steady-state delta -0.12 to -0.17 dB, so there is no attenuation to concentrate at the onset) - the field evidence for this axis lives entirely in the anchored stream / near_speech conditions, which is where the wrong-anchor mechanism is visible. Judged as 5-checkpoint blocks with paired Wilcoxon because single-checkpoint field deltas are noise (same-run spread -0.35..-3.39 dB median, 19.9 dB single-clip swing)._
- The write gate inverts on the QVF chain like every previous readout, so on that chain the slot enrols the far talker and the FiLM actively deletes the user - strictly worse than v16, which at least ignores the readout. Mitigations are read_dropout (making 'memory off' a free eval arm), the zero-init FiLM, and the pre-registered QVF group floor in E1.: In-domain write-gate frame AUC >= 0.90 and held-out (180D real recording) >= 0.75, with the RELATIVE form beating the absolute-sigmoid form on the QVF groups. — _v11's presence heads reached in-domain 0.953 and held-out-180D 0.843 after the reverb-confound fix - the first presence signal that survived an unseen real recording - so this is a demonstrated capability of a per-frame head on this bottleneck. The relative form is required because the trained readout inverts on the QVF chain (DistHead 0.258 there) while the features still carry the cue (device-fitted linear probe reads QVF at 0.789): the information is present, the absolute mapping is what fails, and within-session contrast survived twice where fixed thresholds died twice._

**Cost:** Engineering: ~6 days to implement (memory module + scan 2 d, TemporalFiLM + DPCRN wiring 0.5 d, three losses + provider 1 d, session-row generator + keep_weight + scalars 1.5 d, check_training_data columns + tests 1 d), plus ~1 d for the streaming-export ports (needed for the RTF/export gate, not for the training verdict). Probes: the 48 h kill package is ~1 CPU-day plus ~4 GPU-hours (E2 forwards over DiPCo/AMI/NOTSOFAR segments). GPU: one 20-epoch cosine cycle warm-started from v16 ep19 at the v16 recipe's own throughput (1000 iter/epoch, accumulate 2, 2 GPUs, bf16-mixed) plus a computed ~6% step-cost increase from the new module; I did NOT measure wall-clock and no config records it, so budget it as the same size as the v16/v17/v18 cycles (each of which was one 20-epoch warm-started cycle on 2 GPUs) - roughly 4-6 GPU-days, estimate not measurement. A second cycle to ep39 (another 4-6 GPU-days) only if the ep19 synthetic gate passes; the field block needs no extra training because the cycle already writes ep15-19. The attribution ablation ("memory read zeroed = data-only effect") costs ZERO GPU because read_dropout trains the model to run without the read. Warm-start plan: from /work/any_exp_link/puresound_exp/dpcrn_v16_lengthmix/lightning_logs/version_0/checkpoints/epoch=19-step=10000.ckpt; reload_checkpoint copies by name and only WARNS on missing keys, so the eight new tensors keep their zero/default init - scripts/preflight_ckpt_recipe.py must confirm the missing-key list contains exactly those eight names and nothing else before the run starts. Never train the last stage from scratch: the ladder is load-bearing (final-stage-only from scratch reaches -1.65 dB real far suppression against v8's -9.83).

**Risks**

- The slot enrols the wrong talker on an unseen chain. Every readout so far inverts on QVF (DistHead 0.258, v11 presence 0.253) and here the readout drives the mask, so an inversion is worse than ignoring it. Mitigations: the write gate consumes only (nu - theta), a within-session contrast; read_dropout 0.15 keeps a working 'memory off' mode; the FiLM is zero-init so conditioning must earn its way in; E1 pre-registers a per-QVF-group floor and kills the round before training if the relative gate cannot separate keep from suppress spans.
- The persistence/wrong-anchor coupling is not actually broken. reference_matrix 6.2 warned it, anchor_synthetic confirmed it on synthetic (both halves share one clock and close by 20 s). The demotion clause is the only thing separating them and it is the least-tested piece of the design; if it under-fires the user cannot take the slot, if it over-fires the user's own onset discounts their own template.
- The template anchors the capture chain, not the person. Real-e2e round-1 died exactly this way, and on synthetic a same-speaker-different-room anchor already costs -0.44 dB (about 30% of the wrong-anchor effect). L_mem's same-row far-frame negatives hold the chain fixed while changing the talker, which is the only such pair the pipeline can produce; E2 is the pre-registered kill test on paired corpora because the existing per-frame cache cannot answer it (one talker per Dawn recording).
- The keep-side weight buys deletion relief by not suppressing - the guard's own trade, and v11b's failure signature (representation gain for half the anchored far suppression). W is capped at 3, the existing OverSuppression weight only drops 3.0 -> 2.0 so total anti-suppression pressure is conserved, and the suppression block band is a hard kill.
- The write gate accumulates on room tone during a 3-12 s gap (300-1200 frames), drifting the template toward the floor. L_write labels those frames 0, but real-near rows' own room tone is 18 dB above the synthetic floor and would teach the opposite - hence the -25 dB relative detector and the gap-frame mask on those rows. If this leaks, the gap becomes a slow poison rather than a no-op.
- New parameters on a warm start perturb the ladder. Zero-init makes step 0 bit-identical, but the LSTM can still re-organise around the new conditioning path and lose the ladder's real-recording suppression; that is what the 5-checkpoint field block is for, and why the cosine trough is the only comparison point.
- Row budget: session rows take ~17.6% of rows away from the current distribution, which is already at 88% of the v8 recipe's scene diversity. If suppression degrades uniformly rather than at the session shapes, this is the suspect - checkable by bucketing eval on the new session_row scalar.
- Numerics: rho^T underflows over 3000 frames. The scan is fp32 with per-chunk renormalisation and rho clamped >= 1e-6, and the state is fp32 regardless of autocast (the exact bug heads.py documents for its EMA bank and lfilter clamp). A silent freeze of the slow state would look like a no-op round rather than a bug.
- Metric conflation: keep/suppress span energy mixes speech, noise and reverb (a user-caught error), so this round's keep-side verdicts use the foreground-projection gain 10log10((<enh,ref>/<ref,ref>)^2) where a reference exists and route every flagged field span through the harmonic peak-valley check.
- This axis is not expected to move the QVF cold-far column at all (chain problem: scenario clips are context-insensitive, and eq_probe's compressor verdict is withdrawn because that probe fed the model a DC signal). Reporting it as a v19 gate would make a genuine pass look like a failure, or vice versa.

**Go / no-go**

- Wrong-anchor deletion, synthetic: Δon1 over the user's first 1 s with a 2 s different-talker anchor (paired vs `none`, n=200) — scratchpad/v19_plan/anchor_synthetic/anchor_synthetic_probe.py, utt arm, wer_set_moderate_test, ~10 min/ckpt — pass: >= -0.63 dB (>=50% reduction vs v16 ep19's -1.25 dB) with the same-talker null still within +/-0.10 dB — kill: <= -1.5 dB (worse than v16) or the same-talker null falls below -0.30 dB
- Anchor persistence: gap-corrected decay of lone-interferer suppression after a 10 s quiet gap (and 20 s) — anchor_synthetic_probe.py, bgonly arm, decayq10/gapq10 conditions — pass: <= -6.0 dB at 10 s and <= -4.0 dB at 20 s (v16: -2.01 / -0.70) — kill: >= -3.0 dB at 10 s (no persistence gained) while the wrong-anchor gate also fails
- Write-gate frame AUC (foreground present vs not), in-domain and on the held-out 180D real recording — benchmarks/probes/presence_head_judgment.py (the v11b judgment tool), plus the validation hook logging in-domain AUC per epoch — pass: >= 0.90 in-domain and >= 0.75 on 180D; relative form >= absolute form on all 4 QVF groups — kill: < 0.80 in-domain or < 0.65 on 180D
- Real onset deletion: Dawn foreground-projection excess @0.5 s, onset>=1 s (bystander-first) subset, n=217 — scratchpad/v19_plan/onset_profile/onset_real.py over a new agcache tag for the v19 checkpoint — pass: >= -3.0 dB (v16 ep19: -6.07 dB), with p10 better than -30 dB — kill: <= -5.5 dB (no resolvable improvement)
- Dawn Chorus ASR deletion and WER, OnsetGuard OFF, dry_blend 1.0 — scripts/eval_dawn_chorus.py + scripts/eval_wer.py (Azure), deltas against the unprocessed mix per the 10 s-cap caveat — pass: deletion <= 0.150 and WER <= 0.24 (v16: 0.207 / 0.297); stretch goal deletion <= 0.123 — kill: deletion > 0.207 (worse than v16 unguarded)
- Moderate-reverb WER set (primary WER gate; BUT-OFFICE has no resolution at n=200, CI +/-0.03) — scripts/eval_wer.py on data_report/wer_set_moderate_test, CI printed, compared as deltas vs mix — pass: no CI-resolvable regression vs v16 ep19 — kill: any CI-resolvable WER or deletion regression vs v16 ep19
- Field anchored/session far suppression, 5-checkpoint block, paired Wilcoxon — scripts/eval_field_block.py on field set v3 (benchmarks/field_test_vector/FIELD_BLOCK.md protocol), ep15-19 of the cycle — pass: >= -8.0 dB block median (v16 ep19 block: -9.73 dB), p > 0.05 for the difference — kill: < -6.5 dB block median (the v11b failure level)
- Field set v3 KEEP violations, scored on absolute residual and confirmed by harmonic peak-valley — scripts/eval_realcase.py + scripts/audit_span_metric.py on every flagged span — pass: <= v16 ep19's count, and no new violation on the 0D sentinel recording — kill: > v16 ep19 + 2 confirmed (harmonic-checked) violations
- Deployment budget: CPU RTF and streaming export parity — scripts/streaming_onnx.py export + the existing frame-model parity check — pass: export succeeds with the new state ports, frame-model output matches the offline forward, CPU RTF within +10% of v16 — kill: export fails, parity mismatch, or RTF > +10%

**Implementation**

- 1. puresound/nnet/lobe/memory.py (new): ForegroundMemoryConfig + ForegroundMemory - W_u, the internal VADHead-shaped write_head, the six fp32 state tensors, the chunked associative scan for forward(), and initial_stream_state()/step() copied in shape from VADHead (heads.py:170-215).
- 2. puresound/nnet/lobe/trivial.py: add TemporalFiLM (zero-init Linear(D+4 -> 2C), per-frame, broadcast over freq). Do not touch FiLM (lines 129-167) or the dvec path.
- 3. puresound/nnet/dpcrn.py: DPCRN.__init__ builds self.fg_memory / self.fg_film from backbone_args.foreground_memory; DPCRN.forward inserts memory + FiLM between dprnn_block1 and dprnn_block2 and stashes self.last_fg_memory beside last_vad_logits / last_dist_preds (dpcrn.py:250-300).
- 4. puresound/nnet/loss/memory.py (new): ForegroundWriteGateLoss (masked BCE with the real-near-row exception) and ForegroundMemoryContrastLoss (InfoNCE, same-row far frames as hard negatives), both declaring required_inputs.
- 5. puresound/nnet/loss/stft_loss.py: add OnsetWeightedOverSuppressionLoss beside OverSuppressionLoss (lines 230-259), with the STFT-hop-128 -> VAD-hop-160 index map; leave OverSuppressionLoss itself untouched.
- 6. puresound/system/siso.py: one entry in _loss_providers ('fg_memory' -> side('last_fg_memory')) plus the docstring line (siso.py:317-357).
- 7. puresound/config/augmentation.py: OverlapControlConfig gains session_prob, session_min_row_seconds, session_gap_seconds_short, session_gap_seconds_long, bystander_first_prob, reentry_seconds (extra='forbid' means every key must be declared).
- 8. puresound/task/overlap_gating.py: _session_script + its dispatch in apply(), with the >=0.8 s near-active assertion and the _bernoulli fallback; keep the RNG-order contract (every new draw is documented as changing what a seeded recipe produces).
- 9. puresound/task/ns.py: emit keep_weight on the VAD frame grid in __getitem__ (near the vad_target block, ns.py:600-645), and force absolute_floor on session rows via the noise stage call.
- 10. puresound/task/voice_isolation.py: append session_row / reentry_onset_frame / gap_seconds to VOICE_ISOLATION_SCALAR_KEYS and emit them in _build_voice_isolation_metadata.
- 11. puresound/streaming/dpcrn.py + egs/voice_isolate/scripts/streaming_onnx.py: memory state ports (three tensors) and the FiLM hook in _forward_feature_frame; write_head registered as an ordinary head so it keeps its own triple.
- 12. egs/voice_isolate/scripts/check_training_data.py: the temporal columns (onset, interferer-before-onset, longest target-free gap, re-entry rate, session-row share, target-absent provenance).
- 13. egs/voice_isolate/config/exp/train_dpcrn_v19_fgmem.yaml: v16 verbatim + foreground_memory block + session_* keys + the three new loss entries + OverSuppressionLoss 3.0 -> 2.0.
- 14. test/: unit tests for the scan (parallel path == a reference Python loop to 1e-5 in fp32), for silence being an exact no-op on the state, for step()==forward() frame by frame, for zero-init FiLM being bit-identical to no conditioning, and for the session script's label/mixture envelope invariant.
- 15. Preflight then train: scripts/preflight_ckpt_recipe.py against v16 ep19 (missing-key list must be exactly the eight new tensors), scripts/check_training_data.py for the row shares, then the 20-epoch warm-started cycle.

**48 h kill experiment:** Three tests, no training, all inside 48 h; any one of them kills the proposal. The load-bearing assumption is that the frozen v16 bottleneck carries a talker direction separable from the capture chain AND that a relative write rule can put the USER in the slot on real recordings. E1 (CPU, hours, zero model runs): implement the exact write rule in numpy and replay it over the existing per-frame cache at scratchpad/agcache/{v8,v16}/ - `feat` is the frequency-pooled bottleneck [128, T] at 100 fps for every field clip x context condition and every Dawn utterance x {none, background}. Supply nu from a device-fitted frame-level near/far logistic probe (chain_readability fitted exactly this and it reads QVF at 0.789), then measure (a) write-gate AUC over keep vs suppress spans in the ANCHORED conditions (near_speech dt, stream) where the deletion is measured, per recording group; (b) relative (nu - theta) vs absolute sigmoid(nu); (c) template persistence, cos(m at re-entry, m built from the user's earlier speech) after a simulated 5-10 s gap. KILL if the relative form's pooled anchored AUC is < 0.65 and it is < 0.60 on >= 3 of the 4 QVF groups - the gate cannot be built on this representation and no amount of training the mask around it will help. E2 (~4 GPU-hours): forward v16 ep19 over ~200 segment pairs from the DiPCo / AMI / NOTSOFAR paired corpora already on disk - the only material with two talkers per session AND the same talker across channels, i.e. the confound the agcache cannot resolve (one talker per Dawn recording). Fit nothing; compute Delta_talker = cos(same talker, different channel) - cos(different talker, same channel) on the pooled bottleneck. KILL if Delta_talker <= 0: the slot would enrol the chain, which is precisely how real-e2e round-1 failed, and the design would need a channel-invariance pretext before any of this is worth training. E3 (CPU, ~1 h, dataset code only, no model): build 50 session rows with the new _session_script offline and score v16 ep19 on them with the anchor probe's measure. KILL if the re-entry Δon1 is > -1.0 dB - a manufactured row that shows no deletion gives L_keep_on nothing to push on, which is onset_profile's central lesson, and the row must be redesigned (longer gap, bystander closer) before any GPU is committed.


## v19-A DECIDE: frame-level decision margin on the achieved foreground gain

_Angle:_ Make the foreground keep/suppress decision a first-class training target: supervise the ACHIEVED per-frame gain on the user's own signal (the foreground-projection gain) with within-row relative hinges - onset parity, per-talker keep/suppress margin, and no-anchor-inheritance - so onset and wrong-anchor deletions are penalised directly instead of being a by-product of waveform losses. Presence supervision is coupled to the mask through the gain the mask actually achieves, not through a new head.

Today nothing in the objective says "this frame belongs to the user, keep it, and keep it at least M dB better than you keep the person who was talking a second ago." Every one of the seven v16 loss terms is a mean over a whole row or a whole spectrogram (measured: SDR is a whole-row ratio, MR-STFT / OverSuppression / ResidualReference are torch.mean, DistHead is a global pool, and the only temporal weighting - ASRFeatureLoss's random 6 s crop - covers t=0 with probability 0.000), so a model that deletes the first second of a new talker and then recovers pays almost nothing. v19-A adds one new loss that scores the decision itself. For every frame it computes the least-squares gain the output applied to the user's own reference, P_t = 10log10((<e_t,r_t>/<r_t,r_t>)^2) in dB, and the same quantity against each other talker's reference, Q_k,t. This is exactly the foreground-projection measure the onset diagnosis names as the gate metric, and I verified on CPU that it separates the two things span energy conflates: removing the interferer while leaving the user intact reads -0.00 dB on P (span energy reads -6.41 dB), while attenuating the user 6 dB but keeping the interferer reads -6.12 dB (span energy reads -0.77 dB). Three one-sided hinges then constrain P and Q RELATIVELY, within each row, with no absolute dB threshold anywhere: (A) the first 0.5 s after any keep onset must be treated no worse than that row's own steady-state keep, minus 1 dB slack; (B) the row's keep score must beat every individual other talker's leak score by at least a margin M_B; (C) where a bystander held the floor before the user entered, the user's first 0.5 s must beat the treatment that bystander got by M_C - which is the wrong-anchor error stated as an inequality. Because all three are hinges, they are identically zero on rows the model already decides correctly (measured: 66.2% of current training rows can carry term B today), so the gradient lands only on the failing rows and the existing suppression floors are untouched - the opposite of v18, which capped a floor and made every suppression number shallower. No new parameters, no new head: the supervision goes on the actuator, because the record says the presence SIGNAL is learnable while the presence READOUT is what has failed three times.

**Objective**

FRAME GRID. frame_length 400, hop 160 - identical to the EnergyVADLabeler that produces vad_target (puresound/audio/vad.py:46-79). Measured with the v16 recipe's own ConvEncDec (fft 512 / hop 160): encoder frames vs vad frames are 297/298 at 3 s, 597/598 at 6 s, 1197/1198 at 12 s - left-aligned, same hop, so labels are 1:1 with mask frames after truncation to min(T). No resampling.

INPUTS (all already reachable through the provider table at puresound/system/siso.py:344-357, or added by D5):
  e = enhanced [N,T]; r = target = clean_speech [N,T] (post-device-chain);
  u_k = per-interferer reference [N,K,T] (new batch key far_targets; fallback K=1 with u = batch["consistency_noise"] = noisy-clean);
  v in {0,1}^[N,F] = vad_target (user active); b_k in {0,1}^[N,K,F] = far_vad_targets (fallback: background_vad_target).

PER-FRAME SCORES.
  frame(x) = x.unfold(-1, 400, 160)                       # [N,F,400]
  E,R,U_k = frame(e), frame(r), frame(u_k)
  a_t   = (E_t . R_t) / clamp_min(R_t . R_t, 1e-10)        # LS gain of the user in the output
  c_k,t = (E_t . U_k,t) / clamp_min(U_k,t . U_k,t, 1e-10)  # LS gain of talker k in the output
  pos(z) = softplus(K*z)/K, K = 16                         # smooth positive part
  P_t   = clamp_max(10*log10(pos(a_t)^2   + 1e-12), 0.0)   # user-keep score, dB, <= 0
  Q_k,t = clamp_max(10*log10(pos(c_k,t)^2 + 1e-12), 0.0)   # talker-k leak score, dB, <= 0
Measured envelope of this form: K=16 gives an effective floor of -27.27 dB and max |dP/da| = 100.2 (K=8: -21.25 / 50.1; K=32: -33.29 / 200.5). softplus rather than clamp_min(0) on purpose - a hard clamp leaves fully deleted frames with exactly zero gradient, which is the case the loss exists for. clamp_max(...,0) means leakage above unity gain earns no credit.
Measured behaviour of P (CPU, synthetic, proj_numerics.py): perfect extraction +0.00 dB; pass-through -0.06; enh=0 at the floor; user at -6 dB -> -6.02; user at -20 dB -> -20.00; interferer removed with user intact -0.00 (span energy -6.41); interferer kept with user at -6 dB -> -6.12 (span energy -0.77); phase-inverted user at the floor.

FRAME WEIGHTS AND VALIDITY. w_t = (R_t . R_t) restricted to v_t = 1 and (R_t . R_t) > 1e-4 * max_t(R_t . R_t) (energy weighting inside the activity label). Optional strict variant: also require the frame within 25 dB of the row's peak frame - needed because the data audit measured real-near targets carrying an 18 dB higher noise floor (5th-pct frame -38.9 dB vs -56.5 dB synthetic) and an inflated active fraction (0.98 vs 0.80). Analogous weights w^k_t = (U_k,t . U_k,t) for Q.

ONSETS (label-only, no audio). O = { t : v_t = 1 and v_{t-G..t-1} = 0 }, G = 100 frames (1 s); t=0 included when v_0 = 1. Window W = 50 frames (0.5 s) - the same window the Dawn onset number is quoted at.

TERM A - ONSET PARITY (relative, within-row):
  P_on(o)  = sum_{t in [o,o+W)} w_t P_t / sum w_t
  P_ref(o) = detach( sum_{t >= o+W, v_t=1, valid} w_t P_t / sum w_t )
  L_A = mean over onsets of relu( P_ref(o) - P_on(o) - tau_A ),  tau_A = 1.0 dB
  P_ref is stop-gradient so the term is a parity constraint and can never be satisfied by making the steady state worse. When a row has fewer than 10 valid post-window keep frames, P_ref falls back to the batch's detached mean over valid keep frames; when the batch has no onsets at all the term returns enhanced.sum()*0 (a graph-carrying zero, so DDP never sees an unused path).
  L_A is the Dawn "onset excess" metric with the sign flipped and a 1 dB slack: the training objective and the gate metric are the same function.

TERM B - PER-TALKER DECISION MARGIN (the angle's core; relative, within-row):
  Kn   = { t : v_t = 1, valid };  S_k = { t : b_k,t = 1 and v_t = 0, valid }
  Pbar   = sum_{Kn} w P / sum w
  Qbar_k = sum_{S_k} w^k Q_k / sum w^k     (only talkers with |S_k| >= 10 frames)
  L_B = mean over qualifying k of relu( M_B - (Pbar - Qbar_k) ),  M_B = 15 dB
  Only the difference appears - no absolute dB target, which is the property that survived chain and checkpoint drift twice where absolute thresholds died twice. The hinge saturates: once a row's contrast reaches M_B the term is silent, so it cannot deepen suppression without bound and it does not touch the inactive-SDR floor that v18 proved is load-bearing.

TERM C - NO ANCHOR INHERITANCE (the wrong-anchor error as an inequality):
  For the first keep onset o preceded by a far-only run [s,o) of >= 50 frames, let k* be the talker with the most active frames in that run:
  Qbar_prev = detach( sum_{t in [s,o)} w^{k*}_t Q_{k*,t} / sum w^{k*}_t )
  L_C = relu( M_C - (P_on(o) - Qbar_prev) ),  M_C = 10 dB
  Detached on the previous talker's side so the term cannot be satisfied by suppressing the bystander less.

TOTAL: L_dec = 1.0*L_A + 0.5*L_B + 0.25*L_C, added to the seven v16 terms (total weight 7.0) as one config entry. Row-level skip: terms B and C are skipped on rows whose chain flags volume_clipped or codec_applied fired, because the per-talker anti-signal is only recoverable through a linear chain.

WHY THE GAIN AND NOT A HEAD. b-detector-works-blend-actuator-cannot, presence-readout-confounded-with-reverb and v11b_VERDICT all say the same thing: a presence signal is learnable (cross-recording 0.958/0.969, held-out 180D 0.843) while the readout and actuator are where it dies (gate@0.50 pushed KEEP violations 6 -> 65; the QVF distance readout is inverted at 0.258-0.265 on three checkpoints and 12/12 groups). Supervising the achieved gain puts the target on the actuator. Consequence: zero new parameters, the v16 ep19 warm start is exact, and inference plus the ONNX streaming export are byte-identical.

**Data changes**

- MEASURED ELIGIBILITY FIRST (my computation over the audit's own 1206 rows, v19_plan/training_data_audit/rows.jsonl): Term B is already eligible on 798/1206 = 66.2% of rows (57.7 / 74.7 / 75.2 / 69.6 % on the 3/6/12/30 s buckets), so the MARGIN half of this objective needs no new data at all. Term A (a keep onset after >=1 s of user silence) is eligible on 229/1206 = 19.0% (8.0 / 22.3 / 42.6 / 65.2 %) and Term C (>=1 s of bystander speech before the user's onset) on only 95/1206 = 7.9% (5.6 / 9.4 / 12.9 / 4.3 %). The data changes below are aimed only at A and C. Caveat: interferer-only frames are estimated as itf_active - overlap_fraction*target_active from the audit's logged fractions, not a frame intersection; a direct pass is ~1 CPU-hour and was not run.
- D1 (config only, no code): overlap_control.turn_taking_prob 0.0 -> 0.35 for SYNTHETIC rows, far_first_prob 0.5 -> 0.6, turn_gap_seconds (0.0,0.4) -> (0.0,3.0). The generator already exists (puresound/task/overlap_gating.py:189-226 _turn_script) and today fires on only 11.7% of rows via the realfar 0.30 / realnear 0.50 per-row overrides (voice_isolation.py:336-344). Target stated in ROWS, not batches (the audit measured the configured 0.25 batch share for 3 s arriving as 49.8% of rows): Term C eligibility >= 30% of rows on the 6/12/30 s buckets.
- D2 (bug fix, load-bearing): the audit measured ~1% of 3 s rows where the far-first turn gates the near talker to exact zero while the metadata still says target_present=1.0 (overlap_gating.py:166-187 vs voice_isolation.py:546-557), i.e. a keep row silently becomes a lone-far row with the wrong label. Raising turn_taking_prob multiplies that bug. Add min_near_active_seconds = 0.8 to _turn_script (resample the script up to 5 times, then fall back to Bernoulli gating), and derive the target_absent scalar from the realised waveform the way siso.py:305 already derives the loss's inactive mask, so the two can never disagree.
- D3 (new clause): re-entry rows. overlap_control.reentry_prob = 0.25, restricted to rows >= 12 s - one interior target-free gap of 3-10 s, then the user returns. Today the longest interior target-free gap is median 0.53 s / p90 1.81 s, >=5 s on 4/1206 rows and 0.000 on the 3 s bucket, so this shape simply does not exist. The gap is filled by the row's own recorded-noise / room branch and NEVER by digital silence: measured, a silence prefix is a large suppress bias (lone-interferer arm -5.90 dB on v8, -19.22 dB on v16; keep side -2.33 dB on v16) and it is the one synthetic prefix that already reproduces Dawn's deletion tail (sil1 p5 -70.2 vs Dawn -75.6). Silence stays a probe, not a filler. The user's VAD label is genuinely 0 across the gap - no label hack; the persistence being taught is the DECISION, through Term A applied to the post-gap onset.
- D4 (reuse an existing knob): augmentation_row_initial_ambient at prob 0.25, lead 0.5-3.0 s (ns.py:545-568), as the carrier for Term A's cold onset. v17 judged this knob negative as a suppression fix on its own (far none -1.71 -> -1.48, p=0.15; and the v17 block made cold-far `none` worse, p=0.020); I claim nothing for it alone - it exists here only so the onset-parity hinge has cold rows to score. Class-blind, as it already is.
- D5 (per-talker references, the only real plumbing): the per-interferer gated waveforms exist as a list at ns.py:400 immediately before `torch.cat(...).sum(dim=0)`. Keep the list; recover the single scalar gain the mixing step applied to the sum (add_bg_noise scales the summed interferer by one gain, so g = ||background_speech_reference|| / ||sum u_k||); emit far_targets [K,T] and far_vad_targets [K,F] (per-member create_vad_target); collate pads to K_max with zeros plus a far_mask (mirror the existing far_target block at voice_isolation.py:585-591). Honesty: far_target and background_vad_reference are already pre-device-chain and so is this; since the analogue path is linear (9c56e02), the post-chain sum is recoverable as noisy-clean but the per-talker split is not, so each u_k is scaled by one detached row-level chain gain <x-r, sum u>/<sum u, sum u>. That is an approximation, and it is why rows with volume_clipped or codec_applied set are skipped by terms B and C.
- D6 (no change, but stated): the 3 s bucket carries 49.8% of rows and Term A/C eligibility there is 8.0% / 5.6%; D1/D3 are therefore scoped to the 6/12/30 s buckets and the round's headline row shares are reported per bucket. Nothing new goes on the 3 s bucket, where a 2.0-4.5 s far turn cannot coexist with a near turn at all.

**Architecture changes**

- NONE. Zero new parameters, zero new heads, no change to the DPCRN forward, the mask, the encoder or the inference path. `scripts/preflight_ckpt_recipe.py` must report the v16 ep19 checkpoint loading WHOLE with zero missing and zero unexpected keys - that is the gate on 'the warm start is exact', and it is also what keeps `scripts/streaming_onnx.py` byte-identical.
- Deliberately NOT a presence head. The record is three-for-three against the head route: v11's presence heads fixed the reverb confusion but were never wired to the output and the QVF chain still inverted (scenario3 0.253); the b-detector works but the blend actuator provably cannot move cold start (median gain 0.03 dB); the b gate at 0.50 deleted the user (KEEP violations 6 -> 65, four gates FAIL). The actuator that does move things is a gain, so the supervision goes on the gain.
- One non-parametric addition, training-side only: `on_validation_epoch_end` in puresound/system/siso.py (validation_step logs losses only today) logs L_A / L_B / L_C plus the aggregate onset_excess_db over a fixed decision-row validation set, so the in-loop signal and the release gate are literally the same function and a run can be watched without touching the field set (which the FIELD_BLOCK protocol forbids as an in-loop signal anyway).

**Expected effects**

- The manufactured synthetic carriers (D1-D4) do not reproduce the failure the model commits on real recordings, so the loss trains to zero on rows the model never gets wrong in the field. The measured real/synthetic ratio is 11-23x on the median and 3.9-7.9x on the p5 tail; nothing in this proposal proves that gap closes, and this is the same wall that closed the RIR axis, killed gate-only transfer and turned v17 negative.: Dawn Chorus onset excess @0.5 s (foreground-projection), condition none, n=450: v16 ep19 -3.90 dB -> >= -1.5 dB, guard OFF. — _Term A is that exact statistic with the sign flipped and a 1 dB slack, so the loss and the metric are the same function; and the behaviour is demonstrably reachable at inference on this very checkpoint - the OnsetGuard pna clause alone takes Dawn deletion 0.207 -> 0.111 and keep violations 20/148 -> 7 on v16 without touching a weight._
- The model satisfies Term A by passing the onset through instead of by deciding correctly - which is measured to be an available shortcut (pass-through scores -0.06 dB on P). It would then have learned the guard rather than the decision, buying keep-side deletion with onset-window leakage. The pre-registered onset_leak_db guard exists precisely to catch this.: Dawn ASR deletion, Azure, guard OFF: 0.207 -> <= 0.12, i.e. the model reaches unaided roughly what the guard reaches by hand (0.111), without the guard's suppression price. — _anchor_gate_README sections 2-3, 6: the deletion the guard removes is onset plus wrong-anchor deletion, ~0.13 absolute, all arms p < 1e-26 paired. Terms A and C penalise exactly those frames, and the guard shows the frames are recoverable._
- The synthetic probe improves and Dawn does not - the two live on different chains, and cross-chain non-transfer is this repo's most-reproduced result (QVF distance AUC inverted at 0.258-0.265 on three checkpoints, 12/12 recording groups below 0.20; the real-e2e round-1 zero cross-corpus transfer). A synthetic-only win is a null result, not a partial one.: Synthetic wrong-anchor deletion (anchor_synthetic_probe.py, utt arm, other3, Delta_on1): v16 -1.33 dB -> |Delta| <= 0.5 dB, with the self3 control still null (v16 +0.04). — _The failure is in-distribution and paired-significant on 200 utterances (p ~ 2.8e-24, 173/200 rows down), so there is gradient to shape today; Term C is that contrast written as a hinge, with the previous talker's treatment detached so it cannot be gamed by suppressing the bystander less._
- Term B's contrast is satisfiable from the wrong side: lifting Pbar (more leakage on keep frames) instead of lowering Qbar_k. That would read as flat suppression with worse leakage, visible in the s2f keep curve and in the synthetic far-only stages, and it is why the s2f curve is a hard gate rather than a note.: Far suppression does NOT pay the guard's price: sessions block median stays within 1.5 dB of the v16 ep19 block (-9.73 +/- 1.41), against the guard's cost of -15.2 -> -11.0 dB on the fit set and -22.3 -> -10.0 dB on held-out 180d. — _All three terms are one-sided hinges that saturate on a CONTRAST, so they add no pressure once a row decides correctly (measured: 66.2% of current rows can already carry Term B), and the inactive-SDR 'kill to the floor' term is untouched - the opposite of v18, where relaxing that floor (inactive_mode=relative) made every suppression number shallower and cold-far +0.53 dB, p=0.017._
- If v16's median row contrast is BELOW 15 dB, L_B becomes a broad new pressure on the whole distribution rather than a targeted one, and this expectation is simply false - the objective would then be a disguised re-weighting of everything, the v18 pattern. This is the single cheapest thing to check and it is the second kill condition of the 6-hour preflight.: In-domain SI-SDRi and the moderate WER set stay flat (CI upper bound <= +0.005 dWER vs v16). — _The hinge form makes the term identically zero on rows already inside the margin; on the measured 66.2% Term B-eligible population the question is only whether v16's typical row contrast already exceeds M_B, and where it does the row contributes no gradient at all._
- Violations halve on the device chain and the QVF clips do not move. Nothing in this proposal addresses the chain wall - the QVF distance readout is inverted (0.258 v11b / 0.255 v16 / 0.265 v8; paired bootstrap v11b-v8 = -0.006, p=0.893) and the QVF scenario clips are measured to be context-insensitive, so a chain-side blocker stays a blocker.: The shipped OnsetGuard becomes unnecessary: guard-OFF field keep-near violations <= 3 with worst >= -6 dB, against the guarded arms' 13/148 (v8) and 7/148 (v16). — _anchor_gate_README section 2 prices the guard's effect with no distance readout, no threshold and no checkpoint dependence; if the same decision is trained in, the guard's dry blend has nothing left to restore._

**Cost:** Anchored on a measured epoch time: v16's checkpoint mtimes give 38.07 min/epoch on 2 GPUs (ep18 20:18:29 -> ep19 20:56:33) = 1.27 GPU-h/epoch at 1000 iters, accumulate 2, the four-bucket length schedule.

GPU: one 20-epoch cosine cycle warm-started from v16 ep19 (T_0=20; judge only at the ep19 trough, per the recipe's own note) = 25.4 GPU-h ~ 1.06 GPU-days. New-loss compute is two unfolds and two inner products per row per talker on tensors already resident; estimated < 2% overhead, NOT measured. Preflight scoring pass ~2 GPU-h. 48 h killer probe ~6 GPU-h train + ~4 GPU-h eval. Full gate (run_full_benchmark.sh, 9 stages) plus the 5-checkpoint field block (stage 1b via BLOCK_CKPTS) plus Azure WER on the four sets: ~10 GPU-h, estimated from the script's structure, not timed. TOTAL ~ 1.9 GPU-days.

Engineering: ~4 days. decision.py plus unit tests 1 d; per-talker emission and the two _turn_script clauses plus the target_absent labelling fix 1.5 d; validation hook and probe wiring 0.5 d; gate run and write-up 1 d.

WARM START (non-negotiable): resume from /work/any_exp_link/puresound_exp/dpcrn_v16_lengthmix/lightning_logs/version_0/checkpoints/epoch=19-step=10000.ckpt. The eight-stage curriculum ladder is load-bearing - the last-stage recipe trained from scratch reaches only -1.65 dB real far suppression against v8's -9.83 - so a fresh run is forbidden. Because there are no parameter-shape changes, preflight_ckpt_recipe.py must report a whole load with zero missing/unexpected keys across infer_dpcrn.yaml and every eval recipe; if it does not, the round stops before training. The new run gets its own work_folder (exp/dpcrn_v19a_decision) and its own config under config/exp per the release-layout rule; config/ keeps only the default.

**Risks**

- THE BIG ONE: the onset failure is not in the synthetic distribution. Measured real/synthetic ratios are 11-23x on the median and 3.9-7.9x on the p5 tail, on two independent checkpoints and two independent measures, and on the current sets the synthetic onset is treated slightly BETTER than the steady state (+0.30 / +0.21 dB on indomain). A loss cannot create gradient where the failure is absent, so data clauses D1-D4 are load-bearing and unproven. If they do not reproduce the failure for the model, the round yields a checkpoint that aces the synthetic decision probe and moves Dawn zero.
- Pass-through gaming of Term A. Measured: pass-through scores -0.06 dB on P, so handing back the mixture satisfies onset parity. Guarded by Term B's contrast (pass-through collapses Qbar_k too) and by the pre-registered onset_leak_db metric; but the residual risk is a model that learns the OnsetGuard's trade rather than the decision.
- Coupled clocks. reference_matrix section 6.2 and the synthetic probe agree that anchor memory and the wrong-anchor window share one time constant (decay -8.23/-4.00/-2.81/-0.89 dB at 2/5/10/20 s; wrong-anchor deletion closes by 20 s, p=0.24). Term C attacks inheritance rather than lengthening memory, but a model can satisfy C by forgetting faster, which shortens the anchor and shallows session suppression. Guard = the sessions block median.
- The per-talker anti-signal is an approximation: far_targets is pre-device-chain and split by one detached row-level gain. On rows where a nonlinearity fired (the _volume overload clause, codec, packet loss) the split is wrong; those rows are skipped by terms B and C using the chain_applied flags, which shrinks the eligible population by an amount I have not measured.
- Label quality on real-near rows. The audit measured their targets carrying an 18 dB higher noise floor (5th-pct frame -38.9 vs -56.5 dB) and an inflated active fraction (0.98 vs 0.80), so on those rows P is partly computed over room tone. Mitigated by the energy weighting plus the optional 25 dB strict mask; the diagnosis's own strict-mask control moved Dawn only from -3.85 to -2.87 dB, so the phenomenon survives the stricter mask, but the training-side effect of the mask choice is untested.
- The leak side (Q) still bundles reverb tail with speech, so span-energy conflation is only solved for the keep side. Any keep-violation claim from this round must pass the harmonic peak-valley check (scripts/audit_span_metric.py) before it is written down - user listening has already caught 'keep violations' that were noise, not speech.
- No verdict is possible before the new run's ep19: FIELD_BLOCK requires 5-checkpoint blocks (same-run ep8-19 cold-far median swings -0.35..-3.39 dB, single clips up to 19.9 dB), and choosing the epoch by field score is forbidden.
- This axis does nothing for the QVF chain. The distance readout there is inverted on all three checkpoints (0.255-0.265) and on 12/12 recording groups, the compressor stage is measurably inert on that readout (|dAUC| <= 0.013), and the QVF scenario clips are context-insensitive. If a QVF keep clip is the deployment blocker, v19-A will not clear it.
- Raising turn_taking_prob interacts with the target-gap mass: the audit found turn-taking rows already carry most of the long target gaps, so D1 and D3 move the same population and the round's two shapes are not independent. Report row shares per bucket per shape, not aggregated.

**Go / no-go**

- PRIMARY. Dawn Chorus onset excess @0.5 s, foreground-projection, condition none, n=450 (v16 -3.90 dB) — onset_profile/onset_real.py over an agcache built by benchmarks/probes/anchor_gate_cache.py (dawn), 5-checkpoint block, guard OFF, dry_blend 1.0; paired Wilcoxon vs the v16 ep19 block — pass: >= -1.5 dB with p < 0.01 — kill: <= -3.0 dB (less than a quarter of the gap closed), or p > 0.05
- PRIMARY. Dawn ASR deletion, guard OFF (v16 0.207 guard-off / 0.111 guard-on) — scripts/eval_wer.py with Azure, benchmarks/wer_sets protocol, deltas vs mix (the 10 s cap inflates absolutes), guard OFF — pass: <= 0.12 — kill: > 0.18
- Field keep-near violations and worst keep clip (v16 ep19 block: 3 violations / -8.34 dB) — scripts/eval_field_block.py (whole set v3), block means with per-checkpoint spread, guard OFF — pass: <= 3 violations and worst >= -6 dB — kill: >= 5 violations, or any keep clip worse than -10 dB
- Sessions suppression block median (v16 ep19 block -9.73 +/- 1.41) — scripts/eval_field_block.py compare, per-clip paired Wilcoxon plus the >=1 dB severity table tagged by chain — pass: >= -8.2 dB (within 1.5 dB of v16) — kill: shallower than -6.7 dB (more than 3 dB lost), or any chain-selective damage in the severity table
- Synthetic wrong-anchor Delta_on1, other3 (v16 -1.33 dB) with the self3 null control (v16 +0.04 dB) — benchmarks/probes-side anchor_synthetic_probe.py, utt arm, 200 utterances of wer_set_moderate_test, paired per utterance — pass: |Delta_on1| <= 0.5 dB and self3 within +/- 0.1 dB of null — kill: worse than -1.0 dB, or self3 moves beyond -0.3 dB (the control breaking means the model lost same-talker protection)
- moderate-reverb WER set, paired dWER vs v16 — scripts/eval_wer.py on data_report/wer_set_moderate_test with the paired bootstrap CI (the primary WER gate since but-office-lacks-resolution) — pass: CI upper bound <= +0.005 — kill: CI lower bound > +0.01
- NEW GUARD. onset_leak_db - interferer leak score inside the onset window — the same decision-loss scoring script used in the preflight, run on the moderate set (Q on onset-window frames where an interferer is simultaneously active, energy-weighted) — pass: within +2 dB of v16 — kill: > +4 dB (the model bought onset parity with leakage - it learned the guard, not the decision)
- s2f keep curve (v16 ep19 baseline -0.25 / -0.31 / -0.38 / -0.55 / -1.02) — scripts/eval_s2f_keep_probe.py (native/20/15/12/8 dB added noise), judged against the curve not a count — pass: no point more than 1 dB below the baseline curve — kill: any point more than 2 dB worse, or a new cliff on a clip that had none
- GUARDRAILS. Synthetic far-only suppression (stages 2/4), extreme-reverb WER (stage 8, v8's known hole), and the harmonic peak-valley attribution on moved keep clips — run_full_benchmark.sh stages 2-4 and 8, plus scripts/audit_span_metric.py harmonic peak-valley on every keep clip that moved >= 1 dB — pass: synthetic far-only within 3 dB of v16; extreme-reverb dWER CI not above zero; no keep clip's degradation attributable to speech removal — kill: extreme-reverb dWER CI lower bound > 0, or any keep clip whose peak-valley test says speech (not noise/reverb) was removed

**Implementation**

- 1. puresound/nnet/loss/decision.py (NEW): class DecisionMarginLoss(nn.Module) with required_inputs = ('enhanced','target','batch','vad_target'); the framing helper mirrors VADActivityLoss._frame_power (puresound/nnet/loss/vad.py:48-58); args = frame_length 400, hop 160, softplus_k 16.0, onset_gap_frames 100, onset_window_frames 50, tau_a 1.0, margin_b 15.0, margin_c 10.0, w_a/w_b/w_c, strict_mask_db 25.0, skip_nonlinear_rows True, far_key 'far_targets' with fallback 'consistency_noise'.
- 2. puresound/nnet/loss/__init__.py: import DecisionMarginLoss and add it to __all__ - that list is what recipes.py / the egs main resolve loss_func[].type against, so a loss missing from it is unreachable from a config.
- 3. test/test_nnet/test_loss_decision.py (NEW): the eight-row CPU table I measured as assertions (perfect +0.00, pass-through -0.06, enh=0 at the -27.27 dB floor, -6 dB -> -6.02, -20 dB -> -20.00, interferer-removed ~ -0.00, phase-inverted at the floor), plus zero loss on a satisfied row, positive loss on a deleted onset, gradient flow through a<=0, a graph-carrying zero on a row with no onsets, and the provider-table contract (required_inputs subset of siso.py:344-357, which the existing shipped-loss test already reads from that method).
- 4. puresound/task/ns.py: at line 400, keep the per-interferer list before `torch.cat(...).sum(dim=0)`; emit far_targets (each member scaled by the recovered mixing gain) and far_vad_targets via create_vad_target; derive the target_absent scalar from the realised waveform so it matches siso.py:305's inactive mask (the audit's 1%-mislabelled-row bug).
- 5. puresound/task/voice_isolation.py: extend VoiceIsolationCollateFunc (mirror the far_target block at :585-591) to pad far_targets / far_vad_targets to K_max and emit far_mask.
- 6. puresound/task/overlap_gating.py: _turn_script (:189-226) gains min_near_active_seconds resampling (5 tries, then fall back to _bernoulli) and the re-entry clause (one interior target-free gap of 3-10 s on rows >= 12 s).
- 7. puresound/config/augmentation.py: OverlapControlConfig (:45-59) gains min_near_active_seconds, reentry_prob, reentry_gap_seconds, reentry_min_row_seconds - StrictConfig, so a typo in the recipe fails loudly.
- 8. puresound/system/siso.py: on_validation_epoch_end logging L_A / L_B / L_C and onset_excess_db over a fixed decision-row validation set (validation_step logs losses only today).
- 9. egs/voice_isolate/config/exp/train_dpcrn_v19a_decision.yaml (NEW): the v16 recipe verbatim plus one DecisionMarginLoss entry and the D1-D4 knobs; work_folder exp/dpcrn_v19a_decision; max_epochs 40 with the verdict read at the ep19 trough.
- 10. egs/voice_isolate/scripts/check_training_data.py: add the temporal columns (onset, interferer-before-onset, longest target gap, re-entry rate, target-absent provenance, per-talker eligibility) - report_separability at :161-231 measures none of them today, which is why this distribution was never on the record. The audit's sample_rows.py is a working prototype.
- 11. egs/voice_isolate/benchmarks/probes/decision_objective_README.md: the pre-registration (thresholds above, verbatim) committed BEFORE the run starts.

**48 h kill experiment:** Two stages. Stage 0 can kill the proposal for two GPU-hours, before any data code is written.

STAGE 0, hours 0-6 (~2 GPU-h): "does this loss measure the failure?" Implement decision.py in its fallback form only - K=1 with u = batch['consistency_noise'] and b = batch['background_vad_target'], both already in every batch, so NO dataset change is needed - plus a scoring script that reports L_A, L_B, L_C and their component scalars. Score v16 ep19 and v8 on four populations: (a) 200 current v16 training rows drawn through the real dataloader (the audit's sample_rows.py already does this), (b) the 450 Dawn utterances, (c) the same Dawn utterances but with the output produced through the shipped puresound/system/onset_guard.py, (d) the moderate set's t0 / bg1 / sil1 variants from onset_synth.py.
Three kill conditions, all from that one pass:
  K1 - L_A must be materially lower on (c) than on (b). The guard is the KNOWN behavioural fix (Dawn deletion 0.207 -> 0.111, keep violations 20 -> 7 on v16), so a loss that cannot distinguish guarded from unguarded output is not measuring the failure. Require >= 50% reduction in mean L_A; below 20%, stop and redesign the score.
  K2 - L_B must be ~0 on the bulk of (a). If the median current row already violates the 15 dB contrast, L_B is a broad re-weighting of the whole objective rather than a targeted hinge, expected effect 5 is false, and M_B must be re-derived from the measured contrast distribution before any GPU-day is spent.
  K3 - the added terms' gradient norm at init must be < 20% of the total on a real batch. Above 50% this is v18 in reverse - a global re-weighting wearing a decision label - and it must be re-scaled first.

STAGE 1, hours 6-48 (~6 GPU-h train, ~4 h eval): a 3-epoch warm-start probe from v16 ep19 with the full loss and the CONFIG-ONLY data changes (D1, D2, D4; skip D3 and D5, which need code), at a deliberately high decision-row diet - 50% of rows, 6 s and 12 s buckets only - to maximise signal per hour. Then four reads, in order of cost:
  - anchor_synthetic_probe.py utt arm (~10 min): Delta_on1 must move from -1.33 toward zero by >= 0.4 dB, with self3 still null.
  - onset_synth.py sil1/bg1 on the moderate set (~20 min): the p5 tail must lift from -70.2 dB by >= 15 dB.
  - the bgonly arm's `none` and 1 s-anchor rows: lone-interferer suppression must not shallow by more than 4 dB (v16 -6.25 / -9.72).
  - THE KILL: Dawn onset excess @0.5 s must move by >= 1.0 dB from -3.90. If three epochs of a 50%-decision-row diet with the loss demonstrably firing move the synthetic probe but leave Dawn inside +/- 0.5 dB, the transfer premise is dead and the round must not be run. That null is not a "needs more epochs" result - it is the loss working exactly as designed while the phenomenon sits on the other side of the recording chain, which is the same wall that closed the RIR axis, made gate-only adaptation a no-op on real recordings, and turned v17 negative. Reading it at 48 h costs ~10 GPU-hours instead of the round's 1.9 GPU-days.


# Adversarial verdicts


### EVIDENCE & HISTORY — refutation pass: every expected effect checked against the diagnosis numbers, the shipped guard's code, and the recorded verdicts (v11, v11b, v14, v18, v12 axis ranking, selfcal, anchor_gate).

- score 3/10 — **kill**
- what would change my mind: "Three offline results, all obtainable on CPU from the existing agcache in days and none requiring a GPU. (1) That the free option genuinely cannot ship: a device-fitted frozen-feature readout (R0-R4) evaluated in SESSION scope — the condition the gate actually runs in — collapses to well under 0.60 on QVF and under ~0.75 on device sessions, while a per-frame causal construction on the same frozen features is measurably better; that would convert 'a probe already does this' into 'only a trained per-frame head can do this'. (2) A measured chain operator that reproduces the inversion — device-clip distance AUC driven to <= 0.35 by some stage (codec, noise gate, packet loss, mic/room geometry) — so L_ccprox has a causal target rather than an axis the diagnosis measured as inert or merely degrading; without that, v12 axis-1's own rule refuses the knob. (3) An end-to-end replay on the cache showing that swapping in the better readout, behind a session-relative margin, beats the shipped pna guard by >= 0.05 Dawn deletion or >= 5 keep violations at equal far-suppression median — i.e. that a better readout changes the decision at all. With (1), (2) and (3) I would flip this to amend and support the arm-A/ablation pair; with only (1) I would support a much smaller round (aux weight <= 0.1, no nonlinear term, DistHead weight untouched, gated on keep violations and Dawn deletion). Absent all three, the honest verdict is that this spends 3.7 GPU-days and 4.5 engineering days to raise a metric that a logistic regression already reads at 0.827 and that no shipped component consumes."

**Fatal flaws**

- DOMINATED BY A ZERO-GPU OPTION THE DIAGNOSIS ALREADY MEASURED. The round's primary pass bar is 'QVF clip proximity AUC >= 0.50 median, >= 0.75 on target'. A logistic probe fitted on DEVICE recordings only, on the SAME cached pooled bottleneck window the ProxHead would consume, already reads QVF at 0.827 (v16) / 0.789 (v8) (chain_readability finding 6, linear_probe.json). So a *successful* 3.7-GPU-day round lands below what a few CPU-minutes of sklearn on frozen v16 features delivers today, and the proposal's own 48 h probe kill-rule (a) ('beat R0's 0.827 or reach >= 0.90') is inconsistent with its GPU pass bar by >0.3 AUC. Either probe outcome argues for shipping a fitted readout, not for training one. The proposal cites 0.827 only as evidence that its premise is true and never treats it as the competing option it is — the same error v14 was killed for (a free dry_blend knob beat the trained module).
- THE STATED PRIZE IS MISATTRIBUTED. 'The readout the shipped AnchorGate's relative-DRR arm can actually read off-rig, which is the arm that cut Dawn deletion 0.230 -> 0.096' is wrong twice. (i) That arm reads DRR, and QVF DRR on isolated clips is ALREADY 0.799 on v8 (anchor_gate_README §1) — the inverted quantity (distance, 0.265) is the arm that performs WORSE at every frontier point ('DRR beats distance at every frontier point'). (ii) The recorded blocker for drr3 is not readability but that the margin is an absolute dB quantity that does not transfer across checkpoints — v8's 3 dB costs v16 ten suppress passes (37/46 -> 27/46); anchor_gate §2 reading 2 files it under the same failure as presence_selfcal Finding 4. A better proximity head does not touch that. Meanwhile what actually SHIPS (puresound/system/onset_guard.py, the pna clause) needs 'no distance readout, no threshold and no checkpoint dependence' — its own docstring line 16 — so the round buys the shipped guard exactly nothing.
- THE INVARIANCE TERM TRAINS AGAINST A NON-CAUSAL AXIS, WHICH IS v11b's EXACT ERROR. Both v8 (config/train_dpcrn.yaml:423-430) and v16 (config/exp/train_dpcrn_v16_lengthmix.yaml:447-454) ALREADY train the identical linear channel-consistency term at the proposal's own settings (prob 0.3, weight 1.0, eq_db 6, gain_db 4, max_rows 4), and QVF distance AUC is 0.265 / 0.255 — inverted in both. Worse, on a linear view a channel-LayerNorm'd, difference-only readout is invariant to real-positive H(f) plus per-row gain almost by construction, so L_ccprox's only live gradient comes from the NONLINEAR family — and the diagnosis measured that family as either inert on this readout (envelope compressor |dAUC| <= 0.013 at every operating point, comp2_*.json) or merely degrading (|x|^p at p 0.4: 0.989 -> 0.880, 'never inverts'; 'nothing I applied to a device clip reproduced 0.26'). benchmarks/v12_candidate_axes.md:19 sets the repo's own rule: 'only the factor that moves the readout earns a recipe knob'. None of the proposed operators qualifies. Giving weight 1.0 — the largest of the new terms — to invariance against operators that provably do not produce the failure, while DISABLING the existing mask-consistency L1 on those views, is a net removal of a validated regulariser in exchange for an unmotivated one.
- THE AUXILIARY-HEAD TAX IS MEASURED AND THE MITIGATIONS DO NOT COVER IT. v11 (per-frame presence heads, warm-started v8) cost 3-5 dB synthetic far suppression, and its recorded lesson is explicit: 'multi-task pressure pushes the operating point conservative — visible already at weight 0.1' (v11-presence-heads-verdict). v11b cost roughly HALF the anchored far suppression (-12.35/-11.29/-9.25 -> -6.64/-5.95/-6.02). This proposal runs w_prox 0.3 + L_var 0.1 + L_ccprox 1.0, i.e. 1.4 of new loss weight against a 7.0 baseline — an order of magnitude above the weight already shown to bite. Zero-init plus a 2-epoch ramp only guarantees bit-identity at step 0 (v14 proved that, max|diff| 0.000e+00) and says nothing about ep19 after 17 epochs of aux gradient; v14's own verdict is that a woken zero-init branch changed behaviour and was still beaten by a free knob.
- ROUND FOUR OF A FAMILY WITH THREE RECORDED NON-DEPLOYMENTS, AND IT DISCLAIMS THE ONLY THING THAT WOULD BREAK THE PATTERN. v11: in-domain presence readout 0.529 -> 0.953, held-out 180D 0.843 (a far larger readout win than this round targets) — 'the head is attached to no output', not a deployment candidate. v11b: scenario3 head AUC 0.252 -> 0.411, session 0.596 -> 0.726 — not a v8 replacement. v11c: iced. This proposal's own expected effects concede no first-order onset/deletion gain, no suppression gain, and no movement on the QVF suppression wall — i.e. a pass yields a fourth unattached readout. Worse, v11b ep31 showed that a trained head's ABSOLUTE calibration dies on continued training (factory threshold dead from logit drift, bacc 0.74 -> 0.62), so 'the head is only usable behind self-calibration' — yet every gate in this proposal is an absolute AUC, and none tests whether the readout survives the checkpoint drift that killed the last one.
- THE GATES CONTRADICT RECORDED RESOLUTION CAVEATS. The 'robust' primary gate is '3 of 4 QVF recording groups at AUC >= 0.35' over gym / plumbing / price / scenario3 — and v11b_VERDICT says of exactly those: 'gym/plumbing dips are single-clip groups inside the noise band; watch, don't conclude.' The pooled pass/kill boundary (0.50 pass / 0.40 kill) sits inside the paired half-width the diagnosis itself measured (+-0.14, n = 9 keep / 6 suppress clips); a 5-checkpoint block averages CHECKPOINT noise, not the clip-sampling noise that produces that interval, so the pre-registered decision is not resolvable by the pre-registered instrument. And the device-session DRR guard (>= 0.85, 'v11b reached 0.903') leans on a delta the diagnosis flags as confounded with v11a's presence heads.
- SELECTION HYGIENE AND KNOB DISCIPLINE. (a) The in-loop hook scores the frozen 47-clip field list — which includes the 180D held-out orientation — every validation epoch, and the 48 h probe picks the head's normalisation variant by its QVF score; selfcal-relative-threshold's standing rule is that operating points must be chosen under the held-out protocol (90d/270d tune, 180d read once), never iterated on the field sessions. (b) DistHeadRegressionLoss 0.3 -> 0.15 halves the very term credited with the 0.989 device readout, in the same run that adds three new terms, while the config header claims 'the ONE round knob' — a second, untested knob that will make any device-side regression unattributable.

**Evidence conflicts**

- egs/voice_isolate/benchmarks/probes/anchor_gate_README.md §1: QVF isolated-clip DRR is 0.799 (v8) — the arm the proposal invokes as its prize is already readable off-rig; distance (0.265) is the arm measured to lose at every frontier point ('DRR beats distance at every frontier point').
- egs/voice_isolate/benchmarks/probes/anchor_gate_README.md §2 reading 2 and §5: the relative arm's blocker is an absolute dB margin that does not transfer across checkpoints (v8's 3 dB costs v16 ten suppress passes). The proposal fixes a readout, not the margin, so drr3 remains non-transferable after a pass.
- puresound/system/onset_guard.py docstring (line 16) and anchor_gate_README §5: the shipped guard's clause works with 'no distance readout, no threshold and no checkpoint dependence'. The proposal's benefit chain therefore terminates in an arm that is not shipped.
- config/train_dpcrn.yaml:423-430 vs config/exp/train_dpcrn_v16_lengthmix.yaml:447-454: linear channel-consistency (prob 0.3, weight 1.0, eq 6 dB, gain 4 dB, max_rows 4) is ALREADY trained in both v8 and v16, and QVF distance AUC is 0.265 / 0.255 in both. Chain-consistency training at exactly these settings is measured not to produce a chain-invariant readout.
- chain_readability comp2_v8/v16/v11b.json: the envelope compressor moves the readout by |dAUC| <= 0.013; the |x|^p waveshaper degrades (0.989 -> 0.880) but never inverts; 'no modelled stage reproduces the inversion'. The proposal's nonlinear perturbation group is therefore invariance training against operators that do not cause the failure — v11b's recorded mistake ('trained invariance to an axis that was already flat').
- benchmarks/v12_candidate_axes.md:19: 'only the factor that moves the readout earns a recipe knob'. By the repo's own gating rule the waveshaper/expander/compressor group has not earned one.
- v11-presence-heads-verdict (memory) + benchmarks/probes/presence_head_v11_README.md: per-frame aux heads at weight 0.1 already visibly pushed the operating point conservative and cost 3-5 dB synthetic far suppression; this round carries 1.4 of new loss weight.
- benchmarks/probes/v11b_VERDICT.md: a readout-axis round bought +0.15 head AUC and QVF keep healing for ~half the anchored far suppression, and 'gym/plumbing dips are single-clip groups inside the noise band' — the groups this proposal makes its primary gate.
- benchmarks/probes/v11b_VERDICT.md + selfcal-relative-threshold (memory): a trained head's absolute calibration died on continued training (ep19 -> ep31 logit drift, gate suppression to zero); every gate here is an absolute AUC and none tests self-calibrated / session-relative readout, which is the form that survived twice.
- chain_readability finding 6 / linear_probe.json: device-fit -> QVF logistic probe = 0.827 (v16). The proposal's target band (0.50 pass, 0.75 'on target') is below an existing zero-GPU readout on the same frozen features; the round is not compared against it anywhere.
- distance-cue-survives-mask-ignores-it (memory): cold start's missing quantity is presence ('is there a user'), not distance, and DistHead is utterance-level so 'wiring distance up cannot solve it'. The proposal's downstream story is a distance/proximity readout, and it explicitly does not attach the head to any output.
- onset_profile diagnosis: onset deletion is absent from the synthetic median (-0.25 to +0.30 dB fg-projection excess). The proposal is honest about this, but it means the round's only claimed deliverable is a representation metric — the same category the last three rounds delivered without a deployment.

**Amendments**

- Do the CPU work, then SHIP it instead of training it: run readout_surgery.py (hours, no GPU), and whichever of R0-R4 wins, fit it offline on device recordings and wire it into AnchorGate/OnsetGuard as the readout, with a SESSION-RELATIVE margin (the recorded blocker for drr3, and the form that survived chain and checkpoint drift twice). Then measure the deliverable, not the AUC: Dawn deletion, field keep violations, and the suppression price, replayed on the existing agcache with anchor_gate_sim.py. That is a zero-GPU path from 0.827 to a product change, and it must be exhausted before any training round is scheduled.
- If a training round is still wanted, it needs a justification the offline probe cannot supply. The only candidate visible in the numbers is SESSION-scope readability, which collapses on both chains (device sessions 0.757/0.781, QVF sessions 0.357-0.517) and is the deployment condition; a per-frame causal head is a plausible fix there, whereas the whole-clip probe is not. Re-pre-register the round on session-scope readout with a frozen-feature probe as the control arm, and drop the QVF clip-AUC gate whose CI (+-0.14) cannot resolve its own pass/kill boundary.
- Gate on the decision, not the representation. Primary gate = keep violations and Dawn deletion with the OnsetGuard OFF (does the trained model reach the guarded arm's 13/148 and ~0.10 deletion at the ungated suppression band?), exactly as objective_landscape stipulates. A readout AUC may be a monitor; it must not be a round verdict, because three recorded rounds already won that metric and shipped nothing.
- Remove the nonlinear consistency term until an operator is measured that actually reproduces the QVF inversion (device-clip AUC driven to <= 0.35). Until then it is an unmotivated 1.0-weight term that also deletes the existing mask-consistency signal on its own views; and do not extend the linear group, which v8 and v16 both already train with no readout benefit.
- Cap total new auxiliary weight at <= 0.1 (the level v11 showed already biases the operating point), run the L_ccprox-off ablation FIRST rather than second, and pre-register the suppression band read on a 5-checkpoint block with paired Wilcoxon per FIELD_BLOCK.md before the readout number is looked at.
- Keep DistHeadRegressionLoss at 0.3. Halving the term credited with the 0.989 device readout in the same run makes any device-side regression unattributable and breaks the round's own one-knob claim.
- Move the in-loop field hook off the held-out material: score only the fit-set recordings (90d/270d/qvf fit groups) in-loop, keep 180D for a single read at verdict time, and select the head's normalisation variant on device leave-one-group-out rather than on the QVF score it will later be graded on.

### BUILDABILITY & MEASUREMENT — can v19a be implemented in this codebase, at the stated cost, under the curriculum-ladder constraint, and are its gates measurable with existing instruments at resolvable thresholds?

- score 6/10 — **amend**
- what would change my mind: "Two things would move this to keep. First, a demonstrated readout path: a patched `anchor_gate_cache.py` + `anchor_gate_sim.py --readout prox` run end-to-end on the existing v16 cache with a randomly-initialised ProxHead, printing a QVF clip AUC and a device clip AUC through the SAME aggregation as the 0.255/0.989 baselines -- that removes the fatal measurement gap and pins the comparison. Second, a probe result that covers the actual head: the winning R-variant reproduced with a nonlinear (SiLU-MLP) readout reaching cross-chain QVF >= 0.87 with a clip-bootstrap lower bound above R0's 0.827 AND holding >= 0.95 device AUC under the p = 0.4 waveshaper. Add a green 200-step smoke run on the 6 s x 12 bucket with both perturbed views showing peak memory under ~20 GiB on an L4, a passing test for the all-no-background batch, and a re-measured eligible-row share at the >= 25-frame minimum, and I have no buildability objection left. Conversely, I would move to kill if the eligible-row share at a >= 25-frame minimum comes back below ~25% of rows or is concentrated in the 12 s/30 s buckets -- at that point L_rank/L_frame see too few rows per epoch for a 0.3-weighted term to teach anything, and the round would be spending 2.2 GPU-days on a gradient that fires on a small, silence-biased subset."

**Fatal flaws**

- THE PRIMARY INSTRUMENT CANNOT READ THE PROPOSED HEAD, AND FIXING IT IS NOT IN THE PLAN. Every PRIMARY and GUARD readout gate names `anchor_gate_cache.py` -> `anchor_gate_sim.py readability`. I read both: `anchor_gate_sim.load_head` (:69-77) hard-loads `dist_head_net.pt` and indexes `sd["0.weight"]/"0.bias"/"2.weight"/"2.bias"`; `head_apply` (:81-86) is literally `Linear -> SiLU -> Linear`; `sliding_estimates` (:119-140) evaluates that MLP on a causal cumulative mean over ACTIVE frames only. A ProxHead that is LayerNorm -> Conv1x1 -> causal depthwise k=9 -> Conv1x1 over ALL frames cannot be expressed in that path, and `anchor_gate_cache.py` (:62-66, :71-79) dumps only `dist_head.net.state_dict()` plus `feat`/`dist`. The implementation list (items 1-13) modifies neither file. As written, arm A finishes training and there is no way to compute its primary metric.
- MEASURED DDP-KILLING CRASH PATH ON `background_vad_target`. `ProxContrastLoss` declares `background_vad_target` as a required input, but the provider (siso.py:336-345) returns None when the batch lacks the key AND `last_background_vad_logits` is None -- and v16 enables NO vad head at all (train_dpcrn_v16_lengthmix.yaml builds only `dist_head`, :496), so the zeros_like fallback never fires. The collate only emits the key when SOME row has it (voice_isolation.py:593 `has_background_vad = any(...)`). I measured the frequency on the audit's 1206 real v16 rows: 264/1206 = 21.9% of rows carry no background speech, and the 30 s bucket runs at batch size 2 (`n_spk: 2`), so P(all-no-background batch) ~ 0.219^2 = 4.8% of 30 s batches, which are 20% of batches -> roughly 1% of all steps, several per epoch. This is the recorded background-VAD None-target crash that already severed a DDP job once. The proposal's risk list covers the empty-far-frames case but not the None-target case.
