- THE 48 h KILLER PROBE DOES NOT TEST THE PROPOSED HEAD, SO NEITHER OF ITS KILL RULES BINDS. R0-R4 are all linear/logistic readouts; ProxHead is nonlinear (two SiLU conv stages). And the architectural claim is over-stated: affine-free LayerNorm over C removes only the per-frame SCALAR channel mean and scale, not a per-channel offset VECTOR, and `mu_n(s) - mu_f(s)` cancels an offset only when the readout between the features and s is linear. The loss is exactly invariant to `s -> s + b` (trivially, by construction) but that is not the same claim as "a per-chain offset in feature space cannot change the answer". Consequence: probe PASS does not transfer to the head, probe FAIL does not kill it. Kill rule (a) is also unresolvable as written -- "beats R0's cross-chain 0.827" with no margin and no CI, selected as best-of-4 over five candidate readouts, is passable by noise.

**Evidence conflicts**

- The primary robust gate contradicts the very probe that produced its baseline. `per_group_auc.py` pools FRAMES within a group, and the chain_readability caveat that generated the 12/12 numbers says frames inside a clip are strongly correlated so its own leave-one-group-out figures are optimistic. The 4 gateable QVF groups hold 2/1, 1/1, 2/2 and 2/1 keep/suppress CLIPS (counted from benchmarks/field_test_vector/windows.json: gym near1,near2 + far1; plumbing near1 + far1; price near1,near2 + far1,far2; scenario3 near1,near2 + far1). Plumbing's group AUC is one clip against one clip. "A count is far more robust than the pooled AUC at this n" is therefore backwards: the count's effective n is 4 near-binary comparisons.
- The keep-violation gate cites the wrong instrument. "20/148" comes from the ungated (dry_blend 1.0) row of the gate-simulation table in egs/voice_isolate/benchmarks/probes/anchor_gate_README.md:44-54, not from "the set-v3 keep-violation count in the field scorecard". FIELD_BLOCK.md's keep guard is the s2f-swept curve plus per-clip deltas (lines 29, 68-78) and produces no 148-span count.
- "47 frozen field cold-start clips" does not match the set. I counted the existing cache index: agcache/v11b/field_index.jsonl = 499 records over 64 unique clips, 23 of them QVF, with condition `none` = 64 records; windows.json has 64 clips (qvf 23 / 90d 15 / 180d 13 / 270d 12 / 0d 1). 47 is not any count in this set, and the validation hook and its cost claim are sized against it.
- The gate list omits the one column v18 died on. egs/voice_isolate/benchmarks/probes/v18_VERDICT.md and FIELD_BLOCK.md:106 record the v18 kill as cold-far `none` +0.53 dB, p=0.017 from eval_coldstart_v2 blocks -- the cheapest and most sensitive detector of "suppression got shallower", which is exactly this round's declared main risk. eval_coldstart_v2.py exists and takes `--ckpt` repeatedly (blocks). It is named in the standing instrument list and absent from go_no_go.
- Adding `group` / `mask_on_nonlinear` as raw keys in `channel_consistency` repeats a failure this repo documented in writing. `channel_consistency: Optional[dict]` (siso.py:75, :107-109) is read with `cc.get(...)`, and puresound/nnet/lobe/heads.py:1-16 records that exactly this pattern -- `.get(key, default)` answering a misspelled key with the default -- built `vad_head: {enabled: true, hiden: 64}` at the wrong width and trained a whole run that way without a word. That is why every head now owns a StrictConfig.
- The suppression guard's significance clause is near-vacuous at the recorded n. FIELD_BLOCK.md:42,51 gives the v16 ep19 session block as -9.73 +/-1.41 over n=6 sessions, where an observed +2.18 dB delta scored p=0.062. A paired Wilcoxon at n=6 cannot go below p=0.031, so "no significant loss at p<0.05" can be satisfied by a 2 dB shallowing; only the magnitude kill actually binds.

**Amendments**

- Add to the implementation list, BEFORE launch: (a) `anchor_gate_cache.py` stashes a per-frame `prox` array in the npz and dumps `prox_head.state_dict()` -- the precedent already exists, the cache npz keys I read are `['mix','meta','enh','feat','dist','vad_logit']`, so a per-frame head output is already being stashed; (b) `anchor_gate_sim.py` gains a `--readout prox` branch that bypasses `load_head`/`head_apply`/`sliding_estimates` and reads the cached array. Budget 0.5 d. Without this the round is unmeasurable.
- Pre-register ONE aggregation for both sides of the primary comparison. The 0.255 baseline is a window-pooled, active-frames-only, causal-cumulative-mean readout; s0 is a raw per-frame conv output. Score the ProxHead through the same active-frame 1 s windowing (median of s0 over the same windows `sliding_estimates` uses) and report the raw per-frame number separately, or the 0.255 -> 0.50 claim compares two different statistics.
- Handle the None target explicitly: extend siso.py's `background_vad_target` provider to synthesize `torch.zeros_like` from `last_prox_logits` when `last_background_vad_logits` is absent, AND make ProxContrastLoss return a graph-carrying zero on a None/empty target -- the idiom is already in the tree at puresound/nnet/loss/dist.py:70-71 (`return dist_preds.sum() * 0.0`). Add a test with a batch where no row carries background speech.
- Raise the eligibility minimum above the head's receptive field and re-run the pre-flight before launch, not after. 10 frames is 0.1 s against a 9-frame (90 ms) RF, so the far-only contrast frames are RF-contaminated by adjacent near frames. Use >= 25 frames. For calibration I computed an UPPER BOUND on the current rule from the audit's 1206 rows (has_background_speech AND >=10 near frames AND min(near-inactive, interferer-active) >= 10 frames): 781/1206 = 64.8% overall, 57.1% on the 3.0 s bucket which is 41.7% of rows. The true share is lower because overlap_control puts 35% of rows at 0.5-1.0 overlap, so the proposal's own ~50% relax trigger is live -- and relaxing to >= 5 frames does not fix a structural overlap problem; widening `no_overlap_prob` would.
- Make the probe decisive: add a nonlinear variant (small MLP on the same normalized features, matching the head's SiLU-conv depth) to R1-R4, or restrict ProxHead's readout to linear so the probe covers it. Restate kill rule (a) with a margin and a bootstrap-over-clips lower bound (e.g. best variant's cross-chain AUC >= 0.87 with the 95% lower bound above 0.827), since best-of-5 selection on n = 9 keep / 6 suppress clips will beat 0.827 by chance.
- Replace the primary gate's unit with clips: per-clip median readout over the labelled spans, clip-level AUC over the 9 x 6 = 54 QVF clip pairs, bootstrap over clips (the +/-0.14 half-width already measured), 5-checkpoint block median. Keep the 4-group count as a consistency check, not the robust primary. Define the verdict at 0.40-0.50, which is currently an undefined band between kill and pass.
- Add a w_prox = 0 null arm, or name an existing warm-restart control. Both proposed arms carry a new gradient, so neither attributes a GUARD regression to the head rather than to the fresh CosineAnnealingWarmRestarts T_0=20 cycle from v16 ep19 -- and v18 is proof that such a cycle moves cold-far by itself. Cost +1.1 GPU-days -> ~4.8 total.
- Fix the second-forward cost and memory. The existing block (siso.py:389-403) runs ONE perturbed forward at max_rows=4 whose activations stay alive until backward; the proposal runs a linear AND a nonlinear view per firing, doubling that extra set. The '+3-5% wall' figure was computed for one extra forward. On the 2 x L4 23034 MiB box (verified by nvidia-smi) with the claimed 19.2 GiB peak at the 6 s x 12 bucket, that is the OOM edge. Alternate the two groups across firings (one extra forward per step), or use max_rows=2 for the nonlinear view, and measure peak memory on the 6 s and 12 s buckets in the smoke run.
- Call `compressor_gain` per row. puresound/audio/dsp.py:448 does `flat = wav.reshape(-1)` and :473 normalizes by a single `gain.mean()`, so on a [4, T] sub-batch it computes one envelope across the concatenation and one shared makeup gain -- wrong per row. It also runs two `torchaudio.functional.lfilter` passes at 16 kHz (up to 480 k samples on the 30 s bucket); the docstring's 12 ms/6 s is a CPU measurement, so time it on GPU inside training_step in the smoke run.
- Rewrite test item 11's causality assertion. v16 sets `delay: [1, 1, 1]` and puresound/nnet/unet.py:33 documents delay as 'add lookahead frames in each down cnn layer', so the bottleneck at frame t already carries 3 frames of future. 'A future-only input change must not move s_t' will fail for a correct implementation. Assert instead that ProxHead adds no lookahead beyond the backbone's 3 frames.
- Promote `channel_consistency` to a StrictConfig block (mirroring DistHeadConfig / VADHeadConfig) in the same change that adds the nonlinear-group knobs, so a typo in `mask_on_nonlinear` raises instead of silently disabling the one term the round is about.
- Add eval_coldstart_v2 cold-far (`none` and ambient) blocks as a GUARD with a pre-registered threshold, and set the keep-violation kill at a margin (e.g. > 28/148) rather than at +1 on a count of 20 with roughly +/-4-5 sampling noise.
- Add the round's actual prize to go_no_go, since it is nearly free: `anchor_gate_sim.py sweep` and `dawn` already take `--feature {dist,drr}`; add `--feature prox` and report the guard's keep violations / suppress passes / Dawn deletion driven by the new readout against the recorded arms (v16 ungated 20/148, pna-only 7/148, DRR-relative 3/148). Otherwise the claim 'a readout the shipped AnchorGate can read off-rig' is never tested.
- Correct the clip count (64 field clips / 499 records, 23 QVF, `none` = 64) and the keep-violation instrument attribution (anchor_gate_sim ungated arm, not the field scorecard), and gate the validation hook to one rank with an explicit barrier so a slow field pass cannot desync DDP.

### EVIDENCE & HISTORY (refutation): every expected effect checked against the diagnosis numbers and the recorded negative verdicts (v11 ep19, v11b, v14, v17 ERRATUM, v18, b-gate nine-gate, real-e2e rounds, presence_selfcal, anchor_gate, FIELD_BLOCK). Measurements I made myself are in /tmp/claude-1001/-home-milowu-A4Audio-PureSound/c52f3b49-8e8f-4f9f-a5ca-862771bd494c/scratchpad/v19_plan/verify_foreground_memory_history/NOTES.md

- score 3/10 — **kill**
- what would change my mind: "Four measurements, all cheap, all before any GPU cycle. (1) E3 run for real and PASSING on the corrected geometry: build the session rows and show v16 ep19 deletes the re-entry by worse than -1.0 dB -- if the recorded decayq5/decayq10 numbers (-0.49/-0.46 dB) hold, the row gives L_keep_on nothing and the round is over on the proposal's own threshold. (2) A per-group, non-inverted write-gate readout on an EXISTING checkpoint's head (v11b's cache) under the relative rule, at all four QVF groups -- not a device-fitted linear probe, and not a pooled 0.65. Since chain_readability measures inversion (sign), not offset, show that (nu - theta) recovers the sign, or the FiLM is being driven backwards on unseen chains. (3) The free control result: turn_taking_prob raised, one warm-started cycle, and the other2 Dn1 gate moved -- if a zero-code data knob halves it, the architecture is unnecessary; if it does not move at all, the 'the data lacks the shape' story is refuted for the primary metric and the architecture case has to be made some other way. (4) A pass band restated against v16+OnsetGuard (del 0.111) and v11b@0.85 (del 0.092 at -10.75 dB) with a matched-suppression comparison -- and, if the round intends to produce a deployment candidate, against v8's -11.78 dB field block. Clear all four and I would score this as a keep-with-amendments head-only round; clear none and it is the b-gate nine-gate repeated with more parameters."

**Fatal flaws**

- THE PRE-REGISTERED PASS BAND IS DOMINATED BY CODE THAT ALREADY SHIPS -- the exact v14 kill pattern. Proposal pass: Dawn deletion <=0.150 (stretch 0.123), WER <=0.24, field session suppression >=-8.0 dB, keep violations <= v16's 20/148. Already on record: v16 + the shipped OnsetGuard `pna` clause gives Dawn WER 0.217 / deletion 0.111 with 7/148 keep violations at far -11.81 dB (egs/voice_isolate/benchmarks/probes/anchor_gate_README.md lines 49-51 and 133-134), and v11b at dry_blend 0.85 -- a free inference knob -- gives Dawn WER 0.181 / deletion 0.092 at sessions -10.75 dB (benchmarks/probes/v14_VERDICT.md table). So a v19 run can satisfy every go/no-go and still be strictly worse on the headline metric than a free knob on an existing checkpoint, and worse than an inference guard that costs zero GPU. v14 was killed for precisely this ('at matched session suppression a free knob has HALF v14's deletion'). The stated success criterion ('the model no longer needs the guard') is not testable against a 0.150 threshold when the guard already reaches 0.111 and remains composable.
- THE PROPOSAL'S OWN 48 h KILL TEST E3 IS ALREADY FAILED BY THE DIAGNOSIS. E3 kills if the manufactured re-entry Dn1 on v16 ep19 is > -1.0 dB. The row shape is bystander -> 3-12 s gap -> user. anchor_synthetic/TABLE_utt.md, v16 ep19, utt arm: other2 (2 s wrong anchor, NO gap) Dn1 = -1.25 dB, but decayq5 Dn1 = -0.49 dB and decayq10 Dn1 = -0.46 dB, and the gap-corrected anchor effect is statistically dead by 10 s (p=0.091 at 10 s, p=0.93 at 20 s). Inserting the gap that the persistence half needs destroys the wrong-anchor deletion that the L_keep_on half needs to push on. The measured real failure geometry is the opposite: Dawn's damaging condition is an onset after >=1 s of background with the bystander speaking right up to the user's entry (-6.07 dB on v16), no gap. The two objectives are put in the same row where they cancel.
- THE HEADLINE DEFENCE AGAINST THE QVF CHAIN ANSWERS THE WRONG DEFECT. 'Relative, never absolute' was validated as a fix for a per-recording OFFSET (presence_selfcal_README.md Finding 1: pooled QVF 0.474 vs within-session 0.886/0.911; Finding 4: checkpoint drift kills a fixed threshold, the calibrator follows). chain_readability measures the QVF distance readout as INVERTED (AUC 0.258/0.265/0.255 across v11b/v8/v16; per group 0.000-0.092), and subtracting a session-relative reference cannot flip a sign. Worse, theta is a CAUSAL running mean over speech frames, so at the one condition that matters -- cold start, bystander first -- k~0, theta is undefined, and with beta=0 the FIRST talker writes at w~0.5. The write gate therefore enrols the bystander by construction at exactly the moment reference_matrix_README.md section 6.1 says deployment always produces a bystander first, and presence_selfcal Finding 2 already priced this ('the evidence and the harm run on the same clock'; the guards that make activation safe delay it past the useful window).
- IT WIRES A TRAINED PRESENCE READOUT TO THE MASK WITHOUT THE PREREQUISITE THE REPO PRE-REGISTERED. benchmarks/probes/v11_ep19_VERDICT.md closes with 'Wiring a gate is v12, and only after the cross-device gap is addressed on the data axis' -- and this round explicitly does NOT address the chain axis ('not expected to move the QVF cold-far column at all'). The last time a readout with good in-domain numbers drove suppression, the full nine-gate check produced turn-taking KEEP violations 6 -> 65, SI-SDR +5.42 -> -0.23 and four FAILs (benchmarks/full_gate/v8_gate050_VERDICT.md), with the recorded lesson that presence estimation must be invariant BEFORE it may drive gain. The zero-init FiLM and read_dropout are step-0 and eval-time devices; after training the FiLM is the actuator, and in deployment there is no 'memory off' arm.
- THE DATA DOSE IS OVERSTATED BY ~8x AND MOST SESSION ROWS CANNOT PHYSICALLY EXIST. Monte Carlo over the proposal's own draws with the audit's measured bucket shares (49.8/37.8/9.9/2.5% for 3/6/12/30 s and realised lengths 5.71-6.0 / 11.43-12.0 s): P(far-solo U(1.2,4) + gap U(3,6) + re-entry U(1.8,5) fits a 6 s row) = 0.024 (median need 9.44 s); 12 s bucket 0.270; 30 s bucket 1.000. Effective share of ALL rows carrying the intended shape = 2.1%, not the claimed 17.6%. The 6 s bucket supplies 75% of the claimed eligible rows and admits the script essentially never, so the >=0.8 s near-active assertion silently reverts them to _bernoulli. The whole data axis therefore lands on the 12 s + 30 s buckets (12.4% of rows), where the two ASRFeatureLoss terms cover the row with probability 0.042-0.25 (audit finding 6).
- THE 'TOTAL ANTI-SUPPRESSION PRESSURE IS CONSERVED' CLAIM IS ARITHMETICALLY FALSE, in the direction v18 warned about. OverSuppression 3.0 -> 2.0 plus OnsetWeightedOverSuppression at 1.5 with omega in {1,3}: with the measured onset frame share 14.1% the keep-side effective weight is 2.0 + 1.5*1.28 = 3.92; at 19.9% it is 4.10; if row-start onsets count (81.7% of rows start with the target inside 0.1 s, and on a 3 s row the first second is a third of the frames) it is 4.49. That is +31% to +50% keep-side pressure while every suppress-side weight is unchanged and the total goes 7.0 -> 8.0, further diluting the suppress share. v18's recorded verdict is that reducing the incentive to kill to the floor made EVERY suppression number shallower (cold-far +0.53 dB, p=0.017), and v11b's signature is a representation gain bought with roughly half the anchored far suppression. The predicted outcome is shallower suppression bought with keep relief -- the trade the proposal itself names as its kill condition.
- THE CLAIMED FREE ATTRIBUTION ABLATION IS NOT AN ABLATION. 'memory read zeroed = data-only effect, ZERO GPU' is wrong: a model trained with the read and then lesioned is not the same as the same data without the module. The genuine data-only control (session rows, no ForegroundMemory) needs its own cycle -- and the audit already names a zero-code version of it: overlap_control.turn_taking_prob is 0.0 in v16, so the far-first shape reaches only ~6% of rows through row overrides. That knob is untested and is the cheap control that would separate the data hypothesis from the 6 engineering-days + ~8-12 GPU-days architecture hypothesis. The round bundles data + architecture + three losses + a weight rebalance and calls itself 'one axis' on the sole ground that length_schedule is untouched.

**Evidence conflicts**

- egs/voice_isolate/benchmarks/probes/v11_ep19_VERDICT.md -- v11's heads already reached in-domain 0.959 and 180d 0.843 while reading QVF at 0.474 (chance), with bystanders qvf_scenario2/3_far1 read MORE present (+7.51/+8.05) than true near clips. The proposal's write-gate go/no-go (>=0.90 in-domain, >=0.75 on 180D) is a bar v11 already cleared, and v11 was judged 'Not deployable'. That gate has no discriminating power, and the verdict's explicit condition for wiring a gate ('only after the cross-device gap is addressed on the data axis') is not met by this round.
- egs/voice_isolate/benchmarks/full_gate/v8_gate050_VERDICT.md (and the memory note presence-readout-confounded-with-reverb) -- a presence readout driving suppression: turn-taking KEEP violations 6 -> 65, SI-SDR +5.42 -> -0.23, Dawn/moderate/BUT-OFFICE/extreme-reverb all FAIL, while the field set showed ZERO cost because it is one room. The proposal's keep-side gate is field set v3 only (kill at v16+2 confirmed) with no turn-taking KEEP gate and no reverb-stratified deletion gate -- reproducing the measurement error that made that gate look safe ('passing the field scorecard equals not having been tested').
- egs/voice_isolate/benchmarks/probes/v14_VERDICT.md -- a zero-init parallel addition warm-started on this backbone, with the same 'baseline IS the initialisation' argument, produced keep gains that a free dry_blend sweep beat at matched suppression (0.181/0.092 vs 0.271/0.175). The proposal reuses the method and the argument and pre-registers pass thresholds below that free knob's recorded numbers.
- /tmp/.../v19_plan/anchor_synthetic/TABLE_utt.md -- with a 5-10 s gap the wrong-anchor deletion is -0.49/-0.46 dB (v16) and gap-corrected p=0.091 / 0.93, i.e. the manufactured session row presents almost none of the failure. Meanwhile the primary go/no-go metric is other2 (2 s anchor, NO gap), a condition the new row regime never produces; the immediate wrong-anchor shape it does need already exists in the v16 distribution (training_data_audit: >=1 s of interferer before onset on 6.0% of rows, >=2 s on 4.6%, with KEEP as the labelled action) and the failure survives it at p=1.4e-24. 'The data has never contained this shape' is false for the primary metric.
- egs/voice_isolate/benchmarks/probes/reference_matrix_README.md section 6.2 plus the memory note coldstart-room-reference-causal -- the ~10 s forgetting is currently the ONLY thing that stops a single bystander utterance from arming suppression for the rest of a session, and the recorded behaviour of an armed anchor is that it deletes the next near talker. A decay-free slot ('silence writes nothing and forgets nothing') removes that safety valve by construction, leaving an untested demotion clause whose trigger ('a confidently nearer, dissimilar talker') depends on the readout measured at chance/inverted on the unseen chain. Expected effect 2 asks for persistence at 10 s to go -2.01 -> <=-6.0 dB without naming the keep cost that section 6.2 says is the same variable.
- chain_readability (this round's own diagnosis) -- E1, the load-bearing 48 h kill test, substitutes a DEVICE-FITTED logistic probe for the trained write head. The measured contrast is exactly that the linear probe reads QVF at 0.789 while the trained head reads 0.26. E1 will therefore pass at its 0.65 threshold while testing an object that will not exist in the trained model. The kill package cannot kill.
- benchmarks/field_test_vector/FIELD_BLOCK.md and the memory note field-coldfar-single-ckpt-noise -- block-corrected field session suppression is v8 -11.78 vs v16 -9.73, i.e. v16 (the round's only baseline) is the WORSE of the two, and v8 remains the shipped default. A pass band of '>= -8.0 dB against v16's -9.73' accepts ~3.8 dB below the deployed model; no gate in the list compares against v8 at all. On the repo's own precedent (v10, v11, v11b) a candidate that loses to v8 is not a candidate.
- objective_landscape (this round's own diagnosis) -- 'Treat arrival-order supervision as the reference design for any future explicit slot memory ... but only AFTER M1 has fixed WHICH talker the anchor should be. Both mechanisms make persistence stronger, which on today's model would make the wrong-anchor deletion worse, not better.' And for M3: 'If the bottleneck encodes chain rather than talker, M3 would re-run the real-e2e round-1 channel-anchoring failure and should not be built.' The proposal builds M1 + M2 + M3 + the slot simultaneously and defers the M3 prerequisite to a 4 GPU-hour test whose threshold (Delta_talker > 0) is far too weak given the already-measured ~30% channel component in the template (ident arm samespk -0.44/-0.40 dB against diffspk -1.47/-1.33).
- onset_profile (this round's own diagnosis) -- onset deletion is absent in the median on both synthetic proxies (-0.25 to +0.30 dB) and only -0.52/-0.67 dB with a 1 s own-background prefix, against 11-23x on real. L_keep_on is a loss reweighting aimed at a failure that is not in-distribution, and the row regime meant to put it there is the one measured above at 2.1% of rows and stripped of its deletion by its own gap.

**Amendments**

- If anything from this design survives, run the free control FIRST and alone: raise overlap_control.turn_taking_prob (0.0 today) on synthetic rows, put the far-first turn on the 6 s+ buckets only, and fix the audit's gated-to-zero-but-labelled-present bug (1.0% of rows). Zero code, one warm-started cycle, and it settles whether the wrong-anchor failure is a data-dose problem at all before 6 engineering-days and ~8-12 GPU-days are committed. The audit names this as the cheapest lever; the proposal skips it.
- Re-specify the row shapes as TWO separate regimes, never one: (a) an immediate wrong-anchor row (bystander turn ending 0-0.4 s before the user's entry) which is the geometry the Dawn -6.07 dB condition and the other2 -1.25 dB gate actually measure, and (b) a long-gap re-entry row on the 12/30 s buckets only, with the gap drawn so the script fits the realised row length (my Monte Carlo: 6 s rows admit the current draws 2.4% of the time). Report the realised per-shape share from scripts/check_training_data.py before any GPU, and state it in rows, not in intent.
- Move every acceptance threshold to the strongest baseline on record, not v16 ungated: v16 + OnsetGuard pna (Dawn WER 0.217 / del 0.111, 7/148 violations) and v11b @ dry_blend 0.85 (0.181 / 0.092 at -10.75 dB), plus v8 for the field block (-11.78). A round that cannot beat a free inference knob at matched suppression is closed by the v14 precedent regardless of how it scores against v16.
- Do not touch the loss weights in the same round. Keep OverSuppression at 3.0 and add the onset-weighted term at a weight that leaves the keep-side total at 3.0 (i.e. 3.0 - 1.5*E[omega] for the measured omega), or drop L_keep_on entirely for round 1 -- the onset-weighted term has no in-distribution gradient anyway until the row regime is verified by E3.
- Do not wire the read to the mask in round 1. Train the memory and the write gate as a HEAD-ONLY change (the v11 pattern: heads training-only, inference bit-identical) and judge the representation with presence_head_judgment.py plus the paired-corpora probe. Only if the write gate is non-inverted on all four QVF groups AND reverb-invariant does a FiLM-conditioned round become admissible -- that is the prerequisite v11_ep19_VERDICT and the b-gate nine-gate both pre-registered.
- Replace E1 with a test of the object that will exist: fit nothing, and instead measure whether an EXISTING trained head's logit (v11b's presence head, cached) supports the write rule per QVF group -- and pre-register a kill at group-level inversion, not at pooled AUC 0.65. A device-fitted probe reading 0.789 is the known-good half of the contrast, not the half that fails.
- Add the two gates whose absence made the last readout-driven round look safe: the turn-taking KEEP gate (violations and SI-SDR) and reverb-stratified deletion (Dawn / moderate / BUT-OFFICE / extreme), both from benchmarks/full_gate. Field set v3 alone cannot see a reverb- or turn-conditional failure.
- Drop the 'zero-GPU attribution ablation' claim and budget a real data-only control cycle, or accept that a positive result cannot be attributed to the architecture.
- Specify what bounds the decay-free slot in a bystander-dominant session: with S_w and S_wu never discounted, m_bar becomes a speech-mass majority template, so in a meeting where a bystander talks more than the user the demotion clause fires against the USER continuously. That case is the deployment case for DiPCo/AMI/Dawn and is unaddressed.

### BUILDABILITY & MEASUREMENT — can it be implemented in THIS codebase, at the stated cost, under the curriculum-ladder constraint, and are the pre-registered gates measurable with in-repo instruments at resolvable n?

- score 5.5/10 — **amend**

With those, all eight gates are measurable with in-repo instruments at stated resolution and the round is buildable on the warm start -- the load-bearing pieces the proposal assumes do exist and do what it says (heads.py from_config + initial_stream_state/step contract, the provider table + invoke_loss's required_inputs, preflight_ckpt_recipe.py, eval_field_block.py's compare/Wilcoxon, eval_wer.py's bootstrap CI, eval_dawn_chorus.py's --context and onset-guard args, audit_span_metric.py, corpus_pair_probe.py, gating running before noise_stage, all 20 v16 checkpoints on disk, and the v17 row_initial_ambient block as a working precedent for a row-shape knob). Move me to kill only if the between-blocks FiLM position turns out to be non-negotiable AND the write gate must read block 1: then the E1 kill test, the agcache reuse, gate 3's instrument and the streaming state layout all have to be rebuilt from scratch, and the 48 h package stops being a 48 h package.

**Fatal flaws**

- SILENT NO-OP #1: config/infer_dpcrn.yaml is not in the change list, and four of the eight gates load it. eval_dawn_chorus.py:18, eval_field_block.py:12, eval_coldstart_v2.py:17 and eval_realcase.py:53 all take `config/infer_dpcrn.yaml` as their positional config. Without a `foreground_memory` block there, `runner.py:265` (`load_state_dict(..., strict=False)`) drops `backbone.fg_memory.*` and `backbone.fg_film.*` with a WARNING and the model runs as v16-shaped with no memory at all -- gates 4,5,6,7 would measure a no-op and report it as "the axis produced nothing". This is verbatim the failure preflight_ckpt_recipe.py's own docstring says "has already cost this project one round". FIX: add the block to config/infer_dpcrn.yaml (note the release-layout rule: config/ holds only the default, so this is a deliberate change to the deployed default architecture) and run preflight on BOTH configs before the run.
- SILENT NO-OP #2: theta freezes, so the self-calibrated reference degenerates into an absolute threshold -- and no pre-registered gate can see it. `theta_t = theta_{t-1} + s_t (nu_t - theta_{t-1})/max(k_t,1)` with `k_t = k_{t-1} + s_t` is a running mean whose step is 1/k. At 100 fps with s_t ~ 0.5 the step is < 1e-3 by 20 s and < 1e-4 by 200 s: theta is pinned to whatever the first ~30 s established. The entire justification for the relative rule is that absolute thresholds died twice across chain and checkpoint drift while within-session contrast survived (presence_selfcal_README: pooled QVF 0.474 vs within-session 0.886/0.911) -- but a frozen global session mean IS an absolute threshold, learned in the first half-minute. Every instrument in the plan runs on short material (presence_selfcal_sim.extract() cuts 20 s segments, Dawn utterances ~10 s, the WER sets are 10 s-capped), so the frozen regime is unmeasurable by construction and all eight gates can pass on a design that reproduces the failure it claims to break. FIX: floor the step (alpha_t = max(1/k_t, 1/k_max), k_max ~ 500-1000 frames = a 5-10 s adaptive window) and add a long-stream probe (concatenate 5-10 min from the 180d/*_session recordings, read the wrong-anchor Delta_on1 at the tail against the head).
- The streaming-export plan is internally contradictory, and the warm-up gate -- the one bug this file documents at length -- is unaddressed. streaming/dpcrn.py:186-190 hard-codes `self.heads = [(name, getattr(...)) for name in ("vad_head", "background_vad_head")]`, and that one list drives the state-port layout (_head_state_names:198), the output names (extra_output_names:195), AND the evaluation point: heads run at line 422, AFTER both DPRNN blocks. The write_head must run between block 1 and block 2, so it cannot "be registered as an ordinary head so it keeps its own triple". `state_from_tensors:310-341` slices positionally (`base = n_down + n_up + 2*n_blocks`, head triples assumed to be the trailing 3*len(heads)), so three memory tensors need a new explicit slice on both sides. Worse: lines 417-450 gate every head state AND the inter-LSTM state with tensor arithmetic for `warmup_frames` frames (v16 is delay=[1,1,1] -> 3 phantom frames) because "a head carrying a 4 s EMA integrates those phantoms into an offset that survives hundreds of frames -- measured as a logit error still growing at frame 400". A DECAY-FREE accumulator is the worst possible case for that bug: the phantom write never decays out, ever. The plan mentions none of this, and gate 8 (export parity) is a KILL gate. Budget 1 day -> 2-3 days.

**Evidence conflicts**

- The proposal computes keep_weight and reentry_onset_frame from the session script's frame indices, but speed perturbation runs AFTER overlap gating: gating is at puresound/task/ns.py:386, sox_speed_perturbed at ns.py:487-510, the fixed-length snip at ns.py:595. Every index the script produces is off by the speed factor -- up to ~1.5 s on a 30 s row and ~0.3 s on a 6 s row, comparable to the 1 s window L_keep_on is meant to weight. The training_data_audit's own note ("speed perturb ns.py:487-510 then fixed-length snip") is in the diagnosis the proposal cites. FIX (and it is simpler than the plan): derive keep_weight from the FINAL post-speed, post-snip `vad_target` in the same block that builds it (ns.py:600-602) -- an onset preceded by >=1.5 s of inactive is directly detectable there, it works on ALL rows not just session rows, it needs no GatingResult change, and it removes two of the three new scalars.
- E1's kill test replays a different tensor than the design reads. benchmarks/probes/anchor_gate_cache.py:73-75 builds `feat` from `model.backbone.last_bottleneck`, which puresound/nnet/dpcrn.py:297 sets AFTER BOTH DPRNN blocks. The design's write_head reads the block-1 output. So the pre-registered E1 threshold ("KILL if the relative form's pooled anchored AUC is < 0.65") is measured on a proxy representation and binds nothing. FIX: either move the write_head to the same block-2 bottleneck every other head reads (which ALSO makes the streaming head-registration plan work as written, and makes gate 3 reusable) and put the FiLM on the decoder input instead; or add a mid-bottleneck stash flag and re-cache (~1 line + ~1 GPU-hour).
- Gate 3's instrument cannot read the write gate. benchmarks/probes/presence_head_judgment.py imports extract() from presence_selfcal_sim.py, whose line 61 hard-codes `model.backbone.last_vad_logits`. The write-gate logit lives at backbone.fg_memory.write_head. And the companion "validation hook logging in-domain AUC per epoch" does not exist -- validation_step logs losses only (the chain_readability diagnosis states this and I confirmed no AUC hook in siso.py). Both are unlisted code, roughly a day.
- The preflight gate as pre-registered cannot pass, and the tensor count is wrong. scripts/preflight_ckpt_recipe.py exits 1 on ANY missing key outside its hard-coded `aux = ("backbone.vad_head.", "backbone.background_vad_head.", "backbone.dist_head.")`; it has no expected-missing list to check a name set against, so "confirm the missing-key list contains exactly those eight names" is not a thing the tool can do. And the new tensors are TEN, not eight: W_u (w,b)=2, write_head proj(w,b)+dwconv(w,b)+out(w,b)=6, TemporalFiLM (w,b)=2. The tool also fails in the reverse direction on a v19 ckpt against any inference recipe that lacks the block ("DROPS N trained"), which is finding #1 again.
- Parameter and RTF cost understated. With `ema_taus_s ~ [1.0, 4.0]`, heads.py:113 gives write_head `in_features = enc_channels * (1 + 2) = 384`, so proj = 24,640, dwconv (groups=1, 64->64, k=3) = 12,352, out = 65 -> 37,057 params, not the stated 24,961. Total new = 8,256 + 37,057 + 17,664 = 62,977 = +7.86% on the 801,743-param backbone, not +6.35%. Gate 8's kill is RTF > +10%, so a 1.5-point understatement of the delta is material.
- Two of four primary instruments and their 4.2 GB cache live only in this session's ephemeral scratchpad. Gates 1 and 2 name scratchpad/v19_plan/anchor_synthetic/anchor_synthetic_probe.py, gate 4 names scratchpad/v19_plan/onset_profile/onset_real.py, and both depend on /tmp/claude-1001/<session-uuid>/scratchpad/agcache/{v8,v16,v11b} (I measured 1.9G + 1.9G + 446M). Every other pre-registered instrument in this project lives in egs/voice_isolate/benchmarks/probes/ with its tables in benchmarks/. Nothing in the plan promotes them. A threshold whose instrument vanishes with the session is not pre-registered.
- The claimed 17.6%-of-rows session share is an upper bound that ignores two gates the code imposes. overlap_gating.apply() returns `untouched` when `not interferers` (line 141), and the whole gating call sits inside ns.py:369-386's `augmentation_speech.used and (force_interferer or ... or rand < prob)` branch. The audit's own measurement is the counterexample: turn-taking reaches only 11.7% of rows despite realfar 0.3 / realnear 0.5 overrides. The plan is right to verify with check_training_data.py first -- but drop 17.6% as a prediction and state the number as measured-before-GPU only.

FIELD_BLOCK.md's own basis for the block protocol is that this set has poor resolution (same-run ep8-19 cold-far median spans -0.35..-3.39 dB, 19.9 dB single-clip swing); a real 1.5 dB loss would comfortably pass p>0.05. Restate as an equivalence bound: the paired CI's lower edge must sit above the -6.5 dB kill level.
- Gates 1, 2 and 4 are pre-registered on single checkpoints against single-checkpoint v16 baselines, on metrics whose checkpoint-to-checkpoint noise has never been measured. anchor_synthetic's own caveat says the v8-vs-v16 magnitude differences "are within the range that FIELD_BLOCK says single checkpoints cannot resolve", and the gate-1 pass band (-1.25 -> -0.63 dB) is smaller than that. The probe costs ~10 min/ckpt, so ep15-19 blocks for BOTH v16 and v19 is ~1.7 GPU-hours -- run them and gate on the block median with paired Wilcoxon, the same protocol the field gates already use.
- Two remaining confound leaks the plan already knows about elsewhere. (a) Synthesis boundary: gate 1 pre-registers a v8 target ("v8-equivalent -1.71 -> >= -0.85") on synthetic material, but v8 is pre-2026-08-18 and v19 post; drop every v8 number from the synthetic gates and keep v8 only on real recordings. (b) 10 s cap: gate 5's `deletion <= 0.150` is an ABSOLUTE on a set where 37% of utterances are cut at 10 s against full references (mix del 0.29 capped vs 0.09 uncapped, AZURE_2026-09.md); restate as a delta-vs-mix threshold plus the uncapped subset, the way gate 6 already does.
- Unlisted file: forcing absolute_floor on session rows needs a new kwarg in puresound/task/noise_stage.py. `NoiseStage.apply(noisy, sample_rate, room_scene)` reads `self.config.absolute_floor` and draws against its own `prob` (noise_stage.py:165-166); there is no per-row override. Implementation item 9 states the intent but names only ns.py.
- "read_dropout 0.15 ... makes the 'memory off' ablation free at eval time (no second run)" is false as stated. read_dropout makes the model ROBUST to an empty read; measuring memory-off still requires a second forward with the read zeroed. The GPU-training saving is real; the eval saving is not.

**Amendments**

- Move the write_head to read the SAME post-block-2 bottleneck every other head reads (dpcrn.py:282-297) and apply the TemporalFiLM there, on the decoder input, instead of between the blocks. One change fixes three problems at once: E1's agcache replay becomes exact rather than a proxy; the write_head really can be registered in streaming/dpcrn.py's `self.heads` list and keep its own triple as the plan assumed; and gate 3's readout becomes a one-line change to presence_selfcal_sim.extract() instead of a new extraction path. If the between-blocks position is load-bearing, say why and pay for a mid-bottleneck stash flag plus a ~1 GPU-hour re-cache -- but do not pre-register E1's kill on the wrong tensor.

Without this the design's central claim -- relative, not absolute -- is untrue after 30 s of stream, and no listed gate can detect it.
- Derive keep_weight from the FINAL vad_target at ns.py:600-602 (post-speed, post-snip), not from the session script's frame indices. Onset = a near-active frame preceded by >=1.5 s of near-inactive, computed on the tensor the loss will actually align to. ### EVIDENCE & HISTORY — refutation pass against the diagnosis numbers and the repo's recorded negative results

- score 2/10 — **kill**
- what would change my mind: "Three measurements, all cheap and all missing. (1) A CPU/1-GPU pass showing that the D1/D3 rows, scored on frozen v16 ep19, already exhibit the failure at deployment scale — fg-projection onset excess <= -1.3 dB (a third of Dawn's -3.90) on the manufactured rows without a digital-silence prefix. Today the best comparable number is -0.67 dB with a 1 s background lead, so the loss has ~1/6 of the error to push on. (2) Any precedent in this repo where a training change moved UNGATED Dawn deletion below 0.18 (every recorded round sits 0.175-0.240; only inference-side gating reaches 0.09-0.11). (3) A stated mechanism that breaks the persistence / wrong-anchor coupling without extending memory, since D1 supervises exactly the memory extension that reference_matrix §6.2 and the synthetic 20 s null say lengthens the deletion window. If in addition PRIMARY 3 were re-specified on a real 5-checkpoint block instrument with the bar at the v16 guarded arm (7/148) and the arm compared head-to-head with v16+OnsetGuard, I would move from kill to amend and back the 48 h part (a) alone."

**Fatal flaws**

- PRIMARY 1 has no measured basis and the proposal removes the one construction that has one. The diagnosis measured that a 1 s own-background lead moves synthetic onset deletion only to -0.52 (v8) / -0.67 dB (v16) against Dawn's -3.85/-3.90 (still 9-15x short), and that the ONLY prefix reproducing the real tail is digital silence (v16 sil1 p5 -70.2 vs Dawn -75.6). D3 then sets require_capture_floor=True and states '0% digital-silence leads' as a PASS condition in guardrail 8, i.e. the round deliberately excludes its only measured-effective stressor. The same exclusion drops the largest measured keep failure on record: anchor_gate_README.md §3 'silence prefix catastrophe, dt keep median -35.46 dB'. A suppress bias on a keep row is the training signal, not a contaminant. So the expected move -3.90 -> -1.5..-2.0 dB rests on rows measured to carry ~1/6 of the phenomenon.
- D3 is v17's row_initial_ambient re-run, and v17 is not merely 'null on cold-far' — it damaged exactly the metric this round exists to improve. benchmarks/field_test_vector/FIELD_BLOCK.md: 'v17 vs v16 ep19: cold-far none worse (+0.35, p=0.020) and five QVF keep clips degrade 1.7-6.9 dB'; v17_round_design.md VERDICT: keep near worst -8.34 -> -11.61. The proposal cites only the cold-far half and never engages with the keep damage, although its own PRIMARY 3 kill threshold ('any single keep clip deepening past -12 dB') would have fired on v17. Changing 1-4 s to 0.5-3.0 s and adding a bystander variant is not a measured differentiator; v17's own reading was 'more examples of reference-then-decide cannot conjure a reference. Axis closed for training.'
- The expected effects assume a mechanism the repo says is absent on this architecture, and D1's supervision contradicts the proposal's own stated non-goal. reference_matrix_README.md §6.2: the state is one recent-foreground template, 'the interjection deletion and the cold-start passthrough are two faces of the same mechanism. Any fix to one on this architecture moves the other'; §6.5 says the architecture question ('a persistent representation that survives 10 s of silence and does not transfer to the next talker') must be answered 'before any training'. The anchor_synthetic diagnosis confirms the shared clock (wrong-anchor deletion closes at 20 s, p=0.24/0.93, same constant as the memory decay) and instructs that 'a v19 design must break the coupling explicitly rather than just extending the state's memory'. D1 supervises the SAME near talker returning after a 3-10 s gap — that is memory extension by construction — while expected_effects[2] claims the decay curve is 'allowed to stay as it is'. No mechanism in the proposal breaks the coupling; the round is architecture-unchanged by design.
- The onset weight puts ~90% of its added mass on frames with no measured error. Arithmetic on the proposal's own new length_schedule (row shares 27.8/33.3/29.2/9.7%) and the audit's 81.7% of rows with target onset <=0.1 s: row-head onset frames = 0.1435 of all frames, manufactured re-entry onsets = 0.0150, so 90.5% of m_onset is row heads — where the measured fg-projection excess is -0.25 dB (moderate) and +0.30 dB (indomain), i.e. the model is already correct or better than steady state, and the audit's own implication says 'at t=0 the correct action there is KEEP'. Total onset fraction is 0.159, not the claimed ~0.11, so OverSuppression's value rises 1+7*0.159 = 2.11x (not 1.8x): effective weight ~6.3 of a 7.0 total, all of it keep-side, with suppress-side terms fixed. That is the v18 lesson in mirror image (capping the kill incentive made every suppression number shallower) and the v14 failure shape (a free dry_blend knob beat the round).
- PRIMARY 3 cannot be read with the instrument it names. eval_field_block.py reports, per FIELD_BLOCK.md, 'keep near violations / worst' = 3 / -8.34 for the v16 ep19 block. The 148-span keep objective and the 20/148 and 13/148 numbers come from anchor_gate_sim.py on a single-checkpoint per-frame cache over context prefixes (anchor_gate_README.md §2), a different instrument that violates the 5-checkpoint block protocol the gate claims to follow — and whose cache cost for 5 checkpoints (~25 min each x 2 caches) exceeds the 0.05 GPU-days budgeted. As written the primary keep gate is unreadable.
- A full pass does not change the deployment decision, because the pass thresholds are set below the guard that already ships. puresound/system/onset_guard.py (the pna clause) gives v16 keep violations 20/148 -> 7/148 and Dawn deletion 0.207 -> 0.111 today, and drr3 gives 0.094 (anchor_gate_README §2, §6) — against the raw-mix floor of 0.084. The proposal's PRIMARY 3 passes at <=13/148 (explicitly 'the number the guarded v8 arm achieves', i.e. the weaker arm) and guardrail 5 passes at deletion <=0.15 with WER <=0.25. Both are worse than v16+OnsetGuard on the same tables, while guardrail 4 simultaneously permits sessions to fall -9.73 -> -8.3 dB. Risk 1 says the honest comparison is 'against v16 ep19 + OnsetGuard, not against bare v16', but none of the eight gates encodes it. Best case: 2.1 GPU-days for an arm dominated by a shipped inference flag.
- The pre-registered kill rule is nearly unfireable, and it leans on the metric that is not the deployment failure. Killing requires BOTH (i) synthetic Delta_on1 failing to move 40% toward zero AND (ii) Dawn onset excess failing to improve 1.0 dB. Delta_on1 is the one measure the diagnosis says IS in-distribution with gradient (-1.25 dB, p~3e-24), and D2 raises the row shape ~5x while the loss reweights precisely those frames — so (i) will almost certainly move, buying the remaining ~1.7 GPU-days regardless of the real number. And the real/synthetic magnitude gap applies to the wrong-anchor case too (Dawn background-first onset -6.07 dB vs synthetic -1.25 dB, ~5x), so closing the synthetic metric does not predict the real one.
- Unmeasured collateral bundled into the arm: near-speech supervision mass falls ~31%. Simulating _turn_script (turn_far 2.0-4.5, turn_near 1.5-3.0, gap 0-0.4 / overlap 0-0.3) with the forced gap gives a near-mask fraction of 0.30 (12 s rows) / 0.34 (30 s rows) against v16's measured per-row target-active 0.80 synthetic; with the new schedule the batch mean lands ~0.55 (0.69x). Because reentry_prob is gated only on allow_turn_taking (not on turn_taking_prob), ~0.805 of all rows >=9 s become turn-taking rows, including the real-near-keep and real-far rows whose overrides already fire — and per curriculum-ladder-is-load-bearing those rows carry v8/v16's real far suppression (-9.83 dB vs -1.65 from the last stage alone). The round perturbs the load-bearing rows with no measured basis, and additionally changes v16's own tuned axis (the length schedule) inside the same arm.

**Evidence conflicts**

- egs/voice_isolate/benchmarks/field_test_vector/FIELD_BLOCK.md — 'v17 vs v16 ep19: cold-far none worse (+0.35, p=0.020) and five QVF keep clips degrade 1.7-6.9 dB'; v16 ep19 block keep near violations/worst = 3 / -8.34 vs v17 4 / -11.61. D3 re-runs that knob; the proposal reports only the cold-far half and its PRIMARY 3 uses a keep count this instrument does not emit.
- egs/voice_isolate/benchmarks/probes/v17_round_design.md VERDICT + ERRATUM — the axis was declared closed for training ('more examples of reference-then-decide cannot conjure one'; §2b withdrawn: room tone is a null, the anchor is any talker through the same chain). D3's non-bystander half (0.5 of lead rows, ~7.5% of all rows) is therefore a measured null (true-floor pad delta -0.04 / +0.03 dB, p 0.08 / 0.76 per reference_matrix §1).
- egs/voice_isolate/benchmarks/probes/reference_matrix_README.md §6.2 and §6.5 — persistence and wrong-anchor deletion are one mechanism, 'any fix to one on this architecture moves the other', and the buildability question must be answered 'before any training'. The proposal makes no architecture change yet claims to move the keep side while leaving the decay curve untouched.
- DIAGNOSIS anchor_synthetic implications — 'Do not expect an anchor fix on this set to fix the real-chain cold start... Cold-start severity has to come from a different axis' and 'Never use digital silence as the neutral prefix in a training or eval row'. The proposal both targets the real-chain onset number with synthetic row shape and, in obeying the second sentence, discards the only construction the onset_profile probe measured to reproduce the real tail.
- DIAGNOSIS onset_profile implications — the real/synthetic ratio is 11-23x on the median and 3.9-7.9x on the p5 tail, and 'the synthetic number will be near zero both before and after the change'. Nothing in the round measures that its manufactured rows close more than the ~0.5-0.7 dB the bg1 prefix produced.
- egs/voice_isolate/benchmarks/probes/anchor_gate_README.md §2/§6/§7 — the shipped OnsetGuard already delivers v16 20/148 -> 7/148 keep violations and Dawn 0.207 -> 0.111 (drr3 0.094, raw mix 0.084) with zero retraining, and 'on 180d (held out, nothing to fix: 0/40 violations ungated) the gate is pure cost'. The proposal's pass thresholds (<=13/148, deletion <=0.15) are strictly worse, and it never puts v16+guard on the same table despite its own risk 1 requiring it.
- egs/voice_isolate/benchmarks/probes/v18_VERDICT.md — capping the 'kill to the floor' incentive made every suppress number shallower (cold-far +0.53 dB, p=0.017). The round's one deliberate loss-balance change is a 2.11x rise in a keep-side term at fixed suppress weight, which is the same perturbation from the other side; guardrail 4 pre-authorises a 1.4 dB session loss.
- egs/voice_isolate/benchmarks/probes/v14_VERDICT.md — the round-scale precedent for 'keep improves, suppression pays': v14's keep/deletion gains were beaten outright by a free dry_blend sweep (v11b @ 0.85: sessions -10.75, Dawn 0.181/0.092), and its keep wins were span-energy numbers the ASR contradicted. The proposal names this risk but its gates do not price it against any knob baseline.
- egs/voice_isolate/benchmarks/wer_sets/AZURE_2026-09.md — the standing Dawn record under the strong recognizer is mix 0.234/0.073, v8 0.346/0.226, v16 0.308/0.198, i.e. both models make real-recording WER worse than not processing, entirely through deletion. No training round on record (v9, v10, v11b, v14, v16, v17, v18) has put unguarded Dawn deletion below ~0.175-0.184 on any recognizer; guardrail 5's target of 0.12-0.15 has only ever been reached by inference-side gating.
- DIAGNOSIS training_data_audit — 81.7% of rows already have the target onset within 0.1 s and at t=0 the correct action is KEEP; the ASRFeatureLoss crop is 6 s (verified in puresound/nnet/loss/asr_feature.py:85-91 and the v16 config's crop_seconds: 6.). Simulating the D1 script, an anchored [0,6 s) crop covers the manufactured post-gap onset only 19.3% of the time on 12 s rows and 6.7% on 30 s rows (~16% weighted), so L1b anchors supervision on the already-correct row head, misses ~84% of the round's own target frames, and strips late-row intelligibility supervision on half the steps for the 38.9% of rows that are now >=12 s.

**Amendments**

- Split out and land the two things that are measured and free, with no GPU and no arm: (a) the target_present relabel for rows whose gated target is exactly zero (the audit measured 1.0% of rows, all on the 3 s bucket, scored as lone-far by SDR/MR-STFT while ResidualReferenceLoss(target_present_only) still counted them present) — this is a bug fix, not an experiment; (b) implementation item 10, the temporal columns in scripts/check_training_data.py, so onset/gap/re-entry content is checkable before a run. Both stand on their own merits regardless of the round's fate.
- Before any training, answer the question reference_matrix §6.5 says must be answered first: can this architecture hold a foreground identity that survives silence WITHOUT lengthening the window in which the next talker is deleted? Until that has a measured answer, any keep-side data/loss round is betting against a shared clock the repo has confirmed twice (field and synthetic, p=0.24 / 0.93 at 20 s).
- If a data round is run anyway, make it a single knob with a measured effect rather than a five-change bundle. The only construction the onset_profile probe showed reproduces the real onset tail is a very low / digital-silence lead-in (v16 sil1 p5 -70.2 dB vs Dawn -75.6), which is also the condition behind the -35.46 dB dt keep catastrophe. Train against it explicitly (as a stressor on KEEP rows, with the correct action being keep) instead of forbidding it via require_capture_floor. Drop the length_schedule change, drop onset_crop_prob, and drop the row-head clause from m_onset (is_row_head fires on ~82% of rows where no deletion is measured and accounts for 90.5% of the mask's mass) — weight only post-gap and post-bystander onsets.
- Re-specify the keep gate on an instrument that can produce it: either eval_field_block.py's own block metrics (keep near violations/worst and the >=1 dB severity table, v16 ep19 baseline 3 / -8.34) or the anchor_gate 148-span objective computed on all five block checkpoints with the cache cost budgeted (~4 GPU-hours, not 0.05 GPU-days). Set the bar at the v16 guarded arm (7/148), not the v8 one (13/148).
- Put v16 ep19 + OnsetGuard (pna, and drr6 which is v16's own frontier) in the pre-registered comparison table for every keep and deletion gate, as risk 1 already concedes. A round whose best pass is dominated by a shipped inference flag should not consume 2.1 GPU-days.
- Rewrite the kill rule so it can fire: make the real metric (Dawn fg-projection onset excess, or better, Azure Dawn deletion delta) necessary rather than sufficient-in-the-alternative, and add the part-(a) precondition that the manufactured rows measurably reproduce at least a third of the real onset deficit on the frozen v16 weights (i.e. fg-projection excess <= -1.3 dB on the new row shapes) before any GPU time is bought.

### BUILDABILITY & MEASUREMENT — can v19-RETURN be implemented in this codebase, at the stated cost, under the curriculum-ladder constraint, and are its eight go/no-go rows actually measurable with the named instruments at resolvable thresholds? Every claim below was checked by reading the file.

- score 6/10 — **amend**
- what would change my mind: Two things, both cheap and CPU-only. (1) A 200-row dry run of the v19 config through the TRAIN loader (patched `check_training_data.py --split train`, or the audit's `sample_rows.py`) printing: re-entry-after->=3 s rate, gap floor level per re-entry row, far-speech->=1 s-before-onset rate, per-row target-active fraction, and `onset_frame_fraction` split by row type -- with zero digital-silence gaps and zero rows where the post-gap near turn collapsed to <1.5 s. (2) The beta=0 bit-identity test passing against `OverSuppressionLoss` on random tensors, plus a mask-alignment assertion on a speed-perturbed 30 s row. If those hold and the go/no-go table is rewritten against the instruments that actually emit each number (anchor_gate_sim for /148, train-loader check_training_data for guardrail 8, a pinned recognizer for Dawn, printed CIs instead of hand-set bars) with the length-schedule diversity cut either removed or given its own control arm, this becomes buildable as specified and I go to keep. Nothing here needs a GPU to resolve.

**Fatal flaws**

- PRIMARY 3's named instrument cannot produce its metric. `egs/voice_isolate/scripts/eval_field_block.py` has exactly five score-mode flags (lines 209-213: config_path, --ckpt, --tag, --out, --device, --no-ambient) -- no --dry-blend, no guard flag -- and its keep-violation output is on a 3-5 span scale, not /148 (`benchmarks/field_test_vector/FIELD_BLOCK.md:46`: v8 4/-7.97, v16 ep19 3/-8.34). The x/148 keep objective is `benchmarks/probes/anchor_gate_sim.py`'s span objective over the FIT set (90d/270d/0d/all qvf_*, anchor_gate_README.md:44-54), run on an `anchor_gate_cache.py field` cache. So the round's primary keep gate names the wrong tool, on the wrong denominator, and lands on the fit set while 180d is the set-v3 held-out row that `b_traj.py:training_rows` *raises* on (SET_V3.md).
- Guardrail 8 -- the CPU-only killer, the one thing gating all GPU spend -- is instrumented on the wrong dataloader. `scripts/check_training_data.py:319` does `_train_dl, valid_dl = recipe_main.init_dataloader(recipe)` and then `collect(valid_dl, args.n)`. The validation loader runs at `dataset.training_length_seconds: 6.` with `n_spk_per_batch: 12` and ignores `trainer.length_schedule` entirely, so extending `report_separability` (lines 183-231, which today measures zero temporal properties -- confirmed) would report a fixed-6 s distribution that cannot be compared to the audit's train-loader row shares (49.8/37.8/9.9/2.5%) or to any of guardrail 8's thresholds.
- The onset mask's ground-truth source is time-misaligned with the grid the proposal puts it on. `OverlapGating.apply` is called at ns.py:386; speed perturbation runs at ns.py:487-509 (`augmentation_speed` prob 0.5, +-5%) and perturbs ONLY noisy_speech and target_speech; `vad_reference = target_speech.clone()` is ns.py:583. So `gating.near_frames` (option i) is a PRE-speed-perturb frame mask, while the proposal states the mask is built "post speed-perturb ... so indices line up with vad_target". On a 30 s row a 5% warp is 1.5 s = ~150 VAD frames, larger than the 100-frame L=1.0 s onset window -- the mask misses the onset entirely on the bucket the round raises to 9.7% of rows and where every long gap lives.
- D1's re-entry gap can be digital silence on ~10% of re-entry rows, contradicting the proposal's own text ("never digital silence"). `NoiseStage.apply` fires recorded noise at `prob: 0.8` and `_capture_floor` at `absolute_floor.prob: 0.5` (train_dpcrn_v16_lengthmix.yaml), so P(neither) = 0.10 -- the proposal computes exactly this figure for D3 leads and adds `require_capture_floor`, but never applies it to the interior gap. At 0.212 of rows that is ~2% of all rows re-making the decision across the one prefix the proposal itself measured as a -19.2 dB (v16) suppress bias and a -2.33 dB onset deletion.
- The new loss silently degrades to v16's objective, and the only realisation check is on the wrong side of the boundary. `design_data_loss/loss_sketch.py` ends `m = ... batch.get(self.weight_key); if m is None: return r.mean()`, and the proposal's test list asks for exactly that fallback. The named telemetry, `onset_frame_fraction` in `VOICE_ISOLATION_SCALAR_KEYS`, is a DATASET scalar collated by torch.cat -- it reads correct even if the collate never delivered `onset_weight` to the loss. Additionally `self.verbose` (siso.py:413-417, the `train_step_loss_i` per-term logs the risk section leans on) is `False` by default and is set in neither train_dpcrn.yaml nor train_dpcrn_v16_lengthmix.yaml, so the per-term curves the round promises to read before any field number would not be emitted.

**Evidence conflicts**

- Scene-diversity cut, unmentioned, against v16's own recorded guard. The length reweighting is audio-second neutral (0.10*60+0.20*72+0.35*72+0.35*60 = 66.6 = v16's 66.6 -- arithmetic verified) but rows/batch falls from 0.25*20+0.30*12+0.25*6+0.20*2 = 10.5 to 0.10*20+0.20*12+0.35*6+0.35*2 = 7.2, a 31% cut. `config/exp/train_dpcrn_v16_lengthmix.yaml:25-28` states: "10,500 rows per 1000 iterations against the v8 recipe's 12,000 -- 88% of its scene diversity, deliberately: v15 ran at 1/6 and could not be told apart from a budget shortfall." v19 lands at 7,200 = 69% of v16's. A v19-vs-v16 comparison therefore confounds row shape with the exact budget-shortfall confound v16 was designed to exclude, and the proposal asserts only "epoch wall time is unchanged".
- Part (b)'s immediate cold-start kill rule contradicts the recorded standings whichever column it means. `benchmarks/field_test_vector/COLDSTART_V2.md` standings: far `none` block-median -1.06 (v8) / -1.71 (v16 ep19); far `ambient` -12.57 / -13.19. A rule "shallower than -5 dB median suppression on the cold-start field subset = dead" fires on BOTH baselines if read on `none`, and is a very loose detector if read on `ambient`. The same file also mandates a 5-checkpoint block and BOTH columns ("A cold-start reading is NEVER one checkpoint on one zero-context cut", same-run null band ~3 dB); part (b) reads one checkpoint.
- Two go/no-go thresholds sit inside their instrument's measured spread. (a) Guardrail 4's 2 dB ambient allowance is against `FIELD_BLOCK.md:44` v16 ep19 -12.65 +-2.50, and COLDSTART_V2.md records ambient pads for only 7/20 far clips -- the bar is smaller than the block sd. (b) PRIMARY 3's pass 13 / kill 20 out of 148 binary spans: binomial sd at p~0.135 is ~4 spans, `FIELD_BLOCK.md:11` records "KEEP violation count 2..5" swinging within a block, and no significance test is pre-registered for this row -- unlike PRIMARY 1 and 2, which both carry p-values.
- Guardrail 5's baselines are on a recognizer the named script does not default to, and a second recognizer is on record for the same cell. 0.297 WER / 0.207 del is faster-whisper large-v3 on cache-built audio re-transcribed in one session (`anchor_gate_README.md:118-136`), and the guarded 0.111 comes from the offline gate in that same harness -- not from `eval_dawn_chorus.py`, whose defaults are `--asr auto --asr-model small` (lines 331-333). `benchmarks/wer_sets/AZURE_2026-09.md` records v16 ep19 Dawn at 0.308 / 0.198 under Azure. Neither the recognizer nor the model size is pinned in the go/no-go row.
- Guardrail 6's +-0.02 bar is likely inside the CI its own script prints. `scripts/eval_wer.py:146-164` bootstraps a 95% CI on delta(enh)-delta(mix) over utterances; the moderate baseline on record is the UNCAPPED subset, n=127 (AZURE_2026-09.md: v16 ep19 0.361 / del 0.126, delta -0.166). The round should read the printed CI rather than pre-register a bar below it -- this is the BUT-OFFICE resolution lesson applied to the moderate set.
- The two PRIMARY instruments live only in the session scratchpad. `onset_profile/onset_real.py` and `anchor_synthetic/anchor_synthetic_probe.py` exist under /tmp/claude-1001/.../v19_plan/ (verified present), while implementation item 10 argues the guardrail-8 columns "belong in the repo, not in a scratch script". Inconsistent, and /tmp is session-scoped.
- The "exactly" warm-start command does not parse. `puresound/system/runner.py:149-155` `build_arg_parser` takes a POSITIONAL `config_path` and a `--training` store_true; the proposal's command is `main.py --config config/exp/train_dpcrn_v19_return.yaml --pretrained_ckpt_path ...` -- no `--config` flag exists and `--training` is missing, so it would either error or run no stage. (Everything else about the warm start checks out: runner.py:259-275 loads state_dict weights only with strict=False and logs missing/unexpected.)
- The RNG claim for the forced floor is wrong as stated. `noise_stage.py:166-170`: after the prob gate, `_capture_floor` draws `torch.empty(1).uniform_(low, high)` and `torch.randn_like(noisy)`. Forcing the floor on a row that would not have fired consumes N+1 extra values and shifts every downstream draw on that row, so "forcing the floor does not shift the stream" is false. (The knobs-off bit-exactness claim is separately correct, because require_capture_floor is gated on lead_applied and row_initial_ambient.used is False.)

**Amendments**

- Re-instrument PRIMARY 3: `benchmarks/probes/anchor_gate_cache.py field --ckpt <v19 epN> --tag v19` then `anchor_gate_sim.py sweep --split fit` for the /148 ungated baseline row, and `--split held` on 180d read once (the --split flag exists, sim lines 725). Keep `eval_field_block.py` for guardrail 4 on ITS scale (3-5 violations, FIELD_BLOCK compare mode), and state which denominator each threshold belongs to. Note in the round design that /148 includes every qvf_* clip the round expects not to move.
- Add `--split {train,valid}` to `scripts/check_training_data.py` (default valid, so existing behaviour is unchanged) and run guardrail 8 on the TRAIN loader. Cost is minutes, not hours: the audit's `sample_rows.py` run.log records 1206 rows in 375 s with 20 workers. Port its temporal columns into `report_separability`, and add `--seed` parity with the audit (seed 7, 104 batches) so the v16 control is the recorded one.
- Fix the onset mask's alignment: carry the realised speed factor out of ns.py:487-509 and resample `gating.near_frames` by it before use, OR drop option (i) entirely and always use the strict -25 dB detector on `vad_reference` (which is already post-speed, post-reverb, post-lead). Assert in the unit test that the mask's active runs coincide with `vad_target`'s within 2 frames on a speed-perturbed 30 s row.
- Extend `require_capture_floor` to D1: force `NoiseStage._capture_floor` on any row carrying a >=1 s target-free gap, not only on lead rows, and add a "gap floor level" column to guardrail 8 with the same 0%-digital-silence bar the leads get. Document the RNG consequence honestly (forcing consumes uniform_ + randn_like) instead of claiming stream invariance.
- Make the loss's mechanism observable and non-silent: raise when `beta > 0` and `onset_weight` is absent (keep the graceful path only behind an explicit `allow_missing=True` for the beta=0 identity test), and log a LOSS-side scalar each step (mean of `w`, or realised onset bin fraction after the gather) rather than relying on the dataset's `onset_frame_fraction`. Set `verbose: True` in the v19 recipe so `train_step_loss_i` (siso.py:413-417) actually emits the per-term curves `scripts/dump_loss_curves.py` is supposed to read.
- Re-state the under-resolved thresholds against measured spread: guardrail 4's ambient bar as a paired Wilcoxon vs the v16 ep19 block on the covered intersection (n=7) instead of a 2 dB absolute bar inside +-2.50 dB; PRIMARY 3 with a pre-registered test on the count (block-mean Wilcoxon per clip, or a two-proportion test) and a pass/kill gap wider than ~4 spans.
- Pin the recognizer on guardrail 5: `eval_dawn_chorus.py --asr faster-whisper --asr-model large-v3 --context none` and `--context background` (both are valid choices, CONTEXT_CHOICES line 133), state explicitly that 0.111 is an offline-gate number from `anchor_gate_sim.py` on cache-built audio and therefore an aspiration not a paired baseline, and add the Azure moderate row (uncapped n=127) with the CI `eval_wer.py` prints as the primary WER gate.
- Remove or control the diversity confound: either hold rows/batch at v16's 10.5 (raise n_spk on the 12 s / 30 s buckets and re-verify the memory headroom the v16 header measured -- 19.0 / 16.9 GiB on a 22 GB card), or add a fourth 6-epoch arm in part (b) that keeps v16's length_schedule with D1/D2/L1 on, so a null cannot be read as a budget shortfall the way v15's was.
- Fix the warm-start command to `uv run python main.py config/exp/train_dpcrn_v19_return.yaml --training --pretrained_ckpt_path /work/any_exp_link/puresound_exp/dpcrn_v16_lengthmix/lightning_logs/version_0/checkpoints/epoch=19-step=10000.ckpt` (positional config, --training required). Timestamps verified: 08:51 -> 20:56 on 2026-08-30 = 12 h 05 for 20 epochs = 38 min/epoch, so the 12.5-14 h budget is sound.
- Tighten the re-entry geometry: raise `reentry_min_row_seconds` to 12.0 (realised bucket lengths are 11.43-12.0 and 28.57-30.0 s, so nothing is lost) and cap `gap_s` by remaining frames so the post-gap NEAR turn always gets >= 1.5 s. The proposed validator (`gap_lo*2 <= min_row`, i.e. 6 <= 9) does not bound `turn_far_seconds` max 4.5 + gap max + near min, so at 9 s the post-gap near turn can be truncated to ~0 frames -- turning a re-entry row into a lone-far row that the label-correctness fix then relabels, silently converting the round's flagship row shape into the target-absent rows the proposal forbids.
- Promote `onset_real.py` and `anchor_synthetic_probe.py` into `benchmarks/probes/` with a `--tag`/`--ckpt` interface BEFORE part (b) runs, since they are the two PRIMARY instruments and currently exist only under a session-scoped /tmp path.
- Reconcile the schedule: part (a) claims implementation items 1-5 and 10 land inside 0-6 h, while the engineering plan budgets items 1-2 as Day 1 and items 3-5 + 10 as Day 2. Either the 48 h clock starts after ~2 engineer-days, or part (a) is descoped to items 1-2 plus the train-loader sampler (which is all guardrail 8's row-shape thresholds actually need).
