My reading of the stated definition gives 86.1% eligibility and a zero median on every synthetic population I could score."]

**Fatal flaws**

- TERM B — the proposal's self-declared core — is measurably a no-op at init, and I measured it. v16 ep19 on 51 moderate-set utterances: Pbar−Qbar median 36.74 dB (p10 14.66, p25 24.99, p90 59.39), Pbar median −2.20, Qbar median −40.41. 88.2% of rows already exceed M_B=15 dB; mean L_B = relu(15−margin) = 0.424 dB, firing on 6/51 rows (11.8%). At weight 0.5 that is ~0.2 of a 7.0-weight objective, landing on 12% of rows. Worse, it is structurally blind to the failure it claims to fix: Pbar is a row mean over ALL keep frames, so the measured failure (wrong-anchor Δon1 = −1.33 dB over one 1 s window of a ~9 s row) moves Pbar by ~0.1 dB — inside the hinge slack. And on the 12% where it does fire, the cheap gradient direction is lowering Qbar (deeper suppression), duplicating the inactive-SDR term the round promises not to touch. The proposal's '66.2% of rows can carry term B today' is an ELIGIBILITY count from the audit, silently used as if it were a satisfaction count; combined with my measurement the term is active on ~8% of rows (0.662 × 0.118).
- TERM A's eligibility contradicts its own definition, and both readings are fatal. objective_definition says O = {t : v_t=1, v_{t−100..t−1}=0}, 't=0 included when v_0=1'. Recomputed on the audit's 1165 target-present rows: onset at t≈0 on 895 (76.8%), interior gap ≥1 s on 170 (14.6%), first onset ≥1 s on 77 (6.6%) — union 1003/1165 = 86.1%, not the 19.0% the data_changes section asserts. So either the text is wrong and Term A is a first-0.5-s reweighting of 86% of rows (a global re-weighting wearing a decision label — precisely the v18 failure mode, and K3 only checks gradient NORM, never population), or the definition is wrong and D1–D4 are load-bearing for a term whose row share the proposal has mis-measured. Either way the 'targeted hinge, zero on correct rows' argument does not hold.
- The training signal Term A can actually see on the permitted carrier is ~5x smaller than the effect it must produce, and its median is exactly zero. Computing the proposal's own L_A = relu(g_rest − g_on − 1.0 dB) at window 0.5 s from the diagnosis's foreground-projection rows: v16 Dawn mean 14.90 dB / median 2.90 / frac>0 0.603; v16 moderate bg1 (the D3/D4 carrier) mean 3.04 / median 0.00 / frac>0 0.440; moderate t0 mean 2.12 / median 0.00; indomain bg1 mean 2.30 / median 0.00. v8 agrees (Dawn 14.71, bg1 2.98). The median manufactured row contributes exactly zero Term-A gradient, and the mean is tail-driven at a fifth of the real budget. The one construction that does reproduce the real tail — sil1, mean L_A 8.59 — is the one D3 correctly forbids as a filler. The diagnosis states this outright ('A loss-only change cannot fix onset deletion... there is no gradient to shape'), and the proposal's answer (manufacture rows) is measured to close ~a fifth of the gap in loss magnitude, none of it in the median.
- The headline expected effect — deletion 0.207 → ≤0.12 with far suppression within 1.5 dB — is a claim to move the keep/suppress frontier, and it has no measured basis anywhere in the repo, while five recorded rounds measured the trade instead. OnsetGuard: deletion 0.207→0.111 for −15.2→−11.0 dB fit-set and −22.3→−10.0 dB on held-out 180d. v9: Dawn del 0.086, BUT-OFFICE −0.050, at 3.5 dB shallower far suppression. v11b: keep violations 4→1 for roughly half the anchored suppression (−12.35/−11.29/−9.25 → −6.64/−5.95/−6.02). v14: dry_blend 0.85 halves deletion for free — and beat the round. v18: capping the inactive floor made every suppression number shallower. The 'hinges saturate so it is free' argument is an argument from the loss's algebra, not a measurement, and my M1 measurement shows the hinges are near-silent — a term that adds no pressure also adds no fix.
- Term C's cheapest satisfying direction attacks the mechanism that produces the product's real suppression, and the guard band is inside the instrument's noise. The proposal admits the coupled clock (anchor memory and the wrong-anchor window share one time constant: −8.23/−4.00/−2.81/−0.89 dB at 2/5/10/20 s) and then guards it with 'sessions block median within 1.5 dB of −9.73 ±1.41' — a tolerance smaller than the per-checkpoint sd of the block it is measured against. Since the anchor IS the suppression (1 s knee −12.61 dB on v8, −9.72 on v16; cold start is passthrough), 'forget faster' satisfies C while degrading exactly the axis the round promises to hold, and the gate cannot resolve it.

**Evidence conflicts**

- D1 has already been run, at a comparable dose, with the opposite outcome — and the proposal never cites it. egs/voice_isolate/config/exp/train_dpcrn_coldstart.yaml lines 16–26 define v10's 'T2 cold-open dosage — rows that OPEN with real far-field solo before any near anchor': realfar.turn_taking_prob 0.30→0.45, overlap_control.far_first_prob 0.5→0.75, turn_far_seconds [2.0,4.5]→[2.5,5.0], row-initial-far exposure 10%→23% of rows. egs/voice_isolate/README.md line 74 records the result: v10 'pays for it in WER on the deployment reverberation range (0.024 worse than v8, paired, CI clear of zero) and raises turn-taking KEEP violations 6 → 10'. That is a direct measured contradiction of expected_effects 2 (deletion down), 5 (moderate WER flat, CI upper ≤ +0.005) and 6 (keep violations ≤3). The round's design note even predicted the tension and named the knobs to back off; the proposal re-proposes the knob and asserts the opposite sign.
- The proposal's premise 'turn_taking_prob is 0.0, so this shape is unexplored' is only true of overlap_control on synthetic rows: egs/voice_isolate/config/train_dpcrn.yaml:143 and :153 already ship realfar turn_taking_prob 0.3 and realnear 0.5, and config/exp/train_dpcrn_v16_lengthmix.yaml:152/:162 carry the same. v8 — trained with that far-first exposure — has the WORSE Dawn deletion of the two checkpoints (0.230 vs v16's 0.207, anchor_gate_README §6). More far-first turn exposure is not correlated with less wrong-anchor deletion in the only two data points on record.
- D4 is v17's row_initial_ambient, and the record is not 'null, claims nothing' but negative with a measured cost. benchmarks/field_test_vector/FIELD_BLOCK.md: 'v17 vs v16 ep19: cold-far none WORSE (+0.35, p=0.020) and five QVF keep clips degrade 1.7–6.9 dB. The v17 verdict hardens from "null" to "negative".' Also v17_round_design.md ERRATUM (memory: coldstart-room-reference-causal) retracts §2b: the 90D 'ambience' that unlocked cold start contained unlabelled human speech; pure room tone is a null (−0.04/+0.03 dB, p=0.08/0.76). So D4's stated purpose ('so the onset-parity hinge has cold rows to score') buys rows whose only recorded training-side effect is harm on the round's own suppression guardrail.
- Expected effect 1 gates on Dawn (real chain) a behaviour trained on synthetic rows, against four independent measured non-transfers of exactly that shape: the RIR axis (measured RIRs learn held-out −34 dB, real clip −1 dB); gate-only adaptation (synthetic balanced_acc 0.90, real gate never closes = no-op); real-e2e round-1 (held-out real far −13.6 dB learned, zero cross-corpus transfer, Dawn deletion 0.50); v11 presence heads (in-domain 0.953 / held-out 180D 0.843, QVF scenario3 0.253). The proposal lists this as its 'BIG ONE' risk and then keeps the pass threshold that assumes transfer. Its own diagnosis prices the gap at 11–23x on the median.
- Expected effect 2's basis ('the guard shows the frames are recoverable') omits the price the guard actually pays for those frames. anchor_gate_README §6: in every gated arm the insertion rate returns from 0.014 to 0.036–0.050 (raw mix 0.052) — 'what the model stopped hearing, the gate lets back in' — and drr3 is dry over 39–48% of reference-active frames. The guard recovers deletion by not processing, which is not evidence a trained decision can. The go_no_go gates Dawn DELETION only; Dawn total WER (and insertions) is not a gate, so the pass criterion is satisfiable by the leak the proposal names as its own failure mode.
- The premise 'a model that deletes the first second pays almost nothing' overstates the objective's blindness. The ASRFeatureLoss crop argument (P(t=0)=0.000) applies to the 12 s and 30 s buckets only — 12.4% of rows by the audit's own measurement (rows per bucket 600/456/120/30); on the 3 s and 6 s buckets (87.6% of rows) crop coverage is 1.000 everywhere, and SDR / MR-STFT / OverSuppression cover every onset frame uniformly at weight 4.5 of 7.0. The audit prices onset frames at 14–22% of active energy, i.e. proportionally represented, not unsupervised.
- The s2f gate baseline is the superseded single-checkpoint reading. The proposal quotes −0.25/−0.31/−0.38/−0.55/−1.02 (FIELD_BLOCK.md line 30); the same file's block table (lines 68–76) gives v16 ep19 −0.47/−0.60/−0.56/−0.65/−1.11 and records that no version tilts the curve (every level p>0.1). A round judged 'no point more than 1 dB below the baseline curve' against a stale, non-block baseline cannot fail that gate.
- expected_effects 5's own basis is circular and unmeasured by the proposal: 'the question is only whether v16's typical row contrast already exceeds M_B'. It does — median 36.74 dB, measured here — which the proposal treats as the good case, but it is the same fact that makes the term inert. The proposal's K2 is written as a one-sided kill (fires too broadly) and has no branch for the outcome that actually obtains (fires almost never).

**Amendments**

- Drop Term B entirely, or re-derive M_B from the measured distribution as a per-row RELATIVE quantile rather than a fixed 15 dB. Measured: median margin 36.74 dB, 88.2% already above 15, mean L_B 0.424 dB. If a contrast term is wanted it must be computed inside the onset window (Pbar restricted to [o, o+W) against Qbar over the same window), because the failure is a 1 s-scale event on a row whose mean is dominated by steady state.
- Kill D1 or pre-register it as a REPLICATION of v10's T2 with v10's outcome as the null: keep violations 6→10 and moderate WER +0.024. Nothing in the proposal explains why the same shape at 0.35 synthetic prob should now reduce keep violations rather than raise them; without that argument the round's WER and keep gates are expected to fail on the recorded evidence.
- Drop D4 (v17's knob: cold-far none +0.35 dB worse, p=0.020; five QVF keep clips −1.7..−6.9 dB) and either accept D3's own-room-tone gap as the only cold carrier or reopen the silence question honestly: sil1 is the ONLY measured synthetic construction that reproduces the real tail (mean L_A 8.59 vs bg1 3.04; p5 −70.2 vs Dawn −75.6), and the diagnosis proposes it explicitly as a training stressor. Banning it while keeping a 5x-weaker carrier is the choice that guts Term A.
- Keep only Term C plus the instrumentation, and run it as a 3-epoch probe, not a 1.9-GPU-day round. Term C is the one component with measured in-distribution gradient (synthetic wrong-anchor Δon1 −1.33 dB, p=2.8e-24, 173/200 rows down, self3 null +0.04). Its go/no-go already exists (anchor_synthetic_probe.py). Judged alone at 6 GPU-h it either moves the synthetic probe and Dawn together, or it reproduces the known non-transfer — and either answer is worth more than bundling it with two inert terms.
- Add Dawn total WER and insertion rate as hard gates beside deletion. The guard's own numbers (deletion 0.207→0.111 with insertions 0.013→0.047) show deletion alone is satisfiable by leaking; onset_leak_db at ±2 dB over the onset window does not fence a whole-row leak, and Term B (measured inert) does not either.
- Widen the sessions guard to the instrument's resolution or drop the claim. 1.5 dB against −9.73 ±1.41 (per-checkpoint sd) cannot separate 'held' from 'lost the anchor'; FIELD_BLOCK requires block means with paired Wilcoxon and the proposal's own risk list names 'forget faster' as the likely mechanism. Either pre-register the anchor-length sweep (bgonly 1 s/2 s knee, v16 −9.72/−9.50) as the direct guard, or state that a shortened anchor is an acceptable outcome.
- Keep three cheap by-products regardless of the verdict: (a) the foreground-projection score P as the recorded gate metric for every onset/keep claim (it separates 'deleted the user' from 'removed the interferer' where span energy conflates them: −0.00 vs −6.41 dB on the diagnosis's control); (b) the on_validation_epoch_end hook logging that score on a frozen decision-row set; (c) the temporal columns in scripts/check_training_data.py. None of these needs the loss.

### BUILDABILITY & MEASUREMENT. I read every file the proposal names, checked the line references, verified every named eval instrument exists and takes the flags the go/no-go assumes, re-derived the P-score numerics on CPU, and bootstrapped the primary gate's resolution from the diagnosis's own per-utterance rows. Artifacts: /tmp/claude-1001/-home-milowu-A4Audio-PureSound/c52f3b49-8e8f-4f9f-a5ca-862771bd494c/scratchpad/v19_plan/verify_decision_objective_buildability/{FINDINGS.md,numerics.py}

- score 6/10 — **amend**
- what would change my mind: "Two things move me to keep. First, restate gate 1 as a paired per-utterance delta (measured half-width +/-0.11 dB against the +/-1.694 dB marginal) so the primary threshold is resolvable -- that alone converts the round from unmeasurable to measurable. Second, floor P and Q at a pre-registered value and re-run Stage 0's K1 on the median of the floored score; if K1 still shows a >= 50% reduction from guarded to unguarded Dawn output after flooring, the loss demonstrably measures the deletion rather than the phase tail, and F2 stops being fatal. Beyond that I would want the D5 reference emitted post-speed and post-chain (amendment 4) before any GPU hour is spent, because a per-frame LS gain against a reference that drifts 600 ms on half the rows produces numbers that look like results. Conversely, if Stage 0's K2 finds the median v16 row contrast below M_B = 15 dB, M_B must be re-derived from the measured distribution before the round runs at all -- the proposal already names that as its own second kill condition and I agree it is the cheapest thing to check. I would move to kill only if amendment 4 turns out to require changing device_chain's RNG contract (which would put the ONNX/streaming byte-identity claim at risk) -- I did not verify that a stacked [K,T] pass through device_chain.apply preserves the draws, and that is the one buildability question I could not settle on CPU."

**Fatal flaws**

- PRIMARY GATE IS INSIDE ITS OWN BASELINE'S NOISE. I bootstrapped the diagnosis's own rows (/tmp/.../v19_plan/onset_profile/real_onset_rows.jsonl, Dawn condition none, window 0.5, v16, n=446): median g_excess = -3.905 dB with a 95% bootstrap CI of [-5.982, -2.595], HALF-WIDTH +/-1.694 dB. The pre-registered KILL threshold (<= -3.0 dB) lies INSIDE the v16 baseline's own confidence interval, and PASS (>= -1.5 dB) is only 1.5 dB from KILL on a statistic whose marginal noise is +/-1.7 dB. A re-run of v16 itself would land 'kill', 'ambiguous' or 'pass' depending on the draw. The paired form is fine: paired v16-v8 median delta = -0.182 dB, CI [-0.327, -0.102], half-width +/-0.11 dB -- 15x tighter. The gate must be a PAIRED per-utterance delta on the same items, not an absolute median. As written, the round's single most important number is not resolvable.
- P HAS NO FLOOR, ITS GRADIENT DIES WHERE THE DAMAGE IS WORST, AND THE 'MEASURED ENVELOPE' IS MISLABELLED. Verified on CPU (numerics.py, autograd): with K=16, P(a=0) = -27.2659 dB -- that is the value at a=0, not a floor. P(-0.1) = -38.79, P(-0.5) = -93.56, saturating at -120 dB (the 1e-12 term). max|dP/da| = 138.87 at a = -0.43155; the proposal's '100.2' is the gradient AT a=0, and the same error runs through K=8 (real max 69.45 vs claimed 50.1) and K=32 (277.62 vs 200.5). Gradient collapses for a < ~-0.85: dP/da = 4.02 at -0.8, 0.604 at -0.86, 0.0069 at -1.0, 7.7e-10 at -1.5. So softplus does not remove the dead zone -- it relocates it from a<=0 to a<=-0.85, i.e. onto the anti-correlated / over-subtracted frame, the most damaged case of all, while the fully-deleted frame it was chosen for (a=0) is the one place it works. Consequence, measured on the Dawn rows with tau_A=1: mean L_A = 14.89 against median 2.90; the worst 4.9% of onsets carry 31.1% of the mean and the worst 10.1% carry 53.0%. Flooring P at -30 dB drops mean L_A from 14.89 to 9.41 (-37%). L_A as specified is a phase/anti-correlation tail detector as much as a deletion detector, and Stage 0's K1 ('>= 50% reduction in mean L_A') is pre-registered on exactly that tail-dominated statistic.

**Evidence conflicts**

- far_targets as specified is TIME-MISALIGNED on half the rows, and the code says so. Speed perturbation (puresound/task/ns.py:489-508, prob 0.5, +/-5%) is applied to noisy_speech and target_speech ONLY; the row-initial-ambient comment at ns.py:559-561 states in the source that background_speech_reference is 'a pre-speed snapshot'. The per-interferer list D5 proposes to keep lives at ns.py:399 -- pre-speed, pre-ria, pre-chain, pre-snip. A per-frame least-squares gain Q_k,t against a reference drifting up to 5% of row length (600 ms at 12 s, 1.5 s at 30 s) is not measuring leak. This already bites Stage 0's fallback: consistency_noise (ns.py:631 = noisy-target) IS post-speed and aligned, but background_vad_target, derived from background_vad_reference, is on the pre-speed grid -- so S_k, the frame set that defines Qbar_k, is misaligned on half of Stage 0's rows. Implementation items 4-5 contain none of the required speed/ria/chain/truncate handling.
- The row-level nonlinearity skip guards stages that never fire in v16, and misses the one that does. Measured from config/exp/train_dpcrn_v16_lengthmix.yaml: augmentation_volume.clipping_prob = 0.0 and augmentation_codec.used = False, so volume_clipped and codec_applied (real batch keys, puresound/task/device_chain.py:67-85, reaching the batch via ns.py:774-776) are identically 0 -- the skip is a no-op and the risk it bounds does not exist. What does fire is LTI filtering (hpf 0.25, src 0.5 with P(8 kHz)=0.2, ir_response 0.3), so noisy-target = H(sum u_k) + H(noise): the post-chain per-talker reference is H(u_k), not g*u_k, and a single scalar row-level gain cannot represent a filter. The residual mismatch inflates Q and fires L_B spuriously. Separately, media_voice (prob 0.30, compress_power 0.6-0.9 = a real |x|^p waveshaper) is a genuine nonlinearity in the interferer path with NO batch flag at all; it is applied per interferer at ns.py:173-181, so keeping the pre-sum list preserves it, but the proposal's nonlinearity story names the wrong stages entirely. overload_rescaled is a linear rescale of both signals and is harmless.
- D3's 'the gap is filled by the row's own recorded-noise / room branch and NEVER by digital silence' is not enforceable in this pipeline, and the failure mode is the exact anti-lesson. Gating runs at ns.py:386, the noise stage at ns.py:589; inside a re-entry gap both near_mask and far_mask are False, so the gap IS digital silence until noise fills it. In v16 augmentation_noise.prob = 0.8 and absolute_floor.prob = 0.5, so P(neither fires) = 0.2 * 0.5 = 0.10. About 10% of re-entry rows would therefore carry a multi-second digital-silence gap -- the one prefix the diagnosis measures as a -19.22 dB suppress bias on v16 (anchor_synthetic bgonly Delta_on1) and that reference_matrix_README section 2 already forbids. Nothing in the plan makes the noise branch mandatory on these rows.
- Two of the nine pre-registered gates have no versioned instrument. Gate 1's onset_profile/onset_real.py and gate 5's anchor_synthetic_probe.py (plus onset_synth.py, used in the 48 h Stage 1 reads) exist only in this round's scratchpad -- they are NOT under egs/voice_isolate/benchmarks/probes/ (I listed it; it has anchor_gate_cache.py, anchor_gate_sim.py, reference_matrix_probe.py, eq_probe.py etc., but none of these three). Implementation items 1-11 never commit them, so the pre-registration in item 11 would point at throwaway files.
- Cold-far is absent from the pre-registration. scripts/eval_field_block.py does produce it (kind='far', condition='none', plus the eval_coldstart_v2 ambient conditions it imports at line 34-35), but no threshold is set for it anywhere in the nine gates. Cold-far is the metric that killed v18 (+0.53 dB, p=0.017) and was v17's own pre-registered primary, and a round whose whole point is adding keep-side weight on cold onsets is precisely the shape that shallows it. The proposal gates keep violations, sessions suppression, s2f keep, synthetic far-only and extreme-reverb WER -- and leaves the one column with a recorded regression history ungated.

**Amendments**

- Restate go/no-go gate 1 as a PAIRED per-utterance delta against the v16 ep19 block over the same 446 Dawn items (measured half-width +/-0.11 dB), e.g. pass = paired median improvement >= +2.4 dB with p < 0.01, kill = <= +0.9 dB. Drop the absolute -1.5 / -3.0 dB thresholds: measured marginal half-width is +/-1.694 dB, so they cannot separate pass from kill.
- Add clamp_min to P and Q at a pre-registered floor (-30 dB is the value I checked: it cuts Dawn mean L_A from 14.89 to 9.41 and keeps the gradient alive across the whole clamped-in region). Correct the envelope table in the objective definition to the measured maxima (K=8/16/32 -> 69.45 / 138.87 / 277.62), and state explicitly that the softplus dead zone sits at a <= -0.85, not at a <= 0.
- Re-state Stage 0's K1 and K2 on the MEDIAN (or a winsorized mean) of the floored score. On the Dawn rows the mean is 5x the median and 53% of it comes from 10% of onsets, so a '>= 50% reduction in mean L_A' criterion can be met or missed by a handful of phase-inverted frames.
- D5: emit far_targets AFTER speed perturbation, the row-initial-ambient mask, the device chain and the sample_length snip. The cheap exact route is to stack the K<=2 members as extra rows and pass them through the SAME device_chain.apply call so they inherit the identical draws (K_max is 2 in v16 -- add_n_cases [1,2] -- so the cost is bounded); the sox speed call must be replayed with the same factor. Until this lands, do not run Stage 0's K1/K2 using background_vad_target as the frame mask on speed-perturbed rows.
- Replace the volume_clipped/codec_applied row skip with (a) a new media_voice_applied row scalar and (b) the LTI flags already in the batch (hpf_applied, src_applied, iir_applied), and document that in v16 the two originally named flags are identically zero. Better still, drop the scalar-gain approximation entirely once amendment 4 gives a true post-chain reference.
- D3: force a noise or absolute-floor branch on every re-entry row and assert the gap's frame RMS sits above the capture floor, so the measured 10% digital-silence-gap case cannot occur.
- Commit onset_real.py, onset_synth.py and anchor_synthetic_probe.py to egs/voice_isolate/benchmarks/probes/ as numbered implementation items, and have benchmarks/probes/decision_objective_README.md reference the committed paths.
- Add a cold-far gate read off the same eval_field_block block: kill if the cold-far block median shallows by more than 1.5 dB vs the v16 ep19 block, paired per clip.
- Fix the test path: test/test_nnet/ does not exist. Loss tests live in test/test_losses/, and test/test_system/test_loss_dispatch.py (lines 129 and 145) is the file that already reads siso._loss_providers -- the provider-contract assertion belongs there.
- Make the bucket scoping explicit rather than emergent: add min_row_seconds to OverlapControlConfig and gate the turn_taking_prob draw inside OverlapGating.apply on it. D1's synthetic half IS correctly config-only (_turn_taking_override at voice_isolation.py:336-344 returns None for synthetic rows, so apply() falls through to config.turn_taking_prob), but nothing today keeps D1 off the 3 s bucket -- 49.8% of rows -- except D2's five-try resample falling back to Bernoulli.
- Copy the augmentation_row_initial_ambient block from config/exp/train_dpcrn_v17_roomlead.yaml; it is absent from the v16 recipe, so D4 is a config addition, not a reuse. And note that only leads >= 1.0 s satisfy Term A's G=100-frame onset gap, so at prob 0.25 with lead 0.5-3.0 s the effective Term A carrier is ~20% of rows.
- Add the Dawn agcache block build to the cost line: measured 1.4 GB and 900 records per checkpoint, so a 5-checkpoint block is ~7 GB of scratchpad plus five cache passes (124 GB free on /tmp, so feasible but currently unbudgeted). Raise the engineering estimate from ~4 days to ~6 to cover amendments 4 and 7.
- Pre-register the measured gradient-supply numbers so the D1-D4 premise is falsifiable rather than rhetorical. I measured Term A's hinge (tau=1) on the diagnosis's own synthetic rows: wer_set_moderate_test/v16 t0 frac_active 0.365 / mean L_A 2.12; bg1 (the D4 analogue) 0.440 / 3.04 -- only +43% over t0; sil1 0.470 / 8.59 (4x, but forbidden as a filler); Dawn none 0.603 / 14.89. So the term is NOT dead on synthetic rows -- contra a flat reading of the diagnosis -- but its synthetic mean gradient supply is 7x smaller than Dawn's and the strongest carrier is the one the record bars. State D4's expected contribution as '+43% mean L_A over t0' and kill the round at Stage 1 if the realised figure is below that.

# Rejected by the synthesis

- **v19-RETURN (re-entry rows + bystander-first rows + onset-weighted OverSuppression)** — Bundles three knobs the repo has already run with the opposite outcome and one that cannot help. The turn-taking/far-first dose raise IS v10's T2 (turn_taking 0.30->0.45, far_first 0.5->0.75, row-initial-far 10%->23%), which raised turn-taking KEEP violations 6->10 and cost 0.024 WER in the deployment reverberation range. The lead-in knob IS v17's row_initial_ambient, which the block hardened from null to negative (cold-far none +0.35 dB, p=0.020; five QVF keep clips 1.7-6.9 dB worse). The long-gap re-entry row carries almost none of the failure it is built to teach (wrong-anchor deletion is -0.49/-0.46 dB at a 5/10 s gap, dead by 20 s). And the onset weight lands ~90% of its added mass on row heads, where 81.7% of rows already start with the target talking and the correct action is KEEP -- a global reweighting wearing a decision label. Its two free by-products (the target_present relabel and the temporal columns in check_training_data.py) are adopted into Round 0.
- **v19-FGMEM (decay-free foreground slot, self-enrolled, FiLM-conditioned)** — Its own 48 h kill test is pre-failed by the diagnosis: the session row it manufactures (bystander -> 3-12 s gap -> user) shows only -0.49/-0.46 dB of wrong-anchor deletion on v16, so the loss has nothing to push on, while the damaging real geometry is the immediate handoff with no gap. The decay-free slot removes the ~10 s forgetting that is currently the only thing stopping one bystander utterance from arming suppression for a whole session, and the coupling it claims to break (persistence vs wrong-anchor deletion share one clock) is broken only by an untested demotion clause that depends on a readout measured at chance-or-inverted on unseen chains. It also wires a presence readout to the mask, which the b-gate nine-gate priced at KEEP violations 6->65 and SI-SDR +5.42->-0.23, and the row dose is ~2.1% of rows once the script is fitted to the realised row lengths, not the claimed 17.6%. Its buildability findings are adopted: theta's 1/k step freezes into an absolute threshold, and the streaming warm-up phantom frames would be integrated permanently by a decay-free accumulator.
- **v19-A DECIDE (three-hinge decision margin: onset parity, per-talker margin, no-anchor-inheritance)** — Two of its three terms are measurably inert or misdirected, but the third is the round we are running. Term B (per-talker margin) was measured on v16 ep19 at a median row contrast of 36.74 dB with 88.2% of rows already above the 15 dB margin -- and because Pbar is a row mean, the measured 1 s failure moves it by ~0.1 dB, so it is structurally blind to what it targets. Term A (onset parity) has a definition/eligibility contradiction (86.1% of rows vs the claimed 19%), a zero median on every permitted synthetic carrier, and its only strong carrier is the silence prefix its own data plan forbids. Its data clauses D1 and D4 are v10's T2 and v17 respectively. Term C, floored and windowed, plus the foreground-projection score P as the keep-side metric of record, is what survives -- and it is Round 1.
- **v19a chain invariance (ProxHead + within-row contrast + nonlinear chain consistency)** — Dominated by a zero-GPU option the diagnosis already measured: a logistic probe on the same cached bottleneck window, fitted on device recordings only, reads QVF at 0.827 (v16) while the proposal's pass band is 0.50-0.75. Its invariance term trains against operators measured not to cause the failure (envelope compressor |dAUC| <= 0.013; |x|^p degrades 0.989->0.880 but never inverts) while the linear channel-consistency term it extends is already trained in both v8 and v16 at the same settings with QVF still inverted -- and it would disable the existing mask-consistency signal on its own nonlinear views. Its primary instrument cannot read the head it proposes (anchor_gate_sim hard-loads a two-layer MLP), and it would be the fourth unattached readout in a family with three recorded non-deployments. Its offline probe is adopted as Round 0's dominance test, with a nonlinear readout variant, clip-level bootstrap and session scope added.
- **Raising the far-first / turn-taking row dose as a data lever** — Already executed at a comparable dose in v10's cold-open recipe: turn-taking KEEP violations 6 -> 10 and moderate-range WER 0.024 worse than v8 with the CI clear of zero. Round 1 therefore scores the existing ~6% natural dose rather than manufacturing more.
- **Digital silence as a training prefix or gap filler** — -5.90 dB (v8) / -19.22 dB (v16) suppress bias on the lone-interferer arm, -2.33 dB keep-side onset deletion on v16, and a -35.46 dB dt keep median in the field. It stays a stressor/probe with an explicit KEEP label if it is ever used at all, never incidental padding.
- **Relaxing any suppression floor to make room for keep-side pressure** — v18 measured that relaxing the inactive-SDR floor made every suppression number shallower, cold-far +0.53 dB at p=0.017. Round 1 adds only a detached-on-the-suppress-side hinge at 0.25 of 7.25 total weight and touches no floor.
- **Any absolute dB threshold, margin, or metres target** — Died twice. Pooled QVF readability 0.474 vs within-session 0.886/0.911; v8's 3 dB DRR margin costs v16 ten suppress passes; v11b's factory threshold died from logit drift between ep19 and ep31. Every comparison in this plan is within-row or session-relative.
- **Wiring a presence readout to the mask (FiLM, gate, or gain) in v19** — The b gate at 0.50 pushed turn-taking KEEP violations 6 -> 65, SI-SDR +5.42 -> -0.23 and failed four gates, while the field set showed zero cost because it is one room. The standing prerequisite is that presence estimation must be chain- and reverb-invariant before it may drive gain.
